# PROGRESS: Artificial Paradise, Digital Exhibition

## Status

South → north: bench L, small radiator + window, screen (#3), big radiator + round-vent window, stair opening. Spawn faces Human as Error VI (what you see coming down the stairs). The curtain hangs beside the opening, closes the space between two short walls south of it and does not divide the rooms; see the decision log.)

The 10 paintings and prints use the artist's reproductions (room folders in the Drive folder): the seven paintings (#1, #2, #4, #5, #9, #10, #14) are trimmed to the canvas and set to the listed proportions, the three prints (#11–#13) are used whole (`scripts/crops.json`, `reproductions/`); their in-situ view stays the DSC photo. Paradise Factory and Let it Flow use the five `_artificial paradise` installation photos, and the viewer's in-situ button steps through them.

  - Let it Flow (#16), rebuilt against the documentation video and the photos `_artificial paradise 04 - 01/03`, DSC_0112–0117 (a test camera placed where the photographer stood, Nikon D5300 at 18 mm, 5.97 m from the wall, 1.52 m high, gives the same picture). Not the end wall itself or the pipe over the projection: those were built from the reconstructor's written descriptions (corrected 15 November 2026, see the note after "no beam" below). Room 4 is nearly black and lit only by its projections (the darkness is part of Let it Flow, see the decision log), so its surfaces are dark in `materials.js` (the ambient light is shared by all rooms). Wall: the video Siluett added as light, 4.09 × 2.30 m on the 4 m wall (measured on DSC_0113 at 259 px/m), standing on the floor, so the whole 16:9 picture shows and it stops about 20 cm under the pale 16 cm pipe (axis 2.58 m; re-measured 15 November 2026, see the decision log). This replaces the earlier floor-to-ceiling crop: the owner then asked for real-life sizes. There is no beam against the end wall. (Note of 15 November 2026, correcting this line: the end wall and the pale pipe were built from the reconstructor's written descriptions, by her account, and not measured on the photographs or the video, as this line said. Commit 973c383 (31 October 2026), which wrote this line, named DSC_0113 in its code comment as the source of the pipe's height and thickness; commit c5865eb (7 November 2026) added the pipe's branch and painted it white, citing DSC_0109, DSC_0111, DSC_0113 and DSC_0114. Which values were read off a photograph and which came from the descriptions is not recorded. The pipe as built did not match DSC_0113: four collars where the photograph shows five sockets, the branch turned the wrong way, no cap at the south end. The figures for the picture hold: the measurement of 15 November 2026 gives 652 px/m on the full-size DSC_0113 (about 260 px/m on a 1600 px copy) and a top edge at 2.30 to 2.32 m. That measurement kept the pipe's axis at 2.58 m and made it 16 cm thick, with five sockets, a cap and the branch rising south: see the decision log, "Room 4, the pale pipe along the top of the end wall". For "no beam against the end wall" no photograph is cited anywhere: the beam was taken out in 973c383 (`architecture.js`), where until then the wall picture had reached up to it at 2.72 m; it has not been checked against the photographs since. The recesses in the left wall: see "Room 4, the left (north) wall" in the decision log.) River: rebuilt from the artist's corrected composite `PLANE javított.png` (`scripts/make_river.py`), which lays the plastic out in the floor video's own 16:9 frame, on the video's river, so placing the frame places the plastic and the two coincide (92% of the plastic under the video's river). This log used to say that at 606 px/m (the area of plastic matching the first, tile-scaled composite) the frame is 5.28 × 2.97 m and the river 3.58 m long. Since commit e58490e (the artist's real size, 4 × 7 m) the code has a frame of 7.0 × 3.94 m and a river 4.74 m long (`src/data/river.json`, 457 px/m). On the photograph the river measures 3.55 m. The artist has been asked which is right; the river is unchanged until then. The script also writes the frame and the no-walk boxes to `river.json`, low soft folds on an 8 mm grid, about 3 cm thick at the wall to 9 cm at the white heap, creases baked dark, sharp folds in the normal map. It is lit in the shader by the projected video alone (`letItFlow.js`): the light takes the plastic's colour, falls off as folds turn from the lamp and glints, and takes the brightest video nearby, because the video's river and the plastic differ in outline. Floor: the video Let it Flow, turned 180°, in that same frame (the code has its edge at x 25.74 and its centre at z 3.73, `projection` in `river.json`; this log used to say x 25.65 and z 3.85. The wall tip at z 4.25 as in 04 - 01 was in the old note; no value in the code sets it, so it is unchecked). Its brightness is limited on the floor, which returns far less light than the plastic, so the river of light that misses the plastic stays as faint as the specks. The strips between the frame's long edges and the side walls continue its specks by mirroring them, and its far end fades out. (Note of 16 November 2026: the artist confirms (reported by the reconstructor, 16 November 2026) that Siluett at about 4.09 × 2.30 m, from the floor to under the pipe, is right. Nothing in the scene changed. Status: measured on DSC_0113, now confirmed by the artist.) (Note of 17 November 2026: the river is now 4.05 m long, within the 4.0 to 4.35 m the artist confirms; the floor video comes from the projector on the north wall above Natural Life, keystoned; Room 4 springs at 2.92 m. See the decision log, 17 November 2026.)
  - The artist's videos: short loops (`scripts/make_videos.sh`, `public/video/`, 49 MB with the light versions; what made each file: `docs/video-versions.md`) play on the screens near the visitor: on laptops and desktops the nearest 8 within 16 m, measured straight through walls, and Let it Flow's two projections from the Room 2 | 3 doorway on (since commit 0da6234 of 3 November 2026; "3 at a time, 2 on phones", written here until 18 November 2026, was the rule of 30 October to 3 November); on phones and low-power devices by room, with light versions of what is seen from the next room (decision log, "Videos by room", 18 November 2026); the artwork viewer plays the loop and the full video from Google Drive. The wall projection's loop (`video/w16-projection.mp4`): since commit 0da6234 (3 November 2026) it is cut from `siluett_short.mp4` (called `trees_new.mp4` in the script until 13 November 2026), a 10 s excerpt the reconstructor cut from Siluett where it moves most; the loop is 9 s at 1600 × 900, its start dissolving over its end for 1 s, sound included, encoded without B-frames or skipped blocks (`enc_loop`). Before that, it was one whole 25.8 s cycle of Siluett (`enc_cycle`), whose only movement, leaves in the wind, froze at web bitrates. The floor video is 1280 × 720, its black point raised to keep its haze off the dark floor, and its end dissolves into its start (`enc_fade`).
  - The videos' own sound: the loops of Artificial Paradise – Singapore and – The Eden project, Natural Life and Siluett (the Let it Flow wall projection) keep their sound track (AAC; Natural Life raised by 15 dB when encoded. The Siluett loop was raised by 15 dB too until commit 0da6234 of 3 November 2026 re-cut the wall loop at 0 dB, about −54 LUFS; on 13 November 2026 it was raised by 21.5 dB back to the earlier loop's −32 LUFS, see the decision log). After Enter, Web Audio plays each from its screen (`src/audio/ambience.js`): full within 1.2 m of a screen (4.5 m of the projection), fading with distance, quieter through a doorway, the one you face leading. Nothing else plays (see the decision log, 13 November 2026). On the guided tour the current stop's video leads and the tour stays 14 s at these works. The artwork viewer plays the loop with its sound and mutes the walk's mix. Silent: Paradise Factory and the Let it Flow floor video (silent tracks), Wiena I/II (no sound track).
  - Wall textures: the redrawn brick patterns from the Drive folder (`scripts/make_bricks.mjs`), scaled so a course is 7.7 cm (14, 14 and 28 courses per tile). The folder's grey and warm bump files show a different wall from their colour files, so those two bumps are derived from the colour; every bump keeps the relief strength of the earlier photo-cut walls. (`scripts/make_textures.py`, which cut the earlier bricks from DSC_0074 / 0061 / 0087, is no longer run.)

The documentation videos are named 2025 because they were recorded during the exhibition; the works are dated 2024. Neither is a question any more.)

## References and the Concept page (built on `wip/references`, 16 to 18 November 2026; not merged)
- 13 Nov 2026: reference table settled in the Google Sheet `references` (Drive folder `csv`). 21 references from the exhibition's texts, chosen by the reconstructor, and 17 concepts proposed by the assistant and accepted by the reconstructor. English and Hungarian Wikipedia links and Wikidata identifiers checked through the Wikipedia and Wikidata APIs. R20 links the Hungarian Academy of Arts itself; R16's link (Gardens by the Bay) was sent by the artist. Places carry a subtype (the Tisza is a river; the Eden Project and Gardens by the Bay are visitor attractions). Six themes proposed by the assistant; T1 (Greenhouse effect / Üvegházhatás) confirmed by the reconstructor, the other five wait for the reconstructor's reading of the texts.
- 16 and 17 Nov 2026: built on branch `wip/references` (Brief 4; made from `wip/a11y-pilot` at ba3b7f1 and rebased on 17 November onto `wip/a11y-pilot` at fbf10e4, after Part 1; not merged, not on `main`). Nothing in the geometry, the hang, the light, the sound or any artwork file changed, and no text in `src/i18n` was reworded: the links and footnotes are laid over the texts when they are shown.
  - What was added. (1) The reference table as four CSV files in `references/` (`references.csv`, `suggestions.csv`, `themes.csv`, `texts.csv`) and `scripts/make_references.mjs`, which writes `src/data/references.json` from them: 21 references (R01 to R21), the 17 concepts whose "include" starts with "yes" (X01 to X17), six themes (T1 to T6), the three footnotes and the network's layout. The script keeps the table's ids, leaves empty cells empty, checks every anchor against the English and Hungarian texts with the same matching the site uses (`src/refs.js`), and stops if an anchor is in none of the texts its row names or if two links overlap. (2) Links in the texts: the opening speech and the supporters' line (About panel), the artist's texts in the viewer (Human as Error, the Artificial Paradise videos and prints, Paradise Factory, Let it Flow, Natural Life), and the same texts in the accessible version (`text/`). First whole-word occurrence per text; the page language's own link first, else the other language's link with an "EN" or "HU" mark, else the primary source; R20 as its note says (the Academy's English pages on the English page, no mark); every link opens in a new tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"` and says so in its accessible name. The link is the text's own colour with a thin underline. (3) The three footnotes of the original opening speech (source S12), restored in both languages: a superscript number after the sentence, the notes under the speech under "Notes" / "Jegyzetek", each note the table's citation (R02, R04, R05) with a link to its `url_primary` and a link back to its marker. R02 has no inline link, R04 and R05 keep theirs. (4) The Concept page ("Concept" / "Koncepció" in the navbar, `src/ui/concept.js`): Themes (the default), Works, Timeline and Network behind tabs, and a "Connections" line in the viewer for the works a reference names. (5) Privacy, both languages, asked for by the reconstructor in Brief 4: a paragraph on the links to Wikipedia and other outside sites; "Last updated" is now 16 November 2026. No JSON-LD (Brief 4, step 5: a linked-data export belongs to the catalogue, roadmap item 4). No new storage, no new runtime dependency; `d3-force` 3.0.0 is a devDependency used by the script only.
  - Who chose what. The references were chosen by the reconstructor from the exhibition's texts. The 17 concepts were proposed by Claude (the assistant) and accepted by the reconstructor on 13 Nov 2026. The six themes were proposed by Claude; naming and introducing them is the reconstructor's. Confirmed: T1 (Greenhouse effect / Üvegházhatás, named by the reconstructor on 13 Nov 2026). T2 to T6 are proposed and wait for the reconstructor's reading of the texts. No theme has an introduction in the table yet, so the page shows none: T1 to T6 all wait for one, and none was written by the assistant. The Hungarian links, the English Wikipedia links and the Wikidata identifiers were added from the Wikipedia and Wikidata APIs on 13 Nov 2026. R16's link (Gardens by the Bay) was sent by the artist; the text does not name the gardens, so linking them is his reading. The three footnotes were restored from the original speech; their citations are used exactly as the table has them, the same in both languages.
  - The network. Nodes: the six themes, the 38 references and concepts, and the 17 works a theme or a reference names (61 nodes). Lines: reference to theme, reference to work, theme to work, 81 in all; a theme whose works are "all" (T2) draws none. The layout is worked out at build time by `scripts/make_references.mjs` with d3-force (fixed starting places, a seeded random source, 400 steps), so the same table gives the same picture every time; the browser draws a plain SVG from the stored positions, with no graph library. The position of a node carries no meaning, and the page says so under the diagram. The links come from the reconstructor's reading of the texts. Shape and colour together tell the kind of node (large filled circle: theme; small ring: reference or concept; small square: work) and the line style tells the kind of link; a key lists them.
  - Rules the assistant wrote into the script because the table gives them in words or not at all (each has its comment in `scripts/make_references.mjs`; decided by: assistant, for the reconstructor to check). The supporters (R19 to R21) have no anchor in the table: the three names of the credits' "Supported by" line are linked in the table's order, each checked for a word of its label. X02 ("greenhouse" / "üvegház") is linked in the sentence its note names. X11 is linked in the speech only, as its note says. R02's anchor is its footnote. Where a row names two texts (R17, X13, X17), the anchor has to be in at least one of them; where it is not in the other, the script reports it and goes on (English: "River Tisza" is not in Natural Life, which says "Tisza River"; Hungarian: "szennyezésből" is not in Natural Life; "glitches" / "glitcheiben" is not in Human as Error). The citations' own language is marked for screen readers (R02 English, R04 and R05 Hungarian). The dates the table gives in words are shown in Hungarian on the Hungarian page (`ui.concept.dates` in `hu.json`, listed for a Hungarian reader). Texts are named on the page by their work or series title, with "videos" / "prints" to tell the two Artificial Paradise texts apart.
  - Choices in the page the brief left open (decided by: assistant; each can be changed). The Works view and the viewer's "Connections" line show only the works a reference names (nos 2, 4, 5, 6, 7, 16, 17); a work that only a theme leads to has no line. A theme whose works are "all" (T2) is listed among the themes of each of those works. The accessible version carries the same links and footnotes as the panels, which the brief did not ask for; it does not carry the Concept page's views. A click or Enter on a reference in the diagram opens its card under the diagram.
  - The look (17 Nov 2026, after the rebase onto Part 1). The Concept page is a paper panel like the others and follows H1: ink text, muted ink for secondary text, the blue #072fcc only on paper (the chosen tab's bar, the works and themes as buttons, the footnote markers, the focus ring), no state told by colour alone (the chosen tab is also bold with a bar; a node's kind is told by its shape as well as its colour; the kind of link by the line's style), no type under 12 px. The links laid over the texts keep the text's own colour (ink on paper) with an underline. The viewer stays dark, and its Connections line uses the viewer's colours. Every new colour pair is in the H1 contrast table in the pilot section; the one below 3:1 is the faded part of the diagram while a node is highlighted, on purpose (opacity 0.25, 1.49 to 1.72 against the paper), which the reconstructor can change (raising it to 0.6 keeps 3:1 but leaves the highlight weak).
  - Decided by the reconstructor on 18 Nov 2026 (the questions the build left open). (1) The four anchors found in only one of the two texts their row names (EN X13 "glitches" and HU X13 "glitcheiben", not in Human as Error; EN R17 "River Tisza", which Natural Life writes "Tisza River", and HU X17 "szennyezésből", not in Natural Life) stay as built: linked where they are found. (2) While a node in the Network is highlighted, the rest stays at opacity 0.25 (1.49 to 1.72:1 on paper, below 3:1 on purpose; the highlighted part keeps its full contrast). (3) The Concept page keeps the 560 px paper card of the other panels, so the Network draws its narrow, taller layout on a desktop too. (4) The themes get no introduction for now; the page shows none. (5) The reconstructor read and approved seven Hungarian strings: `ui.concept.title`, `ui.concept.netNote`, `privacy.sections[4].p[1]` (outside links), `ui.concept.mentionedIn`, `ui.concept.goto` and the subtypes `municipality of Hungary` and `historic county of England`. Their fingerprints were added to `tests/tools/hu-checked.txt` one by one (the tool's `--checked` would have taken the whole list as checked); the rest of the list stays open in `docs/accessibility/hu-confirm.md`.
  - Not a reconstruction decision (references and presentation), so measured, inferred and invented do not apply to the page itself. Evidence for the references is the table (`references/*.csv`), whose columns `link_basis` and `subtype_basis` say where each value comes from.

## Known issues / assumptions
- The R2/R3 doorway width and pass-through were fitted to DSC_0098/0130 rather than the plan (the plan's north pillar looked too wide against the photos), then rescaled with Room 2.
- The English exhibition text in the owner's Word file has editing leftovers (repeated words); the site keeps a cleaned version of it. The Hungarian is the original.
- Painting hanging heights come from the panorama and photos (±10 cm). (Note of 16 November 2026: the artist (reported by the reconstructor, 16 November 2026) has no record of the hanging heights and thinks the works hang at the right height in the walk. The ±10 cm estimate stays; still inferred.)
- Outer walls are modelled 40 cm thick. If the real façade walls are thicker (old Budapest basements are often 60–90 cm), the rooms would be up to ~3% shorter than modelled.
- The stair top continues into an invented dark shaft (not photographed). (Corrected 17 November 2026: no longer. The flight was measured on all the stair photographs, DSC_0014 to DSC_0016 and the S34 photographs, and rebuilt with 8 risers to a landing at 1.24 m that runs through the west wall to the closed street door; the shaft is gone and Room 1's ceiling is closed over the flight. See "Room 1, the slope of the stairs, the number of steps and the street's height" in the decision log. The long part of the flight is now measured, with the uncertainties given there. The note below is kept as it was.) (Note of 15 November 2026: the rest of the stairs was built from the reconstructor's written descriptions and her close-ups of 7 November 2026, by her account, and not measured on the photographs. This log had no entry on the stairs until then. The code comment named "DSC_0013–0016, panorama" from the first commit (ab4ee52, 23 October 2026) and later added "close-ups of the flight"; the commits that shaped the open side, the coping, the pier, the treads and the damp stains (c5865eb, 45ac653, fa1efbd, 71fe3a4, 4bdaa4a and a6d3e5d, all 7 November 2026) record no measurement, and the model had no wall-side parapet, which all three photographs of the stairs show. The foot was placed so that the last step ends about 50 cm before Wrong Data Landscape Dresden (9befc56, 23 October 2026, "as in the panorama"). 9befc56 does not mention any report from the reconstructor. The same commit made Room 1 provisionally larger, and a PROGRESS.md line written the same day (454eb74, eight minutes later; dropped in 8e01123, 30 October 2026) gave her report of "little space between the last stair and the first painting" as the reason the room was made larger (corrected 15 November 2026: this note first gave the report as the reason for 9befc56's placing of the foot, and left out a6d3e5d). How the run of 0.26 m, the rise of 0.19 m and the 17 steps were chosen is not recorded. The foot is now rebuilt from DSC_0016, DSC_0014 and DSC_0015: see "Room 1, the foot of the stairs" in the decision log. The long part of the flight is still not measured.)
- The papier-mâché trees (#6 on the measured plan) were not exhibited in the end, so they are not modelled. The Let it Flow video was projected on the floor over the plastic river (confirmed by the owner). Wiena Wrong Landscape I/II: the cyan video is #8 in Room 3, the red one #15 in Room 4 (room sheet and photos). (Note of 16 November 2026, the artist (reported by the reconstructor, 16 November 2026): the river in the floor video fell on the plastic river, mapped onto it sideways. The walk turns the floor video so that its river lies along the plastic (`letItFlow.js`); whether its turn and side match his "sideways" exactly was not checked against the video. Nothing in the scene changed.)
- Website texts follow the owner's spreadsheet where they edited them (Behind the scenes, the works-list note, the MAMŰ link label). Where the sheet still held values from before the Word files (credits order, the text credit, the Hungarian exhibition text, the address with the entrance, the video captions), the Word files' versions are kept. Behind the scenes says "the screens play the artist's videos with their own sound" where the sheet said they "show the frames caught on camera" (true before the videos arrived).
- Room 2: the room sheet (plan and works list) hung on the bar pillar beside the toilet door, as DSC_0048 and DSC_0049 show. It was removed from the model on 9 November 2026 by the reconstructor on her own assumption (this log then named the wrong decider), so the pillar is bare brick for now. It will be put back (a later task). See the decision log. (Note of 17 November 2026: it is back, A4, on the wall west of the toilet corridor, where DSC_0048 shows it, not on the bar pillar; see the decision log.)
- Hosting: Netlify builds `main` (`netlify.toml`). The legal notice and privacy page name Netlify, Inc. as host (address from Netlify's privacy policy). The site moved from the earlier GitHub Pages repository (artificial-paradise) to this one (artificial-paradise-digital), without the original photos in its history; the GitHub Pages workflow was removed. Live at https://artificial-paradise.netlify.app/. The old address (szilvia-barath.github.io/artificial-paradise/) forwards there, keeping ?lang= and any path, from the public repository `artificial-paradise`, which holds only the forwarding page; the original repository is private, renamed `artificial-paradise-archive`.

## Rendering and delivery (from 17 November 2026, branch `wip/p5-rendering`)
These entries are about presentation: how the 3D view is drawn and delivered, from `docs/design/rendering-proposal.md`. None of them changes the reconstruction (geometry, hang, lights, colours, sound) unless the entry says so. Anything that would change how the works or rooms look is built behind a switch that is off, and waits for the reconstructor's yes.

- Rendering tiers vary resolution and antialiasing by device; no light, colour, geometry or sound varies by tier. One material detail does: the anti-tiling bump on the floor tiles is off on the low-power path (`applyPlanAO`, `tileBump`), as before. Logged here as the proposal asks (option 1). Decided by: reconstructor (the proposal's ranked list), assistant (the wording).
- Pixel cap (proposal option 1, 17 November 2026): the drawing buffer holds at most 2.1 million pixels (`MAX_PIXELS` in `src/main.js`, about 1920 × 1080), whatever the pixel ratio. The pixel ratio drawn is the smaller of the wanted one (the screen's, at most 2 on a computer and 1.5 on a phone, then the adaptive step-down) and the one that keeps the buffer under the cap. A 1440 × 900 laptop at 2× drew 2880 × 1800 (5.2 million pixels, about 636 MB of post-processing buffers by the proposal's estimate); it now draws at 1.27×, 1833 × 1146 (about 250 MB). A 1366 × 768 window at 1× (1.05 million) and phones (at most about 0.75 million at 1.5×) are under the cap and unchanged. MSAA: 4 samples on the desktop path as before, 2 when the ratio drawn is 1.75 or more (`src/fx/post.js`), none on low power. Adaptive resolution (`adapt()`): it may change the ratio at most once in 4 s, rises only after two fast 2 s spells in a row and not within 10 s of a step down, and makes no rise the cap would cancel; every change reallocates the composer's buffers. Only sharpness changes. Decided by: reconstructor (the proposal's ranked list, no. 2); assistant (2.1 million, the 4 s and 10 s). Not a reconstruction value. Evidence: the proposal, part a; the measurements under "Rendering measurements" below. Uncertainty: none for the scene; frame times on real devices are to be checked with `tests/perf/fps-overlay.js`.
- Compression and caching on Netlify (proposal option 3, 17 November 2026). The build writes the plant model a second time, gzipped (`models/paradise-factory-plant.glb.gz`, by a small plugin in `vite.config.js`), and `src/world/paradiseFactory.js` loads that file and unzips it in the browser (`DecompressionStream`, in every current browser). Netlify sends a `.glb` as `application/octet-stream` without compression; the proposal's other route, giving `.glb` the type `model/gltf-binary` and hoping Netlify then compresses it, depends on a list of types Netlify does not publish, so the file is compressed by the build instead. Measured on a build: 1,004,584 bytes, gzipped 435,813 bytes (57% less, 569 KB saved on the first visit). The plain `.glb` stays beside it: in dev, in a browser without `DecompressionStream`, or if the `.gz` fails, it is loaded as before. `netlify.toml` now gives `/models/*.glb.gz` the type `application/gzip` (so it is never sent with `Content-Encoding` and unzipped twice), `.glb` the type `model/gltf-binary`, and `/video/*` `Cache-Control: public, max-age=86400` (a browser keeps a loop for a day before asking again; Netlify's default asks every time). The KTX2 textures gain under 1% from gzip and are left as they are. Lossless: the model is the same bytes once unzipped. Decided by: reconstructor (the proposal's ranked list, no. 3); assistant (gzip in the build rather than a content type; one day for the videos). Not a reconstruction value. Evidence: `ls -l dist/models` after `npm run build`. Uncertainty: the headers act only on Netlify; check them on a branch deploy before `main` (`curl -sI <deploy>/models/paradise-factory-plant.glb.gz` should show `content-type: application/gzip` and no `content-encoding`). A loop replaced under the same name may show its old version for up to a day.

- The exhibition-text sheets by device (proposal option 5, 17 November 2026): the two sheets' canvases are drawn no sharper than the screen can show them from the nearest point a visitor can stand, 0.27 m in front of the sheet (the walking radius), with the field of view and the capped pixel ratio of the 3D view (`sheetScale` in `src/world/gallery.js`, using `src/fx/resolution.js`, which `src/main.js` now shares). A phone or tablet is reckoned upright and on its side. The scale never goes above the old fixed one (4× on the desktop path, 2.5× on low power), and not below 1×. Worked out per screen (canvas, GPU memory per sheet with mipmaps): 1366 × 768 at 1×, 2.14×, 1095 × 1548, 8.6 MB (was 2048 × 2896, 31.6 MB); 1920 × 1080 at 1×, 3.0×, 1540 × 2177, 17.1 MB; 1440 × 900 at 2×, 3.2×, 1633 × 2310, 19.2 MB; 2560 × 1440, 3.0×, 17.3 MB; phones and tablets stay at 2.5×, 1280 × 1810, 11.8 MB, as before. So 29 to 46 MB less for the two sheets on the desktop path. The sheets' size, place and text are unchanged; only how many pixels hold the letters. At the nearest point without zoom the letters are as sharp as before; zoomed in closer than that they soften a little, as the proposal said they might (check by eye). If the sheets' size changes, `SHEET_HEIGHT` in `gallery.js` must change with it (it is the plane's height, 0.707 m). Decided by: reconstructor (the proposal's ranked list, no. 4); assistant (0.27 m, the floor of 1×). Not a reconstruction value. Evidence: the formula in `gallery.js`; measured memory under "Rendering measurements" below. Uncertainty: `screen.width` and `screen.height` are read once at load, so a window dragged to a larger monitor keeps the first monitor's sheets.
- Merged meshes (proposal option 6, 17 November 2026). Paradise Factory's small opaque parts are now one mesh per material: the channel with its two end caps and the elbow (4 meshes to 1), the LED housings and the bars' black posts (4 to 1), the diffusers and the bars' light strips (4 to 1), the nine chains (9 to 1) and the twelve white corner joints of the plant's frame (12 to 1), so 28 draw calls fewer when the whole installation is in view. The four radiators were one instanced mesh each; each is now a plain mesh of its fins, which the existing merge in `props.js` folds into the mesh of its material (white, or the pale paint of Room 4's pipes), so 4 fewer. The merge (`mergeStatic`, moved from `props.js` to `src/world/merge.js`) keeps meshes with a different `userData.status` apart, for the planned "measured or guessed" view (no part carries a status yet), bakes the geometry relative to its group, and in Paradise Factory leaves alone what is transparent (clear plastic, the fluid), has several materials (the card) or is instanced (leaves, pebbles). Before it, `src/world/ao.js` was fixed to read the world position of an instanced mesh with its instance matrix (no patched material is on one yet, so nothing changed on screen). Nothing moves: every vertex is where it was, with the same material. Decided by: reconstructor (the proposal's ranked list, no. 5); assistant (which parts). Not a reconstruction value. Evidence: the code; draw calls per room and the A/B screenshots under "Rendering measurements" below. Uncertainty: none for the scene.
- Switch "tone", built and OFF (proposal option 7, 17 November 2026). Waiting for the reconstructor's yes; nothing on screen changes until then. Switches live in `src/fx/switches.js`; for a comparison one can be turned on for one page load with `?render=tone` in the address (nothing is stored). Under this switch: (1) the video screens (`gallery.js`, all six: the Paradise Factory screen and nos 6, 7, 8, 15 and 17) show the video's own values: their shader undoes the composer's PBR Neutral tone mapping in advance, at the renderer's exposure (1.35), so after it each pixel is the file's value, up to 0.97 of white (the curve cannot reach higher), and the 1.1 brightness is dropped. The inverse ignores the curve's small desaturation of very bright colours. (2) The last pass is dithered, so dark gradients do not band in 8-bit output. (3) On the low-power path FXAA's edge thresholds are a quarter of the defaults (0.0078 and 0.031), because it runs on linear values and misses most edges in the dark rooms. The exposure (1.35) and the curve for everything else (walls, paintings, prints, text sheets) are unchanged: choosing another exposure is a reconstruction decision the proposal leaves to a test chart, not taken here. Room 4: its two screens (nos 15 and 17) would change like the others; Let it Flow's floor video and wall projection (`letItFlow.js`) are not touched and stay tone-mapped, so under the switch Room 4's screens and its projection would follow different transforms. Status if switched on: invented (a display transform, not evidence). Decided by: assistant (built, off); reconstructor to decide. Evidence: the tone table under "Display transform" in the decision log; screenshots in the report of 17 November 2026. Uncertainty: the inverse is exact for greys and close for colours below 0.76; on a real screen the result also depends on the bloom, vignette and grain after it.
- Switch "probes", built and OFF (proposal option 11, 17 November 2026; `?render=probes` for one load). Under it the hemisphere light (`lights.js`, sky 0xf2f4ff, ground 0x4a443e, 0.85, the same in all four rooms) is switched off and a grid of light probes is baked in the browser from the model itself (`src/fx/probes.js`, three r186 `LightProbeGridWebGL`): probes every 0.75 m over the four rooms' plan and at 0.3, 1.1, 1.9 and 2.6 m, 8 × 8 cube faces, the direct light and one bounce. Each surface then gets the light its own room throws back (Room 2 warmer from its pendants, Room 4 only what its projection light and screens give). The grid cannot be saved in r186, so it is baked at every load once the title screen says "Ready"; the module is loaded only when the switch is on. No environment map was added: the proposal's `PMREMGenerator.fromScene` for the metals and glass would light every material and is left for a separate decision. Room 4: its lights and materials are not changed, but losing the hemisphere light darkens it, and its materials were darkened earlier to make up for that light; the projection light (`lights.proj`) changes over time with the video and is baked at the moment of the bake. Light leaks through the 30 to 35 cm walls where a probe stands in or beside a wall. Status if switched on: inferred, computed from the 7 lights, so it inherits their "inferred, set by eye" status; removing the hemisphere light ends an invented sky fill. Decided by: assistant (built, off; spacing and heights); reconstructor to decide. Evidence: the code; screenshots in the report of 17 November 2026. Uncertainty: the grid's light is as uncertain as the 7 lights' positions and intensities.
- Switch "ktx2art", built and OFF (proposal option 4, texture encoding, 17 November 2026; `?render=ktx2art` for one load). Under it the paintings' and prints' images in the rooms (the 1024 px `-tex` and the 1600 px detail images, and the installations' in-situ frames) load as KTX2 UASTC from `img/ktx2/` (`gallery.js`, through a second KTX2 loader in `textures.js` made only when the switch is on), and fall back to the WebP if a file is missing. `scripts/make_art_ktx2.mjs` writes them: UASTC level 2, mipmaps, sRGB, zstd, flipped like the brick KTX2; from lossless masters with `--from <folder>`, otherwise from the site's WebP. `public/img/ktx2/` is git-ignored: nothing of it is committed before a yes, and then only from the masters, which are not on this machine (the artist's reproductions in `reproductions/` and the crops of the photographs). Measured on 17 November 2026 from the WebP (second generation, for measuring only), 48 files: GPU memory if all were loaded 277 MB decoded, 69 MB as UASTC transcoded to ASTC, BC7 or ETC2 (a quarter); on disk and on the wire 7.2 MB as WebP, 56.1 MB as KTX2 (7.8 times as much; the 24 room images alone 2.1 MB against 16.9 MB). Fidelity of the KTX2 against the WebP it was made from, decoded with three's Basis transcoder: SSIM (luma, 8 × 8 windows) median 0.9987, lowest 0.9923 (no. 11's print, the fine print the proposal named), PSNR at least 32.1 dB. So the GPU saving costs a much larger download; RDO UASTC or ETC1S would shrink the files and lose more detail. Status if switched on: the works stay "measured" (the artist's files), with "lossy encoding" added to the uncertainty. Decided by: assistant (built, off; settings); reconstructor to decide. Evidence: `public/img/ktx2/report.json` (local); the SSIM run in the report of 17 November 2026. Uncertainty: the SSIM compares with the WebP, not with the masters.
## Decision log (from 13 November 2026)
Each entry names who decided: artist (Kristóf Szabó said so), reconstructor (Szilvia Baráth decided), assistant (the coding assistant decided without being told) or unknown. Each also says whether the content is measured, inferred or invented, the evidence and the uncertainty.

- Titles of nos 6 and 7: "Artificial Paradise – Singapore" (no. 6, Room 2 west wall) and "Artificial Paradise – The Eden project" (no. 7, Room 2 north wall), in place of "Artificial Paradise I" and "II". Decided by: reconstructor. Inferred. Evidence: the artist's text on the two videos (one on Singapore's parks, one on the Eden Project in Cornwall) and the source files of the loops (`artifical paradise_singapore.mp4` → no. 6, `artifical paradise_The Eden Project_sound.mp4` → no. 7); the assignment of videos to screens is unchanged. Uncertainty: the gallery's sheet numbered them I and II; the artist has not confirmed the exact wording or capitals.
- Ids follow the titles: no. 6 `w06-artificial-paradise-singapore`, no. 7 `w07-artificial-paradise-eden-project` (and its second frame `-b`), and the Wiena ids swapped through temporary names, so no. 8 is `w08-wiena-wrong-landscape-i` and no. 15 `w15-wiena-wrong-landscape-ii`. Every file and key that carries them changed with them (`exhibition.json`, `images.json`, `scripts/crops.json`, `scripts/make_videos.sh`, the `works` keys in both i18n files, `public/img`, `public/video`); file contents are unchanged. Decided by: artist (no. 8, the cyan video in Room 3, is Wiena Wrong Landscape I; no. 15, the red video in Room 4, is II; said on 6 November 2026, titles changed in commit 8e65d73, the ids kept the old order until now). Measured (the artist's statement). Evidence: the artist; the gallery's sheet gave the opposite numbering. Uncertainty: none for the numbering.
- Human as Error (#2, #4, #5): life emerged "3.5 billion" years ago (EN) and "3,5 milliárd" éve (HU), where the text had "3.5 million" / "3,5 millió". Nothing else in the text changed. Decided by: artist. Measured (the artist's correction). Evidence: the artist; it also matches the usual scientific figure. Uncertainty: none.
- The English text on the two Artificial Paradise videos (#6, #7): the Eden Project is in "Cornwall, England", where it said "Cornwall, Scotland". The Hungarian ("cornwalli") names no country and is unchanged. Decided by: reconstructor. Measured (Cornwall is in England). Evidence: geography; the Eden Project is near St Austell, Cornwall. Uncertainty: none.
- Sources of the facts and the plan. Titles, sizes, media and years come from two sources: the room sheet displayed in the gallery (beside the toilet door, seen in DSC_0049) and the floor plan image `Alaprajz_ok new.jpg`, with later corrections from the artist. The plan of the rooms was traced from `Alaprajz_ok new.jpg` at 0.026 m per plan pixel and checked against the building's outline on OpenStreetMap; it was not traced from the sheet in DSC_0049, as this log, the source note, the comment at the top of `src/world/layout.js` and the first sentence of Behind the scenes (EN/HU) had said. All five are corrected. Decided by: reconstructor. Measured. Evidence: `Alaprajz_ok new.jpg`, DSC_0049, OpenStreetMap. Uncertainty: none about the sources; the scale is as stated above (0.026 m per plan pixel).
- Sound: the four synthesised sounds are removed: the drone, the room tone (with a 50 Hz hum), the bar murmur with glass clinks and the projector fan. They were added by the assistant on 22 October 2026 (commit ceba631): the sound of the exhibition was the sound of the videos. Decided by: reconstructor. Invented (the removed sounds). Evidence: the reconstructor's account of the exhibition. Uncertainty: none. Kept as before: the videos' own sound, its placing in the rooms (`src/audio/ambience.js`), the sound question after Enter and the mute control. Nothing else read the removed layers: the image effects (`src/fx/post.js`) read only the `glitch` value, which the removed layers never fed. Behind the scenes (EN/HU) no longer says the room tone is synthesised. The noise burst between rooms and the echo on the videos were removed afterwards (the next two entries), so no invented sound or sound effect is left. What remains only sets each video's level and place: its gain by distance and room, its stereo pan, the master gain, and a compressor on the master bus (threshold −18 dB, ratio 3, added by the assistant on 22 October 2026 in ceba631, a presentation choice, invented; it changes loudness, not the sound, and stays, see the entry on the compressor). When the loops are encoded, Natural Life is raised by 15 dB and the Let it Flow wall loop by 21.5 dB (see the entries on sound levels).
- Sound: the short noise burst played when you cross between rooms or jump to a room (`glitchBurst`) is removed. It was added by the assistant on 22 October 2026 (commit ceba631). The visual pulse on the same moments (`post.trigger`) is an image effect, not sound, and stays. Decided by: reconstructor. Invented (the removed sound). Evidence: the sound of the exhibition was the sound of the videos. Uncertainty: none.
- Sound: the echo on the videos' own sound is removed: a feedback delay (113 and 171 ms, 55% feedback, low-passed at 2.2 kHz) that each video fed at 0.25 (`space` in `ambience.js`). It was added by the assistant on 22 October 2026 (commit ceba631). Kept: the videos' sound, its placing in the rooms, its fading with distance, the sound question and the mute control. Decided by: reconstructor. Invented (the removed effect). Evidence: the sound of the exhibition was the sound of the videos. Uncertainty: none.
- No file names in text a visitor reads. The source note under the works list now says (EN) "Titles, sizes, media and years come from the room sheet displayed in the gallery and the gallery's floor plan, with the artist's corrections." and (HU) "A címek, méretek, technikák és évszámok a galériában kifüggesztett teremlapról és a galéria alaprajzáról származnak, a művész javításaival.", each followed by the sentence on where the plan comes from; it no longer names `Alaprajz_ok new.jpg` or says "beside the toilet door". Behind the scenes now says "the gallery's floor plan" / "a galéria alaprajza", without the file name or "own" / "saját". No other visitor text names a file (the works' `photo` numbers are never shown). File names stay in this log and in code comments. Decided by: reconstructor. Measured (the sources themselves are unchanged; only the wording). Evidence: the two sources named in the entry on sources above. Uncertainty: none.
- The Paradise Factory screen (`w03-screen`) is titled "Paradise Factory" in both languages. It had shown "Paradise Factory (screen)" / "Paradise Factory (képernyő)", which is not a title: the room sheet lists only "Paradise Factory" (no. 3), and the screen is part of that work. (The assistant's commit of 13 November 2026 that matched the EN/HU keys wrongly called "Paradise Factory (screen)" the official title.) The title is set in `exhibition.json`; the EN/HU `works.w03-screen.title` overrides are gone, and the separate name lives only in the id. The details line is unchanged ("Video on a small screen, part of the installation, 2024" / its Hungarian). The title shows in the viewer (heading and image alt text), the hover prompt and the tour caption; the works list and the rooms menu leave the screen out. Decided by: reconstructor. Measured (the room sheet). Evidence: the room sheet (DSC_0049). Uncertainty: none.
- Hungarian label: "Kulisszák mögött" is now "A kulisszák mögött" in both places it starts a label: the navbar button and the small label above the panel's heading (the heading itself is "Hogyan készült"; the footer does not use it). English unchanged. Decided by: reconstructor. Not a reconstruction fact (interface wording), so neither measured, inferred nor invented applies; no evidence needed. Uncertainty: none.
- Sound levels, checked against `main` on 13 November 2026, nothing changed: every setting that sets the videos' volume is the same on this branch as on `main` (master gain 0.9; master compressor −18 dB, 3:1; each video's gain = 1.2 × its distance and room level; the viewer's focus 0 for a video, 0.4 for an image; the loops' files are byte-identical, only renamed). What the branch removed beside the videos: the synthesised sounds, which played under the videos, and the echo, which added about 0.05 dB to them; neither lowers the videos themselves. Measured loudness of the loops (EBU R128 integrated): Singapore −26.0 LUFS, The Eden project −26.2, Natural Life −32.1, Let it Flow wall −53.7. The last is about 28 dB below the others because commit 0da6234 (3 November 2026, already on `main`) re-cut it from `trees_new.mp4` with a sound gain of 0 dB, where the earlier Siluett loop was raised by 15 dB. Levels left as they are, pending the reconstructor's decision (the wall loop was restored afterwards, see the entry on its level). Decided by: reconstructor (to check, not change). Measured. Evidence: `scripts/make_videos.sh`, `src/audio/ambience.js` on both branches, ffmpeg `ebur128`. Uncertainty: ±0.5 LU on the loudness figures.
- Sound: the compressor on the master bus (`DynamicsCompressor`, threshold −18 dB, ratio 3, in `ambience.js`) stays. It was added by the assistant on 22 October 2026 (commit ceba631) as a presentation choice: it evens out the loudness of the videos, it is not part of the exhibition's sound. Decided by: reconstructor (to keep it). Invented. Evidence: git history; the gallery played the videos without it. Uncertainty: none.
- Sound: the Let it Flow wall loop (`video/w16-projection.mp4`) is back at the loudness of the Siluett loop it replaced. That loop (as before commit 0da6234) measures −32.2 LUFS; the re-cut of 3 November 2026 was encoded at 0 dB and measured −53.7 LUFS, because the 15 dB raise of the earlier loop was not carried over: the level was lost by accident. Re-encoded from the source, `siluett_short.mp4` (the reconstructor's 10 s cut of Siluett, −53.6 LUFS), with the same settings and a sound gain of 21.5 dB: it now measures −32.1 LUFS, peak −12.6 dBFS, so the limiter does not act. The picture is bit-identical to the previous loop (PSNR infinite); no other loop changed. Decided by: reconstructor. Measured (EBU R128 loudness of the old and new loops). Evidence: the loop before 0da6234 from git history, ffmpeg `ebur128`, `scripts/make_videos.sh`. Uncertainty: ±0.5 LU.
- The room sheet beside the toilet door (Room 2, bar pillar): it hung there, as DSC_0048 and DSC_0049 show. It was removed from the model on 9 November 2026 by the reconstructor on her own assumption; the entry written then named the wrong decider. It will be put back in a later task. Decided by: reconstructor. Measured (its place, from the photographs). Evidence: DSC_0048, DSC_0049. Uncertainty: none about its place. (Note of 17 November 2026: put back, on the wall west of the toilet corridor's mouth and not on the bar pillar: DSC_0048 shows the corridor on the left of the wall that carries it. See the entry of 17 November 2026 on the toilet corridor.)
- Wall labels: the gallery had none beside the works. The assistant had added them; they were removed because the artist said there were none. Decided by: artist. Measured (the artist's statement). Evidence: the artist; no label shows in the installation photographs. Uncertainty: none.
- Let it Flow, sizes: the code has a floor-video frame of 7.0 × 3.94 m and a river 4.74 m long (`src/data/river.json`, since commit e58490e, from the artist's real size of 4 × 7 m). This log had said 5.28 × 2.97 m and 3.58 m. On the photograph the river measures 3.55 m. The artist has been asked which is right; the river is unchanged. Decided by: unknown until the artist answers. Inferred (7 m from the artist's size, the river scaled with the frame). Evidence: the artist's size, `PLANE javított.png`, the photograph. Uncertainty: the river's length, 3.55 m or 4.74 m. (Note of 16 November 2026: the artist (reported by the reconstructor, 16 November 2026) says 4.74 m, the river's length in the walk, is right. The river stays inferred, now confirmed by the artist; the 3.55 m read on the photograph is the figure that is off, for a reason not looked into. He also gives Let it Flow as about 4 m wide, 7 m long and 2 m high, and says the 7 m is the whole installation, not the floor picture. The floor-video frame here (7.0 × 3.94 m) was scaled from that 7 m, so its size now has no source of its own and is open; on the reconstructor's instruction nothing in the scene is changed. Decided by: artist (the sizes); reconstructor (no change to the scene).) (Floor projection measured on the photographs, 16 November 2026; nothing in the scene changed. No photo shows the projection as a lit frame: on DSC_0112 to DSC_0117, all taken from one standpoint, it shows only as the video's light specks on the black floor, so every figure here is where the specks stop, not a frame edge. Width: 2.4 ±0.3 m across the room. North limit z 2.5 to 2.6 at x 22 to 23.5 (DSC_0112, DSC_0113), z 2.7 to 2.85 nearer the camera; south limit z 5.0 to 5.05 at x 22 to 24 (DSC_0113, DSC_0115, DSC_0117), z 4.8 to 4.95 nearer the camera (DSC_0114 to DSC_0117). Centre line z 3.78 ±0.1 (the walk: 3.73). Empty floor 0.7 to 1.1 m wide runs along each side wall, in view on DSC_0112 (north) and on DSC_0113 and DSC_0115 to DSC_0117 (south). Length: not measurable, because no photo shows the end towards the door. The specks run from x 20.1 (DSC_0117, at the bottom of the frame) to x 24.9 (DSC_0113), so the length is at least 4.8 ±0.2 m, or 5.3 m if the picture reaches the end wall's foot (the last 0.4 m before the wall is too foreshortened to read). Status: width measured, as the extent of the specks (the frame itself could be wider only if its margins were black); length a lower bound only. How: two measurers worked separately (camera solved from the end wall's corners and the side walls' foot lines, checked on the 30 cm floor tiles; specks detected and mapped onto the floor), a third compared them and checked the disagreements on the photos; their floor positions agree within 0.03 to 0.09 m. Against the walk: its frame is 3.94 m wide, about 1.5 m wider than the specks, and its east edge (`xEast` 25.739 in `river.json`) lies 0.34 m behind the end wall's face (x 25.40 in `layout.js`). Found on the way, not followed up: on these photos the plastic river measures about 4.1 m against the 4.74 m the artist confirms, and the camera comes out about 7.1 m from the end wall and 1.24 to 1.30 m high, against this log's 5.97 m and 1.52 m; lengths along the room are uncertain by up to 15% (the focal length), widths hardly at all. Decided by: reconstructor (to measure, and no change to the scene); assistant (the measurement).) (Note of 17 November 2026: superseded. The artist now confirms the river was about 4.0 to 4.35 m long (reported by the reconstructor, 17 November 2026); the digital exhibition has it at 4.05 m, and the floor picture is no longer a 7.0 × 3.94 m frame but the keystoned picture of the projector on the north wall. See "Let it Flow, the plastic river's length" and "Let it Flow, the floor projection" in this log, 17 November 2026. Decided by: artist (the range); reconstructor.)
- Let it Flow, floor projection: the code has its edge at x 25.74 and its centre at z 3.73 (`projection` in `river.json`); this log had said x 25.65 and z 3.85. Decided by: unknown (the values came with the e58490e rescale). Inferred. Evidence: `river.json`. Uncertainty: about ±10 cm, as the earlier and present values differ by that much. The "wall tip at z 4.25" from the old note is not set anywhere in the code and is unchecked.
- Reproductions: the seven paintings (#1, #2, #4, #5, #9, #10, #14) are trimmed to the canvas and set to the listed proportions; the three prints (#11–#13) are used whole (`scripts/crops.json`, `"full": true`). Decided by: unknown (the trim was set up before this log named deciders). Measured (the listed sizes). Evidence: `scripts/crops.json`, the listed sizes. Uncertainty: the trim lines are hand-placed, within a few pixels.
- The Paradise Factory plant model: `scripts/make_plant.mjs` makes four changes to the artist's model: the aluminium uprights are cut at the top frame (0.612 m; in the model they run on to 1.5 m), the two fittings on the plant box's rim are painted red, the pump's hose is made clear like the other hoses, and the water is made brownish. Decided by: assistant, matching the photographs at the reconstructor's request. Inferred. Evidence: the installation photographs and the documentation video. Uncertainty: the colours are by eye.
- The wall projection's loop: since commit 0da6234 (3 November 2026) the loop is cut from `siluett_short.mp4` (the script called it `trees_new.mp4` until 13 November 2026), a 10 s excerpt the reconstructor cut from Siluett where it moves most. Decided by: reconstructor. Measured (an excerpt of the artist's video, not altered). Evidence: `scripts/make_videos.sh` (`enc_loop "siluett_short.mp4"`). Uncertainty: none.
- The MAMŰ logo comes from an SVG the reconstructor supplied, not from a PDF as this log had said. Decided by: reconstructor. Measured (the gallery's own logo). Evidence: `public/brand/mamu-logo.svg`. Uncertainty: none; the gallery's permission to use it is still to be asked.
- The curator's text on the sheets and in the About panel is a placeholder from the exhibition announcement, to be replaced once the curator has signed (added under "Next"). Decided by: reconstructor. Inferred (placeholder text). Evidence: the exhibition announcement. Uncertainty: the final text is not known yet.
- Lighting: the general lighting belongs to the gallery; the darkness of Room 4 is part of Let it Flow. (Note of 18 November 2026: Room 4's surfaces were brightened against its projections to the ratios of the 2025 photographs; see "Room 4, the pipes' colour and the room's light".) Decided by: reconstructor. Inferred (the light levels are set by eye from the photographs). Evidence: the installation photographs; Room 4 is lit only by its projections in DSC_0112–0117. Uncertainty: brightness and colour of the light are approximate.
- A generative projection existed from 22 to 23 October 2026: an audio-reactive shader piece standing in for Let it Flow, added by the assistant in commit ceba631 and removed in 30aaa0d, when the photographed projection and the plastic river replaced it. Decided by: assistant (added), reconstructor (the reconstruction from photographs that replaced it). Invented. Evidence: git history. Uncertainty: none.
- Baked shadows: the darkening along walls, floors and furniture (`src/world/ao.js`, ambient occlusion baked from the plan) and the soft contact shadows under the works (`src/world/gallery.js`) are a rendering technique, not a record of the gallery's light. Decided by: assistant. Invented (a visual aid). Evidence: the code. Uncertainty: not applicable; they do not stand for measured light.
- Display transform: every pixel of the 3D view, the artworks included, passes through Khronos PBR Neutral tone mapping (`ToneMappingEffect`, mode NEUTRAL, inside the postprocessing composer) at `renderer.toneMappingExposure = 1.35` (`src/fx/post.js`, set by the assistant in the render-quality pass of 23 October 2026, commit 885afcd). It does not keep the artworks' colours, as this log said: it keeps hue, darkens the dark tones and lifts the light ones. For a surface whose lit value equals its texture, computed from the three.js r186 shader:

  | sRGB in the file | 50 | 128 | 200 | 255 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Artworks and walls, on screen | 28 | 137 | 223 | 247 |
  | Video screens (brightness 1.1), on screen | 31 | 144 | 232 | |

  `toneMapped: false` on the light pools, the wall projection and the floor video has no effect under the composer (three.js applies a material's tone mapping only when it draws straight to the screen), so those surfaces get the same exposure and curve. Bloom, the vignette, grain and colour fringes come after it and are display effects too. Decided by: reconstructor (to keep it for now and to log it as it is); assistant (the original settings). Invented (a display transform, not evidence of how the works or the rooms looked). Evidence: `src/fx/post.js`; the tone table and its sources in `docs/design/rendering-proposal.md`, option 7. Uncertainty: the table is computed, not measured on a screen, and holds for a fully lit surface; in the dim rooms the works are darker again by the lighting.
- Wiena Wrong Landscape I (no. 8, Room 3) and II (no. 15, Room 4): the 4:3 loops (960 × 720) are cropped to the 16:9 screens (1.21 × 0.68 m) at full width, as the photographs show. Until now each screen showed the whole frame squashed to 0.75 of its height. Now each shows a band 0.749 of the frame's height (4:3 on a 1.21 × 0.68 m screen), starting at `cropTop`: no. 8 from 0.085 to 0.834, no. 15 from 0.148 to 0.897, as fractions of the frame's height from the top (a centred band would run from 0.125 to 0.875). Set in `src/data/exhibition.json` (`video.mediaAspect` 1.3333 and `video.cropTop`) and applied in the screen shader in `src/world/gallery.js`, to the loop only: the photographed stills shown until the loop plays are already cut to the screen's shape and are not cut again. A loop with `mediaAspect` and no `cropTop` is centred. The Paradise Factory screen (`w03-screen`, top-level `mediaAspect` 0.8313, cropped on the geometry) is unchanged. The video files stay 4:3, and the viewer still plays the whole frame. Evidence: the in-situ derivatives of DSC_0089 (`img/w08-wiena-wrong-landscape-i-situ.webp`) and DSC_0120 (`img/w15-wiena-wrong-landscape-ii-situ.webp`), each the whole photograph at 933 × 1400, and the 1600 × 900 crops `img/w08-wiena-wrong-landscape-i.webp` and `img/w15-wiena-wrong-landscape-ii.webp`; the full-size originals were not used. The lit picture on each TV was fitted edge by edge and matched against frames of the loops: no. 8 at 00:17.7 (also 00:10.3, 00:15.9, 00:18.4, 00:29.1, 00:36.5 and 00:37.2; the loop repeats about every 18.8 s), no. 15 at 00:10.7 (also 00:08.6, 00:08.9, 00:10.5 to 00:10.8, 00:12.4, 00:12.5, 00:18.9 and 00:33.7). Both TVs show the full width of the frame, overscanned by about 1.5%, and 0.74 of its height. A stretch to 16:9 matches far worse (gradient match 0.08 to 0.25, against 0.60 to 0.88 for the crop). Band as photographed, measured, ±0.005 per edge: no. 8 top 0.0885 (first measurement), 0.093 (second measurement, by two methods), 0.091 to 0.095 (the assistant's check while building this, from the thickness of the dark strip along the top of the picture against the frame at 00:17.7); bottom 0.828 to 0.829. No. 15 top 0.151 to 0.152 (0.155 to 0.156 if the dark line under the bezel is bezel and not picture), bottom 0.894 to 0.895. The top edge of no. 8 is the least certain: one pixel of the derivative is 0.0035 of the screen's height, and dark picture runs right under the bezel. The 0.75 band is inferred: centred on the measured band, assuming the overscan was the same at top and bottom. For no. 8 that gives 0.083 to 0.833 from the first measurement and 0.086 to 0.836 from the second; the code takes the middle, top 0.085. For no. 15 both give 0.148 to 0.898. So the picture of no. 8 sits 0.040 of the frame's height above centre and that of no. 15 0.023 below. Uncertainty: ±0.01 of the frame's height per edge (about ±7 of 720 rows), which covers both measurements. No bricks were counted: everything is measured in fractions of the screen and of the frame, so no scale was needed. The overscan of the photographed TVs (a slight zoom) is not copied. Also corrected: the comment in `scripts/make_videos.sh` said "the TVs stretched 4:3 to 16:9"; it now says they cropped. No line of this log said the walk or the gallery stretched the Wiena loops. Not changed: in `scripts/crops.json` the quad of no. 8 sits about 7 px (of 933) inside the lit picture at its top right, so `img/w08-wiena-wrong-landscape-i.webp` cuts a sliver off that corner; a separate small fix. Decided by: artist (crop, not stretch, 13 November 2026, reported by the reconstructor); reconstructor (to match the photographs); offsets worked out by the assistant. Band measured; offsets inferred. (Note of 16 November 2026: the artist (reported by the reconstructor, 16 November 2026) confirms again that a 16:9 crop cut a little at the top and the bottom, and that nothing was stretched. Nothing changed.)
- Room 4, the left (north) wall: two recesses, A and B, built from the photographs. Until now the wall was one solid block, x 15.6 to 25.8, z 1.33 to 1.73, 2.9 m high (`src/world/layout.js`, old line 160). It is now built by `room4NorthWall()` (`layout.js` lines 101 to 138, called at line 201), all bare brick (`rawBrick`, as before): the wall west of A, recess A, the pier, recess B. The strip of the old block behind the end wall (x 25.4 to 25.8) is dropped; the end wall's own block already fills it. The end wall had no recess or setback in the model, and the photographs show none, so nothing was removed there. The vault (`architecture.js`) is unchanged: both recesses run up to its springing at 2.75 m, and the wall from 2.75 to 2.9 m closes them at the top. The walk is unchanged: each recess is filled out to the wall face below its sill or its lowest step, so the collision line stays at z 1.73.
  - Evidence. DSC_0112 (28 January 2025, 17:51:14), the only one of DSC_0112 to DSC_0117 that shows this wall; DSC_0113 and DSC_0114 show the end wall flat, with no recess; DSC_0115 to DSC_0117 show the right wall only. The photograph `_artificial paradise 04 - 03` (its derivative `public/img/w16-let-it-flow-b.webp`). `w16-documentation.mp4` frames 00:00.0, 00:01.0 and 00:02.0 (frames 00:03.0 to 00:08.6 show no left wall). MAMŰ's photograph of the bare room in 2013 (`mamu.jpg`, source S32, reference only): both recesses, the toothed fill, the bare brick. The crop of DSC_0109 (`public/img/w17-natural-life-situ.webp`). Scale: brick courses of 7.7 cm on the pier in DSC_0112, 7 courses over 504 px at image x 1050 and 11 courses over 758 px at x 1080 (first measurement), about 74 px a course at x 1000 (second measurement); about 12 to 12.5 courses below A's sill; checked against the Natural Life screen (0.52 m picture height, a 42-inch panel) and the 30 cm floor tiles.
  - Two measurements, and the choice between them. The first put A at x 21.95 to 22.82 (0.87 m wide), the pier to 24.12, B from 24.12; the second, checking by cross-ratio on the 04-03 photograph and the video scaled by the screen's width, put A at 21.78 to 22.75 (0.97 m), the pier to 24.08, B from 24.08. The cross-ratio of the four edges (A's west arris, A's east arris, B's west arris, the end-wall corner) needs no camera and no scale, and it is the same in all three views: 1.255 on DSC_0112, 1.258 on 04-03, 1.258 in the video. So the views agree; the two results differ only in the values each took for A's east arris and B's west arris. The model takes the mean of those (22.78 and 24.10) and solved A's west arris from the cross-ratio: 21.86. With either measurer's pair the same cross-ratio gives 21.74 to 22.01, which the uncertainty below covers.
  - A: x 21.86 to 22.78, width 0.92 m. Measured, west arris ±0.15 (covers 21.78 and 21.95), east arris ±0.08, width ±0.12. Sill flat at 0.95 m: measured, ±0.06 (0.91 and 0.98 in the first measurement, 0.96 and 0.99 to 1.01 in the second). Toothed fill on the sill against the back, stepping down eastwards from 1.49 m at the west jamb through 1.43, 1.38, 1.32 and 1.25 to 1.15 m, ending at x 22.46: measured on DSC_0112 (the first measurement's steps, moved 0.09 m west with the arris), confirmed in kind by the second and by `mamu.jpg`; step heights ±0.05, step ends ±0.08. How far the fill stands proud of the back, 5 cm, is invented (it leaves 7 cm of sill, where the video shows two bottles). A round pipe, 8 cm across, stands on the sill against the east jamb, x 22.72, up to the big pipe (`src/world/props.js`, under the comment "a round pipe standing on recess A's sill" (this reference gave line numbers until 15 November 2026; later edits higher up in the file moved the code, so it now names the code comment)): its place is measured on DSC_0112, 04-03 and the video at 00:00.0 (±0.05); what it is (a pipe, or a post) is inferred; its distance from the back is invented. Fix of 15 November 2026: this line said the pipe ran "up to the big pipe", but it stopped at y 2.46, level with the big pipe's underside and 3 cm behind it (the pipe spans z 1.63 to 1.71, the big pipe 1.74 to 1.94), so its open top end could be seen from the room. It now runs on up to 2.78 m, into the wall that closes the recess at the vault's springing (2.75 m), and passes behind the big pipe. Its top is hidden behind the big pipe in DSC_0112, 04-03 and the video, so where it goes above 2.46 m is not seen: running it into the wall is inferred, not bent out to the big pipe, because nothing shows a bend. Fix decided by: assistant, after a review of the model.
  - Pier: x 22.78 to 24.10, 1.32 m. Measured, ±0.10.
  - B: x 24.10 to the end wall (25.40), 1.30 m. Measured, west arris ±0.10 (covers 24.08 and 24.12). Its bottom: the west arris comes down to 1.38 m (1.36 in the first measurement, 1.40 to 1.42 in the second; ±0.06), then steps down eastwards to 1.15, 1.02, 0.88 and 0.71 m (measured, heights ±0.05, step positions ±0.08) and about 0.51 m from x 25.18 to the corner, which the low pipes hide (inferred). Filled out to the wall face, as Room 3's openings are.
  - Depth of both, 0.12 m (backs at z 1.61): inferred. For A the first measurement read 0.10 to 0.13 m from the east jamb, but the second showed that this strip is probably the pipe itself; range 0.05 to 0.15 m. For B nothing can be measured (range 0.04 to 0.20 m); it copies A.
  - Tops: hidden behind the big pipe in every photograph, 2025 and 2013. Running them up to the vault is inferred from `mamu.jpg`, where the recess goes on above the pipe, and from Room 3's openings. Top shape (flat or arched) unknown.
  - Kept for January 2025: no radiator and no ledge under A or B. In `mamu.jpg` (2013) a radiator stands on a concrete ledge in front of A; neither is in DSC_0112.
  - Moved with the recesses, so nothing hangs in front of an opening. Natural Life (`src/data/exhibition.json` line 497): along 22.077 to 21.32, height unchanged (1.5; the first measurement gives 1.52, within ±0.03). The first measurement gave 21.32; the second gave 20.95 ±0.2, correcting for the screen standing about 6 cm off the wall; mapping the 04-03 photograph and the video onto the wall from the four edges above puts the screen's centre at 21.28 to 21.30 before that correction, about 21.1 after it. 21.32 is used because the slanting drain pipe in the model (placed earlier from DSC_0109 and DSC_0111) would run through the screen's top corner at any value further west. Inferred, uncertainty −0.40 / +0.05 m. The black box with its light (`props.js`, under the comment "small black box with a light and a round camera" (this reference gave line numbers until 15 November 2026; later edits higher up in the file moved the code, so it now names the code comment)): x 22.75 and 22.77 to 21.80 and 21.82 (first measurement 21.97; 21.77 to 21.95 on 04-03), measured, ±0.15. The round camera (under the same comment): x 22.48 to 21.66, height 2.22 to 2.15 m (first measurement 21.74 and 2.15; 21.66 on 04-03), measured, ±0.10 along and ±0.05 in height.

The model's props on this wall placed from DSC_0109 and DSC_0111 sit 0.7 to 1.0 m east of where DSC_0112, 04-03 and the video put them; the drains probably do too, and need the full DSC_0109 to DSC_0111 to check. The narrow vertical member west of Natural Life in the DSC_0109 crop (x about 20.4 to 20.6) is not modelled; it may be a third, filled opening or only a pipe.
