- Room 4, the pale pipe along the top of the end wall, above the Siluett projection: thinner, with five sockets, a domed cap at its south end and its branch turned the right way. Code: `src/world/props.js`, from the comment "Measured on DSC_0113 (4 m wall, 652 px/m)" to the branch's rounded end (this reference gave line numbers until 15 November 2026; later edits higher up in the file moved the code, so it now names the code comment) (the comment at `src/world/letItFlow.js` lines 21 to 24 only). All numbers below are in the model's frame (Room 4 4.00 m wide from the plan; a 2.9 m ceiling over shallow vaults that spring at 2.75 m, so the end wall shows only 2.75 m) unless they say "courses".
  - Evidence. DSC_0113 (28 January 2025, 17:51:20, turned upright, 4000 × 6000), the frontal view: the end wall spans x 780 to 3395 px at the pipe's height, so 652 px/m on the plan's 4.00 m (about the 259 px/m this log gave on a 1600 px copy), floor at y 4063, wall top at the corner y 2142 to 2144 (2.94 m). DSC_0116 (17:51:40): the cap at the SE corner and the brick courses (7.7 cm) of the south wall next to it. DSC_0117 (17:52:03): a second course count on the south wall. DSC_0115 (17:51:34) looked at, no numbers taken. `mamu.jpg` (MAMŰ, 2013, source S32): the end wall still bare painted brick, so courses can be counted on the end wall itself; the same pipe, white, with its sockets in the same places. `w16-documentation.mp4`: frames at 00:00.0, 00:00.3 and 00:00.8 show the pipe only at the very top edge, with a clear gap of about one pipe diameter over the picture; frames at 00:01.5, 00:03.0, 00:04.5, 00:06.0, 00:07.5 and 00:08.5 do not show it. The video gave nothing to measure, only that the picture never touches the pipe.
  - Two measurements, and where they differed. Both measured the same pixels. They differed on the brick-course pitch at the SE corner of DSC_0116: the first measurer used 55.3 px per course (from the south wall rectified through its vanishing point); the second counted a chain of 18 courses next to the corner (y 2697 to 3659, 962 px) and found 53.4 ±0.8 px, confirmed by a frequency count in six column bands. The assistant checked it a third way: the course pitch measured in 21 columns across the south wall (x 1100 to 3100, about 60 to 125 px) and fitted as a straight line gives 53.35 px at the corner; the same fit forced through the vanishing point gives 55.6. The direct count and the free fit do not depend on where the vanishing point is, and `mamu.jpg` counted independently agrees with 53.4 (axis 32.8 courses), so the assistant used 53.4 and gives 55.3 alongside. In courses this makes the end wall 2.77 to 2.81 m high at the corner (2.70 at 55.3 px) and Room 4 about 5% smaller than the plan (8% at 55.3 px). That is a question for the whole room and is not changed here.
  - Diameter: was Ø 0.17 m, now Ø 0.16 m (r 0.085 to 0.08). Measured. DSC_0113: 110 to 113 px between the centres of the dark outlines along the main run, 0.17 m (inner edges 0.15, outer edges 0.19). DSC_0116 in courses: 0.162 m at 53.4 px (2.1 courses), 0.156 at 55.3. `mamu.jpg`: about 0.19 m (2.5 courses), 0.17 to 0.23 m as a share of the wall's width, soft and low resolution. The assistant took 0.16 m: both course counts give it, it lies inside the photo's 0.15 to 0.19, and the reconstructor saw the pipe as too thick. Uncertainty ±2 cm. The last metre before the cap reads thinner on both DSC_0113 (0.147 m) and DSC_0116 (about 0.14 m in courses); both are lit by the same projector from below, so this may be light on the lower edge and not a reducer. Not modelled.
  - Axis height: 2.58 m, unchanged. Measured. DSC_0113: 2.60 to 2.61 m in the middle (2.59 at the north end, 2.63 at the south end), 0.88 to 0.90 of the end wall's height at the corner on DSC_0113 and DSC_0116. On the model's end wall, which shows 2.75 m (the last vault bay springs there; the vault reaches 2.89 m only in the middle of a bay), that share is 2.42 to 2.48 m, close to the course-based figures below; the 2.58 m kept here is 0.94 of it. (This line first said "which on the model's 2.9 m wall is 2.55 to 2.61 m": 2.9 m is the wrong height for this wall. Corrected 15 November 2026.) In courses: 32.6 courses = 2.51 m (53.4 px), 31.6 = 2.43 m (55.3 px); `mamu.jpg` 32.8 courses = 2.53 m; the photographs of 2013 and 2025 agree. Uncertainty ±5 cm in the model's frame; in true metres it depends on the room-wide scale (2.43 to 2.61 m). The underside is now at 2.50 m (it was 2.495); measured 2.51 m on DSC_0113.
  - Distance from the wall: still 2 cm (the axis moves from x 25.295 to 25.30 to keep it with the thinner pipe). Invented: the frontal photographs cannot show it.
  - Ends. North: unchanged at z 1.85, behind the rounded end of the dark pipe along the north wall; the pale pipe comes out at x 870 to 900 px on DSC_0113 (z 1.86 to 1.91). How the two join is hidden; that they join is inferred from `mamu.jpg`. South: was cut flat at z 5.60; now a domed cap (a half sphere of the pipe's radius) with its tip at z 5.68, 5 cm short of the south wall. Measured: tip at x 3360 to 3365 px on DSC_0113, about 4 cm (0.54 course) from the corner on DSC_0116; ±2 cm. It does not turn up into the ceiling.
  - Sockets (the collars): were four, at z 2.43, 3.43, 4.46 and 5.54, Ø 0.20 m, 10 cm long; now five, at z 2.00, 2.45, 3.44, 4.45 and 5.45, Ø 0.19 m, 12 cm long; the last is the cap's socket. Measured: centres at x 960, 1255 to 1260, 1900, 2560 and 3210 px on DSC_0113 (both measurements); `mamu.jpg` shows them at 0.44, 0.69 and 0.94 of the wall's width against 0.43, 0.68 and 0.93 on DSC_0113. Position ±3 cm, diameter ±2 cm, length ±2 cm.
  - Branch near the north corner: was r 0.075, slanting up towards the north from z 2.62 to 2.42 and then straight up to 2.92. Now r 0.062 (Ø 0.125), leaving the top of the pipe at z 2.30 and rising towards the south at about 47° to a rounded end just under the model's ceiling line, centre at y 2.685 and z 2.33, top at 2.75. Measured (both measurements: it leaves at z 2.32 to 2.33 and rises south at 45 to 50°, its end at z 2.47 just under the ceiling line); direction certain, diameter ±2 cm. The rounded end just under the ceiling line is as photographed; what is above it is unknown. Fix of 15 November 2026: first built with its end at y 2.84 and z 2.47, as measured, the end sat above the model's vault (2.76 to 2.81 m over the branch at x 25.24 to 25.36), which cut it off, so the rounded end could not be seen. The model's end wall shows 2.75 m where DSC_0113 shows 2.94 m at 652 px/m and the courses give 2.77 to 2.81 m, so the gap above the pipe's top is 0.09 m in the model against 0.25 to 0.27 m on DSC_0113 at 652 px/m (0.18 to 0.22 m in courses). The branch keeps the measured angle and its end stays just under the ceiling line, so it is shorter than photographed: its end is at z 2.33, not 2.47. End: inferred, ±4 cm. Making it full length needs a higher end wall, a question for the whole room (see the two measurements above), not changed here. Fix decided by: assistant, after a review of the model.
  - Colour: unchanged (0x8e8a83). In `mamu.jpg` the pipe is painted white like the wall; in 2025 it is pale grey-pink in the projector's light. Not part of this change.
  - Not modelled: a thin cable or small conduit (2 cm or less) about 5 to 7 cm above the pipe from about z 2.1 to the cap, a thin cable down the wall from the pipe at z 2.29 behind the projection, small dark points under the pipe next to three sockets (probably the screws of pipe clips, inferred). No bracket rings other than the sockets show.
  - The projection's top edge: 2.30 m, unchanged (`letItFlow.js` line 25, `TOP`). Measured: 2.30 m (first) and 2.32 m (second) on DSC_0113; in courses 28.7 = 2.21 m (53.4 px) or 28.0 = 2.16 m (55.3 px); 0.78 to 0.80 of the wall's height. The gap from the pipe's underside to the picture's top is 0.20 to 0.24 m on DSC_0113 and 3.0 courses (0.23 m) on DSC_0116; in the model it is 0.20 m. Only the comment changed: it said the picture stops "just under the pipe"; it now says about 20 cm under it.
  - Photo names: the DSC numbers are the files' own names as received.
  - Decided by: reconstructor (the pipe is too thick, 15 November 2026); assistant (the measurements).
- Room 1, the foot of the stairs: rebuilt from the three photographs of the stairs. Code: `src/world/props.js`, the Room 1 stairs block (from the comment "Stairs along the north wall"), and the tread colour, `stair` in `src/world/materials.js` (this reference gave line numbers until 15 November 2026; later edits higher up in the file moved the code, so it now names the code comment and the material). Frame: x east, y up, z south from the north wall's face. The bottom riser stays at x 2.31; run 0.26, rise 0.19 and 17 steps are unchanged.
  - Evidence. DSC_0014, DSC_0015 and DSC_0016, the three photographs of the stairs. The reconstructor first sent them without their numbers (the assistant's files 5.jpg, 7.jpg and 4.jpg). On 16 November 2026 she named them by content: DSC_0014 the stairs from behind the plant, close up, with the hanging bottle and the stacked chairs; DSC_0015 the whole flight from the side, with the plant at the left edge and the brick block at the foot; DSC_0016 the stairs with the painting on the wall to the right. The assistant matched the files to those descriptions (DSC_0014 = 5.jpg, DSC_0015 = 7.jpg, DSC_0016 = 4.jpg); the copies she sent again that day are pixel for pixel the same files. Decided by: reconstructor (the numbers); assistant (the matching). DSC_0016 is the main source: the foot from the side, with the 30 cm floor tiles and the north wall's brick courses. DSC_0015 was taken from practically the same spot (one homography maps DSC_0016 onto it to within 2 to 8 px), so it is a check on the reading, not a second viewpoint. DSC_0014 shows the middle of the flight and the wall-side parapet with the folding chairs behind it; the foot is out of its frame. The close-ups of 7 November 2026 add nothing independent: 1.png and 2.png are crops of DSC_0016, 3.png a crop of DSC_0015. No video frames were used.
  - Scale. Plan positions come from the floor tiles: two homographies fitted on DSC_0016 (13 joint intersections, residuals up to 1.7 cm; and 17, rms 0.44 cm), both assuming 30 x 30 cm tiles. Heights come from brick courses of 7.7 cm: 3 courses counted on the pier (2 full ones and about 2/3 of a third above the tiles; 45 px a course in DSC_0016, 47 to 48 px in DSC_0015), and the north wall's courses near the wall-side end (28.5 px a course in DSC_0016), plus a level camera model (camera 1.24 m above the floor).
  - Two measurements, and where they differed. The main difference was where the wall-side parapet stands: 0.41 m from the north wall in the first measurement, 0.59 m in the second (18 cm apart, more than either's uncertainty). The two homographies agree on the floor to a few centimetres; the difference is in where each put the foot of the north wall and of the base in DSC_0016. Read again: the whitewash ends at rows 1382 to 1390 and the base's south face meets the tiles at rows 1441 to 1446. Through the 17-point homography that is 0.47 to 0.55 m. The model takes 0.50 m, ±0.09 so that it covers both. Read at the base's own position, the wall line is at v −183 cm, not −190 (the second measurement read it further east, where the homography extrapolates furthest). With that line the second measurement's pier and open side move 7 cm north and agree with the first's.
  - Bottom step: its riser was 0.19, now 0.12. Measured: 0.11 to 0.12 (first) and 0.114 (second), ±0.015. To get it, the whole flight is lowered 0.07 m (each tread top is 0.19 × its number − 0.07), so the risers above it stay 0.19 and the slope is unchanged. Inferred. The risers above it do not fit 0.19 well: risers 2 and 3 measure 0.15 to 0.18 and 0.17 to 0.19 (first) or 0.144 and 0.148 (second), ±0.02. The second measurement also reads a slope of 0.52 to 0.55 near the foot (the underside of the open-side coping on three planes, and the line of the step noses), against the model's 0.73. The upper flight was not measured; the long part of the flight is left as it was until it is checked against DSC_0014 with its own tile fit. Why the bottom riser is short is a guess (invented): the floor may have been raised against the stair, since the pier's lowest course is partly under the tiles (about a third of a course, 2.5 cm).
  - Tread depth: 0.26, unchanged. Measured for treads 1 and 2: 0.30 (first) and 0.26 to 0.28 (second), ±0.03; 0.26 is inside the range that covers both.
  - Tread width: the steps ran from the north wall to z 1.95. Now they run between the two parapets: visible from z 0.50 to 1.69 (1.19 m), and 2 cm into each parapet. Measured clear width: 1.30 (first) and 1.16 to 1.20 (second), ±0.08.
  - Wall-side parapet: new. Until now the treads ran to the north wall and nothing stood on that side. All three photographs show a second whitewashed parapet with the same brick-on-edge coping, with the chairs behind it. Inner face at z 0.50 (measured, ±0.09, as above); back face at z 0.37, 0.13 thick (inferred from the very narrow east face of the base, ±0.04). Its top runs 0.06 m above the step noses, as on the open side (inferred). Coping: the same bricks, the inner face flush with the parapet, so the brick's back hangs 0.12 m over the gap behind it (invented: the back is not in any photograph).
  - Damp stains: moved from the north wall (a strip 0.6 m tall at z 0.003) to the wall-side parapet's inner face (z 0.503), between the treads and the coping. In DSC_0016 that face is flaked render with brown damp and dirt (median #614d3c); the north wall above the chairs is clean whitewash. Where the stains are is measured; their pattern is the existing texture.
  - Wall-side end: new. A base at x 2.31 to 2.48, 0.25 m high, z 0.37 to 0.50. Measured 0.18 (first) and 0.16 (second) wide, 0.26 and 0.24 high, ±0.03; its west face against the bottom step. In the photographs it is grey cement render over brick, whitewashed only in patches (median #52493d, 0.55 of the whitewash). No grey render material exists, so it uses the tread concrete (it renders at about 0.45 of the wall); the whitewash patches are not modelled. On it, one brick laid flat: x 2.34 to 2.48, 0.25 to 0.32 m, z 0.38 to 0.50; measured 0.15 (first) and 0.13 (second) wide, 0.065 to 0.076 high, its top at 0.325 and 0.32, ±0.02. Its east face is flush with the base's (measured), and the lowest brick of the wall-side coping meets it.
  - Behind the wall-side parapet (z 0 to 0.37): open tiled floor at the foot. Measured: tiles show right up to the north wall behind the base, and the base's east face is far too narrow for the base to reach the wall. The gap: 0.25 to 0.30 m (first) or 0.44 to 0.51 m (second); 0.37 m in the model. Further up nobody photographed it; the chairs standing higher up the flight suggest it was filled. In the model it is filled in steps 0.30 m below the treads from the third step up (invented). The chairs are not modelled. The foot of the gap is closed to walking (obstacle x 2.29 to 2.50, z 0 to 0.52).
  - Open-side parapet: was z 1.95 to 2.10 (0.15 thick), cut off vertically over the bottom riser at x 2.31, 0.25 m high. Now z 1.69 to 1.92 (0.23 thick). Outer face: 1.93 to 1.95 (first) and 1.98 in the second measurement's frame, which is 1.91 with the wall line read at the base; ±0.06. Thickness: 0.23 to 0.25 from the coping bricks across it, inferred, ±0.04. Its end now reaches the floor at the pier's west face (x 2.39), with a level top at 0.18 m from the riser to the pier (inferred).
  - Open-side coping: the bricks were centred on z 2.025 and overhung the wall by 5 cm on each side; now centred on z 1.805, their outer face flush with the wall (measured: flush, 1 to 2 cm proud at most). It starts at (x 2.31, y 0.18), was (2.31, 0.25); its top is about 0.31 m above the floor at the riser (measured 0.30 to 0.31, ±0.02). At the foot two bricks stand level: one over the wall's end and one on the pier's cap, slightly tilted. Measured: the last brick sits on the cap and is cracked through. Inferred: that the lowest two are level. The 7.7 cm pitch is unchanged (measured 8.0 ±0.5 cm).
  - Fix of 15 November 2026, the bricks at the foot: as first rebuilt, the lowest sloped brick of each coping started at the foot and ran into the level brick on the open side (by about 6 cm, its cut corner visible) and into the brick on the base on the wall side (by about 4 cm). Now the sloped bricks start further up the slope: one pitch (7.7 cm) on the open side, so the lowest stands beside the level brick with its top corner about 3 mm clear; 5 cm on the wall side, so the lowest meets the brick on the base about 4 mm short of it. The gap under each is the pale mortar bed. The open side's level bed of mortar now stops at the pier's west face (x 2.387) and no longer runs under the pier's cap, whose south face it nearly shared (0.5 mm apart). The coping's top line, the level bricks, the pier and the brick on the base did not move. Inferred: how the sloped bricks meet the level ones is hidden by the bricks themselves in DSC_0016 and DSC_0015; ±3 cm along the slope. Fix decided by: assistant, after a review of the model.
  - Fix of 15 November 2026, the wall-side coping's mortar: its face towards the treads was at z 0.50, in the same plane as the wall-side parapet's face, where the two overlap in a 5 mm band under the coping, so they could flicker as the camera moves. It now stands 3 mm back, at z 0.497. Not proud of the face, as on the open side: on this side the bricks' ends are flush with the parapet, so mortar standing proud would cover the lower 10 cm of every brick's end. Invented, as the mortar's depth was before (a 3 mm detail no photograph resolves). Fix decided by: assistant, after a review of the model.
  - Pier: was 0.26 × 0.36 × 0.27 m, x 2.27 to 2.53, z 1.89 to 2.16. Now x 2.39 to 2.51 (0.12 m: measured 0.13 first and 0.10 second, ±0.03), 0.19 m of brick plus a 0.012 m grey cement cap (measured 0.20 and 0.18 to 0.20 above the tiles, ±0.02), z 1.71 to 1.92 (measured 1.72 to 1.93 first; the second's 1.79 to 2.00 is 1.72 to 1.93 with the wall line read at the base; ±0.05). Bare brick, one brick per course laid flat, not whitewashed; joints at 0.045 and 0.12 m, the lowest course partly under the tiles (measured). The cap uses the tread concrete (photo #70654b). Obstacle x 2.37 to 2.53, z 1.69 to 1.94 (was x 2.27 to 2.53, z 1.89 to 2.16).
  - Railing: the first post is at x 1.95, 0.36 m up from the riser; it was at 2.16. Measured 0.33 (first, about 7 coping bricks) and 0.38 (second), ±0.05. Posts on the coping's centre, z 1.805 (was 2.025); the 0.75 m spacing is unchanged.
  - Walking: the flight's obstacle now ends at z 1.94 (was 2.11), so the floor beside the flight is 17 cm wider. The view of Wrong Data Landscape Dresden is unchanged: the pier and the base end at x 2.51, the painting starts at x 2.79.
  - Tread colour: the material colour was 0x8e8b86, now 0xd6d2cb (same texture). In DSC_0016 the treads are #413c36 and #38322d and the risers #2c2520, against the whitewash #95918a: 0.44, 0.38 and 0.30 of its brightness. The model rendered the treads at 0.24 of the wall beside them and the risers at 0.10; now the treads render at 0.45 and 0.37 in two views. Measured as ratios in one exposure, ±0.05.
  - The reconstructor's description against the photographs. Open-side coping down to a low pier about three courses high at the front of the bottom step: matches; the pier stands 8 to 20 cm east of the riser, is one brick wide and is not whitewashed. Wall side, one brick block on a base: matches. "Whitewashed concrete": partly; the base is grey render over brick, whitewashed in patches. "Against the wall": does not match; the base ends the second, wall-side parapet, and there is open floor between it and the north wall, where the chairs stood. Dark, worn treads: match, though lighter than the model had them. Whitewashed, cracked brick under the coping: matches on the open side; on the wall side the face is mostly flaked render with brown stains.
  - Doubtful still: every plan position comes from one viewpoint (DSC_0016; DSC_0015 is the same spot) and assumes 30 cm tiles. The wall-side parapet's thickness and the gap behind it rest on a face a few pixels wide. The cracks and the whitewash patches on the base are not modelled.
  - Decided by: reconstructor (rebuild the end from these photographs, 15 November 2026); assistant (the measurements).
- Room 1, the folding chairs stacked on the stairs (Decision 1c, 17 November 2026): omitted. DSC_0014, DSC_0015 and DSC_0016 show black and red folding wire chairs stacked against the north wall behind the wall-side parapet, higher up the flight; the S34 photographs `room_1_stairs_important.webp` (S34) and `stairs_room1.jpg` (S34) show stacked chairs in the same place years earlier, so they were kept there by the gallery. The digital exhibition never modelled them (the only folding chairs in the scene are the two at the café table in Room 2, which stay), so nothing was removed and nothing is added. Only the code comment on the fill behind the wall-side parapet (`src/world/props.js`) now says the chairs are left out on purpose. The chairs were in the gallery during the exhibition and are left out on the artist's request. Status: omitted. Not a reconstruction value, so measured, inferred and invented do not apply. Uncertainty: none. Decided by: artist (asked for it, reported by the reconstructor, 17 November 2026).
- Room 1, the stairs in two tones (Decision 1a, option C, 17 November 2026). Code: `stairRiser` in `src/world/materials.js` and the treads in the Room 1 stairs block of `src/world/props.js`. The front face of every step (the riser, facing east towards the foot) now has its own material, `stairRiser`: the same worn concrete texture at colour 0xb0aca5. The tread tops and every other face keep `stair` at 0xd6d2cb, unchanged. The fill behind the wall-side parapet, the base and the pier's cap keep `stair`.
  - Evidence. DSC_0014, DSC_0015 and DSC_0016 (median colours of 44-pixel squares, `reports/decisions.md`, Decision 1): lit lower tread tops 0.33 to 0.47 of the whitewash in the same photograph, upper treads 0.20 to 0.33, risers 0.15 to 0.25. One material could not give both. Nosings are rounded and worn with no lighter strip, so none is modelled. The S34 photographs (`room_1_stairs_important.webp`, `stairs_room1.jpg`) show the treads in other light and were not used for colour.
  - Measured: the target ratios. Inferred: that the stone is the same colour all the way up and the difference is light and wear, so the riser keeps the tread texture. The colour value is set so that the risers render inside 0.15 to 0.25 of the whitewash; the render check is in the entry on the slope below. Uncertainty: ±0.05 on each ratio (one exposure per photograph).
  - Decided by: reconstructor (option C); assistant (the colour value).
- Room 1, the slope of the stairs, the number of steps and the street's height (Decision 1b, 17 November 2026). Code: the Room 1 stairs block in `src/world/props.js` (`run`, `rise`, `steps`, `sunk`, the landing, the level copings and handrail at the top), the street door in `src/world/architecture.js` (in place of the stair shaft), `STAIRWELL` and Room 1's ceiling in `src/world/layout.js`, the comment on `lights.stair` in `src/world/lights.js`, the material `streetDoor` in `src/world/materials.js`. Frame: x east, y up, z south from the north wall's face.
  - What changed. Run 0.26 → 0.29 m; rise 0.19 → 0.16 m; 17 steps → 8 risers. The bottom riser stays 0.12 m (the flight now sits 0.04 m lower than rise × steps, was 0.07). Slope 36.2° (0.73) → 28.9° (0.55). The eighth step is a landing at 1.24 m: its nosing at x 0.28, and it runs west through Room 1's west wall (x −0.15 to −0.55, in the existing opening z 0 to 1.95) to the street door in the wall's outer face. The old flight had nine steps in the room, 1.64 m at the west wall, and climbed on to 3.16 m at x −2.11 inside an invented shaft 2 m deep and 5.6 m tall west of the wall. Both copings and the handrail rise with the steps to the landing's nosing and then run level to the west wall (the copings as bricks on edge along the landing). Room 1's ceiling is now closed over the flight: it had a hole x −0.15 to 1.25, z 0 to 1.95, for the shaft. The opening in the wall's thickness rises to 3.45 m above a closed street door (1.24 to 3.45 m), dark blue-black (0x1b2030). The foot rebuilt on 15 November is unchanged: bottom riser at x 2.31, 0.12 m high, the pier, the base, the brick on it, the parapets' faces, the first railing post. The damp stains on the wall-side parapet are computed from run and rise and follow the new flight as they are. Walking is unchanged: the flight's obstacle (x −0.55 to 2.33, z 0 to 1.94) is the same, so the stairs were not walkable before and are not now.
  - Evidence, per photograph (S34 = the MAMŰ building photographs of 2014 to 2021). Lengths come from 30 cm floor tiles, 7.7 cm brick courses, the camera height of 1.24 m and the focal length from EXIF (18 mm, 4596 px) found for DSC_0016 on 15 November, or from people's eye levels.
    - DSC_0015 (the flight from the side). Slope 28.5° ±2° (rise to run 0.54, 0.50 to 0.59): measured on 16 November (`reports/decisions.md`, Decision 1) from the vanishing point of both copings. New today, the top: the horizon fixed by the north wall's floor and ceiling lines at x 3800 px (rows 4255 and 870 for 0 and 3.0 m: horizon at row 2856, the camera pitched up about 1.8°); the open-side coping at the left edge of the frame (rows 2665 to 2700) lies about 3.8 m in front of the camera near the west wall, 1.37 to 1.40 m high, so the nosings there are at 1.19 to 1.25 m. The flight is at or near its top at the west wall. The dark door above it has its head at about 3.2 to 3.5 m and its foot hidden by the coping. Measured with an inferred camera position, ±0.12 m. Six whole steps from the foot and part of a seventh are visible; the rest hide behind the open-side parapet.
    - DSC_0016 (same standpoint as DSC_0015; one homography maps one onto the other): risers 2 and 3 measured 0.144 to 0.19 m, treads 0.26 to 0.30 m, slope 0.52 to 0.55 near the foot (15 November). Not an independent slope. An automatic vanishing-point fit tried today on the brick edges was unstable (few inliers) and is not used.
    - DSC_0014 (the middle of the flight, from behind the plant, the dark door at the top of the frame): slope from the vanishing point of the two copings' lower edges, each read by hand at three points on full-resolution crops (open side: rows 3240, 3890 and 4500 at columns 2000, 2900 and 3750; wall side: 2915, 3205 and 3545 at 2750, 3200 and 3700), the camera taken as level or pitched up 1.8° as in DSC_0015: 33.8° to 34.4°. Measured, rough, ±4°: the two lines are almost parallel in the picture, and the reading leans steep against DSC_0015's. It shows Room 1's ceiling closed over the flight, with the lighting track, up to a beam over the opening in the west wall, and the dark door in the opening.
    - `room_1_stairs_important.webp` (S34, 768 × 409, the main source): the whole flight from the south with five people standing in front of it, the stacked chairs behind the wall-side parapet, the grey rendered base at the wall-side foot, and at the top a landing in front of a dark blue door, the open-side coping ending at the landing. Height of the landing: the people's eyes put the horizon at rows 57 to 107; the door's threshold is at rows 106 to 110, about 25 px below it; at the man leaning on the parapet 1 m is 197 px, and the door is 1 to 5 times further away, so the landing is 1.05 to 1.45 m above the floor (camera at eye height, 1.55 to 1.65 m, inferred). Steps: about six treads between the parapets below the man's shoulder and two risers under the landing; the middle is hidden by the man, so the count is 8 to 10 risers, not exact. The copings look steeper in this picture (33° to 40°) because the view is oblique (the handrail and the coping are 1.7 times further apart in the picture at the foot than at the top), so it gives no slope.
    - `room_1_from_top_of_stairs.webp` (S34, 1920 × 1282, Room 1 from the top of the flight, looking down over the open-side coping): vanishing points of the floor tiles (14 segments in one direction, residuals under 1°), the focal length from the two tile directions (1050 to 1300 px), and the spacing of the tile joints (0.101 to 0.110 of the camera's height per 30 cm tile; four tiles check at 0.424): the camera was 2.73 to 2.97 m above Room 1's floor. With the photographer standing on the landing and the camera at eye height (1.5 to 1.7 m, inferred), the landing is 1.0 to 1.45 m high. The ceiling and its lamps are very close above the camera, as they would be.
    - `main_entrance.webp` (S34, the street door from the street): through the open leaf the landing's tiles run in at the threshold's level, a beam crosses at about the camera's height and the far wall of Room 1 is seen from above. Read with the door's proportions (the opening 1.43 times as tall as wide) and the camera 1.6 m above the threshold: the camera stood about 1 m from the door, the beam is 1.6 to 1.9 m above the threshold (2.9 to 3.1 m above Room 1's floor with the landing at 1.24, so at Room 1's ceiling), and the visitor inside stands about 0.3 m below the threshold about 0.7 m inside the door, on the first step down. Inferred, ±0.3 m. It agrees with a street door on the landing, not with a deep shaft.
    - `stairs_room1.jpg` (S34): the open-side coping running straight up to the west wall under the dark door, people standing at the top. Not measured (low resolution, oblique).
    - `room_1_left_side_from_above.jpg` (S34): looked at; another view from high on the flight. Nothing measured.
    - Window sills: the windows in Room 1's street wall have their sills 1.7 m above the floor inside (`NICHE.sill`), so a street at about 1.24 m puts them just above the pavement, as basement windows are. A consistency check, not a measurement.
  - Combined. Slope: 28.5° ±2° (DSC_0015) and 34° ±4° (DSC_0014), weighted by their uncertainties: about 29.5° ±2°. Landing (the street door's threshold): 1.19 to 1.25 m (DSC_0015), 1.05 to 1.45 (room_1_stairs_important), 1.0 to 1.45 (room_1_from_top_of_stairs), main_entrance agrees: 1.24 m ±0.12. Steps: (1.24 − 0.12) / 0.16 = 7 risers above the bottom one, 8 in all; the photographs' 8 to 10 and the measured risers (0.144 to 0.19) allow 8 or 9 (9 would mean risers of 0.14 and treads of 0.26). The run of 0.29 is inside the measured 0.26 to 0.30 and gives the slope 28.9°. The street's height above the basement floor: 1.24 m ±0.12, inferred; the pavement outside the door is not seen and may be a step off the threshold.
  - Measured: the slopes (DSC_0015, DSC_0014), the camera heights and horizons above. Inferred: the landing's height, the number of steps (8, ±1), the run of 0.29 within its range, the street door on the landing in the wall's outer face, the closed ceiling (seen; the beam's depth and height are not measured and are not modelled). Invented: the door's head at 3.45 m (a door about 2.2 m tall, from DSC_0015's 3.2 to 3.5 m and main_entrance's proportions, ±0.3), the reveal above Room 1's ceiling, the door as one flat dark panel with no frame or leaves, the level copings along the landing, the level handrail at the top.
  - Not changed, an open question: `lights.stair` (a cold point light, intensity 4, at x −1.2, y 4.6, z 1.05) stood in the invented shaft as light from the street. It is unchanged and now stands outside the closed door; there are no shadows, so Room 1 is lit as before. Options: keep it as an invented fill light; move it into the opening; take it out and let the room's own spots light the stairs.
  - Contradictions with the foot rebuilt on 15 November: none found. `room_1_stairs_important.webp` shows the grey base at the wall-side foot, the open side's coping coming down to the pier with level bricks, and open floor behind the base, as rebuilt.
  - Decided by: reconstructor (measure on all the photographs, build to the figures, keep the foot); assistant (the measurements, 8 risers, the run, the door and the closed ceiling).
- The record of how parts were built (15 November 2026). Some parts that this log, the code comments or the commit messages said were built from the photographs or the documentation video were built from the reconstructor's written descriptions: the Room 4 end wall and the pipe over the projection, and the stairs in Room 1. Both places are corrected in this log with a dated note and nothing is deleted: the Let it Flow line under Status, and the line on the stair shaft under Known issues. The code comments that made the same claims were at first left as they were; after a review the same day they say it too, keeping the old source in quotes: the Room 1 stairs comment and the comment over the Room 4 pipes in `src/world/props.js`, and the stairs' material in `src/world/materials.js`, whose trailing "dark, worn" stood beside the paler colour of the tread fix. Checked with `git log -p --follow PROGRESS.md` and the history and commit messages of `props.js`, `layout.js`, `architecture.js` and `letItFlow.js`. Where the record does not say how a value was found, the notes say that and do not guess. Found on the way, and not in this log before:
  - Room 4, north wall: commit 4d42336 (3 November 2026) made it bare brick and gave the big pipe along its top white paint, the whole room's length and four collars. The code comments give "the lit photo of the empty room" as the source of the bare brick (`layout.js`) and of the white paint and the length ("painted white like the walls and runs the whole room", `props.js`); they give no source for the four collars, and the commit message names no photograph. Which photograph it was, and of which year, is not recorded. Probably MAMŰ's photograph of 2013 (`mamu.jpg`, S32), and so not one of January 2025: inferred, because that photograph is lit, no exhibition is up, and it shows the white pipe along the top of this wall and the radiator on a concrete ledge that the same commit added. People are filming in it, though, so "empty" does not fit it well, and the same commit cites other MAMŰ photographs for Room 3's floor ("Kivonat, 2015"). (Corrected 15 November 2026 after a review: this line first said as fact that it was a MAMŰ photograph of another occasion, and that both the collars and the length came from it.) The same commit added a concrete ledge along the wall's western half with a radiator on it and cites no source for them. The ledge (x 15.9 to 20.2) and the radiator (x 17.1) are still in the model; whether they were there in January 2025 has not been checked.

That is the reconstructor's annotation, not a measurement; this log said only that the work was in Room 4.

The walk has nos 6, 7 and 17 at 0.94 × 0.53 m and nos 8 and 15 at 1.21 × 0.68 m (`hang` in `src/data/exhibition.json`), so it agrees. - Per-work `rights` and `reconstruction` in `src/data/exhibition.json` (17 November 2026, no change to the scene). Every entry under `works` (the 17 works and the Paradise Factory monitor, `w03-screen`) has `rights` = the rightsstatements.org URI In Copyright (`http://rightsstatements.org/vocab/InC/1.0/`) and the credit "© Kristóf Szabó / KristofLab", the same for every work, from the Google Sheet "works" (tab `works_4`, columns rights_statement and rights_holder, last changed 16 November 2026 11:43 Italian time). It covers the work only: the installation photographs and the documentation videos are "© Kristóf Szabó" (NOTICE) and are not mixed in. `reconstruction` comes from the Google Sheet "paradata", tab `paradata_3` (last changed 16 November 2026 14:35 Italian time), copied to `src/data/paradata.json`: every row whose related_titles names the work becomes an aspect (row id, element, status as the Sheet gives it). Three rows name "all placed works" and are read from their element: ap-d25 (paintings' hanging height) for the paintings, ap-d26 (prints and screens) for the prints and the screens, the monitor included, ap-d22 (the light pools) for the hung works, which are the ones with a pool. No work has one status for all its aspects, so `status` is empty for all 18 entries and `note` lists the statuses per aspect; nothing is filled in that the Sheet does not give. The site does not show either field (no one asked for it), and the accessible version does not list credits per work, so it does not list rights either. Decided by: reconstructor (the fields, CLAUDE.md roadmap item 1); assistant (reading the three "all placed works" rows by their element). Not a reconstruction value, so measured, inferred and invented do not apply to the change itself. Uncertainty: the per-aspect reading of ap-d22, ap-d25 and ap-d26 is the assistant's; the Sheet may want to list the works by id.
- Room 4, its scale and height (Decision 2, 17 November 2026): the width (4.00 m) and length (9.5 m) stay as on the gallery's measured plan; the room is now 2.92 m high to where its vaults spring, where it was 2.75 m. Code: `R4_VAULT` in `src/world/layout.js` (spring 2.92, rise 0.14 unchanged, so the crown is at 3.06; Room 4's `ceiling` 3.06 for the shading), used by the vaults and beams in `src/world/architecture.js`, the recesses' tops (`R4_RECESSES.top`) and every Room 4 wall block, which now run up to 3.1 m behind the vaults (the north wall, the south wall, the end wall, the two stubs and the header between Rooms 3 and 4; they were 2.9 m). Props that hang from the ceiling or run into it moved with it: the dark conduit with the four floodlights from 2.665 to 2.72 m to 2.835 to 2.89 m (under the beams' bottom flanges), its lamps from 2.6 to 2.77 m; the round pipe on recess A's sill now runs to 2.95 m (it ran to 2.78), into the wall above the recess.

New on 17 November: on the same photograph the steel beams' ends on the south wall stand as high as the end wall's top. Heights on a wall read against its own foot line and the horizon (vanishing point of the side walls' feet at x 1963, y 3237 px, fitted to 88 and 49 points, sd 1.4 and 1.7 px): end wall top at the south corner 2.333 camera heights, the nearest beam's bottom where it meets the south wall (x 3667, y 1917 px) 2.354 camera heights. So the vaults spring at the end wall's height along the room, not only at the end wall (inferred for the bays further west, which this photograph shows less clearly). Measured, ±0.03 m at the plan's width. Pipe axis on the end wall 0.88 to 0.90 of the end wall's height (DSC_0113, DSC_0116): 2.57 to 2.63 m at 2.92 m; it stays at 2.58 m.
  - Width: the floor tiles test. Two counts of tiles across the room at the end wall, each from the tile joints' spacing on one row of the floor carried to the end wall's floor line through the vanishing point of the joints (no camera height or focal length needed): DSC_0113 (2025; joints legible only in the lower left corner, under the specks; the side walls' foot lines and the end wall's floor corners at x 847 and 3398 px fitted by edges): 13.0 tiles, range 12.8 to 13.4 from the vanishing point's uncertainty and the lens's distortion in that corner. `mamu.jpg` (S32, 2013, lit, joints clear; five joints tracked over 20 rows, their vanishing point (379, 245) fits a square tile and a phone's focal length of about 966 px): the end wall 458 px wide at its foot, a tile 31.5 px there, 14.5 tiles ±0.5. The two disagree by 1.5 tiles, and the tile's size is not measured anywhere in Room 4 (30 cm is assumed in this log and in the river's scale). At 30 cm tiles they give 3.9 m (3.84 to 4.02) and 4.35 m (4.2 to 4.5); at the plan's 4.00 m, tiles of 0.31 and 0.276 m. Neither supports the brick-course scale's 3.80 to 3.87 m better than the plan's 4.00 m, so the measured plan stays. Status: width measured (the plan); the tile test inconclusive. S34 `room_4_left_wall.jpg` and `corridor_room3-4.jpg` show no floor across the whole width; `tubes_room4.jpg` shows no floor.

DSC_0113's count does not support this. Not changed; a question for the artist (the tile size, or the river's length taped). (Later the same day: the artist confirmed 4.0 to 4.35 m and the river is now 4.05 m; see "Let it Flow, the plastic river's length".)

- Let it Flow, the plastic river's length (17 November 2026): 4.05 m, where it was 4.74 m; range 4.0 to 4.2 m. The artist confirms the river at the exhibition was about 4.0 to 4.35 m long (reported by the reconstructor, 17 November 2026); he gave no exact figure, so the 4.05 m is inferred from the photographs and the video, within his range. The 4.74 m of 16 November (entry "Let it Flow, sizes") is superseded; that entry keeps its text with a dated note. Evidence: DSC_0113, 4.04 m from the wall to the tip of the white heap (3.9 to 4.2; `reports/decisions.md`); the documentation video, 4.35 m (4.2 to 4.4) taking Natural Life's screen as 0.94 m wide; the video at 0.00 to 2.44 s, the screen's centre 0.3 to 0.5 m further from the end wall than the river's tip: with the screen at x 21.32, as DSC_0108 to DSC_0112 place it (entry "Room 4, the left (north) wall"), that puts the tip at 21.62 to 21.82 and the river at 3.6 to 3.8 m, below the artist's range; with the second measurer's 20.95 ±0.2 it gives 3.95 to 4.15 m. The floor tiles of `mamu.jpg` (S32), if the room is 4.00 m wide, make the tiles 27.5 cm and the river, scaled from 30 cm tiles in PLANE javított.png, 4.35 m (DSC_0113's tile count does not support that; see Decision 2). The low end of the artist's range fits the photograph and the screen best, so 4.05 m. Uncertainty ±0.15 m. Code: `RIVER_M` in `scripts/make_river.py` (the river's length is now its own value; the composite's frame follows the river, no longer the other way round) and, without re-running it (NumPy, SciPy and Pillow are not installed on this machine), `node scripts/set_river_length.mjs 4.05`, which rescales `src/data/river.json` as `make_river.py` would: about the wall tip's x (25.38) and the composite's centre line (z 3.73). So the tip stays at the wall (x 25.40), the far end moves from x 20.66 to 21.35, and the band across from z 2.82 to 4.42 to z 2.95 to 4.32; the obstacles and the composite's frame (now `composite` in river.json, 5.98 × 3.36 m; it was `projection`, 7.0 × 3.94 m) scale with it. The textures and the relief's thickness (`hmax` 0.13 m) are unchanged. Natural Life's screen stays at x 21.32 (DSC_0108 to DSC_0112); against the video it is now 0.03 m further from the end wall than the tip, where the video shows 0.3 to 0.5 m. That residual is noted, not fitted: moving the screen would contradict the photographs. Decided by: artist (the range); reconstructor (apply it, 17 November 2026); assistant (4.05 m within the range).
- Let it Flow, the floor projection (Decision 4, 17 November 2026): the video is now thrown by the projector on the north wall, keystoned as that projector throws it. (Note of 18 November 2026: the fit below, a bare 16:9 projector, is superseded by "Let it Flow, the floor projection, refitted", after the artist said he fitted the picture with Resolume's keystone. The rest of this entry stands: the projector's place, the projective texture mapping, no mirrored strips or fade.) It replaces the flat 7.0 × 3.94 m rectangle with its mirrored side strips and its 1.4 m fade at the west end. Code: `scripts/fit_projector.mjs` (new; Node only, deterministic: fixed starting points and Nelder-Mead, no random numbers) fits the projector and writes its pose and the lit quad into `src/data/river.json` (`projector`). `src/world/letItFlow.js` maps the video onto the floor and onto the plastic by projective texture mapping from that pose (each pixel's texture coordinate from the projector's view and projection), so the light the plastic takes from the video uses the same mapping; the plastic's folds are now shaded against the projector's lens (they were shaded against a lamp invented 2.8 m above the river's middle). Outside the picture the floor gets nothing: the mirrored strips and the fade are gone (the photographs and the video show full-strength specks to x 19.2 to 20.1 and no reflected band). The picture's edge is softened over 0.5% of the frame.
  - Projector, measured: on DSC_0108 to DSC_0112 (and 04-03) the "small black box with a light and a round camera" of 15 November is a projector body standing on end on a wall arm, with vents and a lit indicator on its side and light at its top; the round "camera" is the arm's wall plate. It hangs just under the big pipe, above Natural Life and at recess A's west arris. Modelled as photographed in `src/world/props.js` (body 0.12 × 0.42 × 0.17 m at x 21.82, 1.97 to 2.39 m high; plate at x 21.66, 2.15 m), place ±0.15 m along the wall, size ±3 cm. Its lens is taken at the light at its top: x 21.82, 2.36 m high, z 1.87 (measured, ±0.15 / ±0.08 / ±0.05). Which way the lens faces cannot be seen; the body is not turned to the fitted aim. The ceiling-hung projector at the conduit's west end (x 17.48) stays, now named as the wall projection's (inferred).
  - Aim and lit area, inferred by the fit: a 16:9 picture (the file is 1920 × 1080, not stretched) from that lens, pointing almost straight down (forward 0.074, -0.988, -0.132), the picture shifted 1.5 half-heights to the south of the lens axis, as a short-throw projector's lens shift does, with the widest lens allowed (throw ratio 0.40, about the widest short-throw projector without a mirror). Floor corners: (25.86, 2.02) and (25.60, 5.05) at the east end, (19.87, 2.60) and (20.45, 5.02) at the west end. The east end lies 0.2 to 0.46 m beyond the end wall, so specks reach the wall's foot. Against the evidence: south limit z 5.03 at x 23 (wanted 5.0 to 5.05) and 5.02 at x 20.5 (wanted 4.8 to 4.95); north limit z 2.32 at x 22.75 (wanted 2.5 to 2.6) and 2.54 at x 20.5 (wanted 2.7 to 2.85); width 2.74 m at x 23 and 2.48 m at x 20.5 (wanted 2.3 to 2.4, ±0.2); the video's river on the plastic's centre line within 0.11 m on average, 0.16 m at most; the west end at x 20.16 on the room's middle line, 5.2 m from the end wall, where the video shows specks at least 6.2 m out (±0.5). So the fit meets the river, the south limit and the east end, and misses the north limit by about 0.25 m and the length by about 1 m. No aim meets all of it: a 16:9 picture is never narrower than about 0.56 of its length on the floor, and an oblique throw from a side wall stretches it across the room, not along it. The length and the width together need a wider lens than projectors have (with the throw ratio left free the fit reaches x 19.36 but needs 0.27, and the north edge stays 0.3 m out), or a picture squeezed across or cut. With the 4.74 m river the same fit was worse (width 3.0 m at x 23, river within 0.22 m on average and 0.49 m at most, west end at 19.9). How the projector hung and whether its picture was stretched is a question for the artist; `node scripts/fit_projector.mjs --max-tan=<v>` refits with another lens limit.
  - Decided by: reconstructor (the projector's place, the keystone, no mirrored strips or fade, 17 November 2026); assistant (the fit and its lens limit). Status: projector position measured; aim, lens and lit area inferred; the west end inferred from the projection geometry, not seen in any source. Uncertainty: lit edges ±0.3 m, the west end ±1 m.
- `make_river.py`'s frame and river scales separated (17 November 2026, Decision 4.4): see the river's length above. The script no longer writes the floor picture's frame; `fit_projector.mjs` does. No geometry changed by this step alone.
- Let it Flow, the floor projection, refitted (18 November 2026): the picture's place on the floor is now fitted as one projective map from the frame to the floor (the projector and the artist's keystone together), not as a bare 16:9 projector. New evidence: "he used the keystone effect in Resolume to make it fit and then rendered it" (the artist, reported by the reconstructor, 18 November 2026). Checked against the projected file `Let it flow_ok.mp4` (22 frames, one every 10 s, read at 960 × 540 with FFmpeg 6.1.1 essentials, gyan.dev): the content fills the whole 16:9 frame; no black corners, no straight skewed edge, and the dark purple haze falls off towards all four edges like a vignette (mean level 2 to 5 of 255 on the top row, 6 to 13 on the bottom row, 18 to 21 in the middle), so the keystone was not baked into the frame's edges. The specks are a little larger and sparser in the top third (median 8 to 10 px, 1,900 to 4,100 per third) than in the bottom third (7 px, 6,000 to 8,700), which may be a warp of the speck layer inside the file; it cannot be told from the file alone. Either way the file reached the floor through one projective map (the projector's throw and Resolume's keystone, if it was applied on output), and a keystone can squeeze the picture across and stretch it along, which a bare 16:9 projector cannot (entry of 17 November). Code: `scripts/fit_projector.mjs` now fits the floor positions of the frame's four corners (8 numbers; a homography) to the same evidence as before, with the west end taken at the 6.2 m bound (x 19.2), and writes `projector.floorToFrame` (the 3 × 3 map from the floor to the frame), `quad` and `position` to `src/data/river.json`; `src/world/letItFlow.js` takes the picture through that map, for points above the floor (the plastic's relief) along the ray from the lens at the measured position. Fitted (inferred): floor corners (25.45, 2.55) and (25.92, 4.82) at the east end, (19.56, 2.60) and (19.38, 5.11) at the west end; the east end lies 0.05 to 0.5 m beyond the end wall, so specks reach the wall's foot. Against the evidence: width 2.38 m at x 23 and 2.47 m at x 20.5 (wanted 2.3 to 2.4, ±0.2); north limit z 2.57 at x 22.75 (wanted 2.5 to 2.6) and 2.59 at x 20.5 (wanted 2.7 to 2.85); south limit z 4.95 at x 23 (wanted 5.0 to 5.05) and 5.06 at x 20.5 (wanted 4.8 to 4.95); length 5.96 m on the north side, 6.24 m in the middle, 6.52 m on the south side, west end x 19.47 in the middle; the video's river on the plastic's centre line within 0.07 m on average and 0.20 m at most (the 16:9 projector of 17 November: 0.11 and 0.16 m, width 2.74 m, length 5.2 m). So the refit meets the width, the length, the east end and the river together; it misses the north limit nearer the door by 0.2 m and the south limits by 0.1 to 0.2 m. The 16:9 projector fit of 17 November is superseded. Decided by: artist (the keystone, reported by the reconstructor, 18 November 2026); assistant (reading the file and the fit). Status: projector position measured; the picture's place on the floor inferred; the west end inferred (the 6.2 m bound), not seen in any source. Uncertainty: lit edges ±0.2 m, the west end ±1 m. Open: how the projector itself was aimed (the artist has not answered); it does not change the picture's place on the floor, only the direction from which the plastic is lit.

Evidence: DSC_0048 at full size, the west side of the toilet corridor's mouth in Room 2's south wall (the wall right of the bar and left of the corridor, which she named): one brick's length of whitewashed reveal, 0.25 to 0.30 m, and behind it the dark lined door frame, about as deep again; OpenStreetMap, a 5.2 m wide wing for the 4.0 m deep rooms, about 0.6 m a wall; the reference's 0.6 to 0.9 m. Inferred, ±0.1 m. The walls grow outward, so no room's inner size changes (assumption). Code: `T`, `N_OUT`, `S_OUT` in `src/world/layout.js`; with it the street wall, Room 1's north and south walls, the south wall of Rooms 2 to 4, the north wall of Rooms 3 and 4, the end wall, the storage's shell and the toilet corridor behind the south wall. Decided by: reconstructor (thicker, from the photographs); assistant (the value).

- Room 1, the pier at the foot of the stairs (19 November 2026; to be checked by eye against DSC_0016): re-measured on DSC_0016 at full size. Seen from the south, the pier's face towards the room is one brick long (0.22 to 0.25 m) and shows two courses of ridged red bricks set on edge, about 0.13 m each with the joint, a third partly under the tiles; it is as thick as the parapet it ends (0.23 m) and carries a lump of grey cement, on which the lowest coping brick sits. Built so: 0.25 m (x 2.46 to 2.71) × 0.23 m × 0.27 m high, two bricks with a mortar joint, a 3 cm cement cap, one coping brick on the cap (the second level brick beside it is gone: it stood where the pier now is). Material: the copings' own (their canvas, mapped once per face as on the copings' long faces), in the deeper of their tints, since the photograph shows the same ridged brick, darker and dirtier than the copings; no texture was edited. The pier is a little taller than the first step, as in the photograph. Measured against the brick's 25 cm and 12 cm: inferred, ±0.03 m. Decided by: reconstructor (correct it from DSC_0016); assistant (the reading).

  - Evidence: DSC_0099 to DSC_0112 (the north wall and all its pipes painted one dark blue-grey: median #212027 on the wall, #1e1d23 on a low pipe, #141318 on the big pipe in shade, DSC_0110; until now the wall was bare brown brick, from the photograph of 2013, and its pipes a light grey 0x55575c and 0x3f3c37); DSC_0113 and DSC_0116 (the pipe over the projection as bright as the end wall around it, 1.04 and 1.07; it was 0x8e8a83, about 2.5 times the wall); DSC_0110 and DSC_0113 for the whole room (below). The photographs' brightness was read as linear luminance (sRGB decoded) in fixed areas: the plain end wall between the pipe and the picture ("wall"), the pipe, the picture, the dark floor between the specks, the north and south walls, the ceiling. Ratios do not depend on the exposure; the camera's tone curve still compresses its highlights, so the bright picture reads too dim against the wall in the photographs (picture/wall 1.5) and that ratio was not matched.
  - Screenshots of the digital exhibition from the photographs' standpoints (camera 6.95 m from the end wall, 1.25 m high, 0.25 m north of the room's middle line; looking along the room for DSC_0113, turned 24.6° to the north and tilted up 10.8° for DSC_0110; 600 × 900 px; the interface hidden; the two Let it Flow videos played from short VP9 cuts because the test browser has no H.264). Ratios, photograph / before (commit fbf10e4) / after: DSC_0113: pipe/wall 1.04 / 4.38 / 1.14; floor/wall 0.30 / 0.27 / 0.27; north wall/end wall 0.17 / 0.23 / 0.34; south wall/end wall 0.61 / 0.23 / 0.62; ceiling/end wall 0.70 / 0.00 / 0.56; big pipe/north wall 0.79 / 2.05 / 1.02; picture/wall 1.50 / 9.85 / 4.91. The two standpoints disagree on the north wall (0.17 seen along it near the end wall, 0.73 near the camera); the model sits between them.

- The old spreadsheet of interface texts, source S14 (16 November 2026, nothing in the texts changed): the findings are recorded in `docs/texts-s14.md`. S14 is the Drive file `Artificial Paradise - texts EN-HU.xlsx`, and there is no newer version (confirmed by the reconstructor, 16 November 2026; row S14 of the `sources` sheet names no file, and the name S14 was not in this repository before). Findings: three strings still stand exactly as they were edited in the spreadsheet (the Hungarian tab title `ui.docTitle`, "Lemegyünk a pincébe..." in `ui.loader.preparing`, and the photography credit, which the artist confirmed again on 12 November 2026); six more carry part of its wording inside later text (`ui.soundAsk.hint` and `.hintTouch` in Hungarian, `ui.panels.rights`, `ui.docDescription` in both languages, `behindTheScenes`, `source`); about 170 short strings (85 English, 80 Hungarian) are identical to their cell, but they were written for the site on 22 and 23 October 2026 and only copied into the sheet; nothing else on the site comes from it. Evidence: the history of every string in `src/i18n/en.json` and `hu.json` at ba3b7f1 (the 75 commits that touched the two files); the spreadsheet's rows, read through the Drive connector and compared with the site of 23 October 2026 (885afcd) and of ba3b7f1; commits ba982c2 and a3df1b1 of 30 October 2026; the two lines of this log that name the spreadsheet ("Next", item 0, and "Known issues"). Not recorded: who typed each edit in the sheet (the `sources` row names the reconstructor and the artist, this log says "the owner"); who cleaned the English exhibition text, when and from which copy; what the spreadsheet's last save changed (30 October 2026, 15:09 UTC, about four hours after the two commits that used it). This log's own list of the edits taken from the sheet (Behind the scenes, the works-list note, the MAMŰ link label) is eight short of the history: the Hungarian tab title, "Lemegyünk a pincébe...", "fejhallgatóval izgalmasabb", the page description, "were created by" in the rights line, the photography credit, the hyphened dates (since replaced) and "Két fal" in the old Room 4 description (since removed). "Next", item 0, says the site shows "the spreadsheet's interface texts"; by the history most interface strings were written for the site, and about 150 of today's roughly 350 in each language were first written on or after 13 November 2026. That line is left as it stands and is to be read with this entry. Nothing in the texts changed: no string in `src/i18n`, `index.html`, `src/data/exhibition.json` or anywhere else. Decided by: reconstructor (record the findings and change nothing). Not a reconstruction value, so measured, inferred and invented do not apply; in the document, "recorded" marks what the repository says and "inferred" what was read from its history. Uncertainty: the check worked from the connector's text of the spreadsheet, which leaves out formatting, cell comments and hidden cells.
- The curtain by the opening between Room 3 and Room 4, rebuilt (16 and 17 November 2026). The reconstructor: "it hangs at the side of the opening and covers a space between the walls (DSC_0064); it does not divide the rooms. Rebuild it." On 17 November 2026 she repeated it with two more sources, the S34 photographs `curtain_room3.jpg` and `corridor_room3-4.jpg` ("it hangs at the side of the opening, floor to ceiling, and covers a gap between the walls; it does not divide the rooms").
  - What was there: a strip of cloth 0.55 m wide and 2.42 m high, bunched, standing on the Room 4 face of the partition at the south side of the opening (x 15.986, z 4.09 to 4.64), with a footprint (z 4.077 to 4.666) that took 0.59 m off the opening's walkable width. The Status line called Rooms 3 and 4 "one long room split only by two wall stubs and a curtain". No photograph shows a curtain in the opening or on the Room 4 face. How the old one was arrived at is not recorded (the code comment named no photograph).
  - What the photographs show: south of the opening stand two short walls, the one that carries no. 14 on its west face and, about 0.8 m east of it, the south stub of the partition. The space between them is open to the north, towards the way through, and the curtain closes that north side from wall to wall. It hangs in the line of the two wall ends, parallel to the long axis of the rooms, and it is drawn shut in every photograph that shows it: DSC_0064, 0069, 0070, 0071, 0096 and 0097 from the Room 3 side, DSC_0126 to 0130 from the Room 4 side. The opening itself is clear from stub to stub (DSC_0069, 0097, 0130). The floor-plan guide (S33, page 1) draws the same thing: two short walls south of the opening with a grey field between them; its arrows place DSC_0064 to 0067 by the bar looking east, 0070 to 0072 in Room 3 looking south-east, 0125 to 0127 in Room 4 looking south-west and 0128 to 0130 in Room 4 looking west.
  - What is built (`src/world/props.js`, `materials.curtain` and `materials.curtainShade` in `src/world/materials.js`): one drawn curtain on the north side of the closet block, from x 14.58 to x 15.60 (1.02 m), 2.6 cm in front of the block's north face (z 4.64), from the ceiling (2.75 m) down to a roll 3 cm thick whose underside is 4 cm above the floor. Shallow uneven folds about every 14 cm, 1 to 2 cm deep, flattening over the last 20 cm above the roll. Cream cloth (0xe2dac4, was 0xcdc7bb). The old bunched strip and its footprint are removed, so the opening is walkable over its full modelled width again (z 2.918 to 4.666, 1.75 m). The curtain has no footprint of its own: the block behind it already stops the visitor. Nothing else moved: the walls, no. 14, the lights, the sound and the tour path (`DOORS` in `src/world/route.js`) are as they were, and the sound's "other room" level in `src/audio/ambience.js` goes by the room rectangles, not by the curtain. The corner map draws walls only, so it does not change.
  - Values and their status:
    - Side and place (south of the opening, between the wall of no. 14 and the south stub, closing the north side of the space): measured, seen from both rooms. Uncertainty: none about the side; ±5 cm about how far the cloth stands in front of or behind the line of the wall ends.
    - Drawn shut: measured (11 photographs, none shows it gathered).
    - Top at the ceiling: measured that the cloth runs up to the ceiling (DSC_0064, 0127, 0128); the 2.75 m is the model's ceiling height for Room 3, not measured again.
    - Top fixing: not established. No rail, rod or batten shows in any photograph, so none is built.
    - Foot: the cloth is rolled on a tube that hangs clear of the floor (DSC_0069, 0127, 0128). Thickness of the roll 2 to 3 cm, built 3 cm: measured, ±1 cm. Clear of the floor about 2 cm at the east end (DSC_0127, and DSC_0069 against the north stub's height) and about 5 cm at the west end (DSC_0069), built level at 4 cm: measured, ±2 cm; the slope is not built.
    - Width: built 1.02 m, which is the model's distance between the two walls (1.10 m) less the bare end of the wall of no. 14. Inferred from the model, and it does not agree with the photographs: counted on the 30 cm floor tiles along the foot of the curtain, the cloth is 2.65 tiles long in DSC_0128 and 2.85 in DSC_0127, so 0.80 to 0.87 m, ±0.07 m. The model's walls here are further apart than the photographs show (see "Open" below).
    - The bare end of the wall of no. 14, 8 cm: measured on DSC_0069 as a strip 5 to 10 cm wide between the wall's corner and the cloth.
    - Colour: inferred. Sampled on DSC_0127 and DSC_0128 against the white plaster of the stub's end in the same shade: about 0.95, 0.92 and 0.80 of the plaster's red, green and blue. In the dim photographs from Room 3 it reads grey.
    - Cloth: plain, soft vertical folds: measured as seen (DSC_0127, 0128). The fold spacing and depth as built: inferred, by eye. Opaque: inferred; every photograph lights it from the front, none shows light through it.
    - Behind the curtain: not known and not built. The block in `layout.js` stays solid plaster. A dark panel (`curtainShade`, 0x0c0c0d) on the block's face behind the cloth is invented: it stands for the unlit space where the eye gets past the cloth, under the roll and at its edges, where the photographs show shadow.
  - Open, found while measuring and not changed (outside this task): the same tile counts give the south stub about 0.17 m thick (DSC_0128; the model has 0.30 m), and the west face of the wall of no. 14 4.1 to 4.4 tiles, 1.2 to 1.3 m, wide (DSC_0071 and 0070; the model has 1.06 m). With the 0.80 to 0.87 m between the walls, this corner of the model is about 0.3 m longer west to east than the photographs show, and the wall of no. 14 is 0.15 to 0.25 m wider than modelled, so either its end reaches further north or Room 3's south wall lies further south. Which wall is out of place cannot be told without measuring Room 3's length again, and moving either would move no. 14 or the edge of the opening. Asked of the reconstructor.
  - The S34 photographs (MAMŰ's own, 2014 to 2021, other exhibitions; checked 17 November 2026) agree and change nothing in what is built. `curtain_room3.jpg`, taken in Room 3 looking south-east, shows the same arrangement years earlier: a pale grey cloth in narrow vertical pleats, drawn shut from the ceiling to the floor on the north side of the plastered wall that carried no. 14 in 2025, running east from that wall's corner towards the south stub, with Room 4 open beyond it. `corridor_room3-4.jpg`, taken in Room 3 looking east through the way into Room 4, shows that way clear under its header, with no cloth across it. Two differences, not built: in `curtain_room3.jpg` the cloth starts right at the corner of the wall, where DSC_0069 (2025) shows a bare strip of 5 to 10 cm, and it reads greyer, under other lights. The 2025 photographs are kept for both, since the model shows the exhibition of 2025. The S34 photographs give no scale for the width.

- Room 2, the wall above the doorway from Room 1 (17 November 2026). The reconstructor: the wall above the opening, where the Singapore video (no. 6) hangs, is cold white while the rest of Room 2 is warm beige; give it the same warm light.

Room 2's side of the wall between Rooms 1 and 2 is drawn with the warm whitewash (`warmBrick`, 0xefe2bf) on every block except the lintel over the doorway (x 5.40 to 5.85, z 3.70 to 4.55, from 2.10 m to the ceiling), whose Room 2 face had no face of its own and so took the block's material, the cold white of Room 1 (`whiteBrick`, 0xf4f2ec). The lintel's underside was already warm. The Room 2 pendant light and the ambient light reach that face as they reach the rest of the wall; nothing there is lit differently.

The floor-plan guide (S33, page 6) groups DSC_0050 to 0052 with the Room 2 views; that they look west at this wall is read from the photographs themselves (the doorway into Room 1, with its window and radiator beyond).

The drawing (the canvas, 512 × 724 at the same 1 : 1.414 proportion, and its resolution per device) is unchanged, so the type is now as small on the wall as on the real sheets; the full text still opens with a click or Enter. The centres stay where they were: `text-r1` 1.38 m above the floor, along z 5.48; `text-r2` 1.50 m, along x 8.395.

Both are within ±5 cm of the old centres, so the centres were kept. Measured, ±5 cm. Keeping the centre (and not the top edge) where the photographs have it is the assistant's choice; with A4 the top edge is about 4 cm lower than the photographs show it if the real sheets were larger (next point).

The sheets are built A4 as the reconstructor decided; the difference for the two text sheets is asked of her (Uncertainty: the courses vary by ±1 cm between walls, so 0.32 to 0.41 m).

- Room 2: the toilet corridor beside the bar, and the room sheet back on the wall beside it (17 November 2026). The reconstructor: coming from Room 1 there is a longer wall where the room sheet hangs, and the toilet is in a dark corridor next to the bar, behind the room sheet; the model had a door and no corridor. Make the wall left of the door (left on the plan, west) longer and add the dark corridor; the toilets need not be walkable, but the corridor's opening must look right. Put the third sheet back, A4, where the photographs show it.
  - What was there (since 30aaa0d, 23 October 2026, source not recorded): Room 2's south wall from x 5.85 to 7.46 with a closed grey door 0.87 m wide in it (x 6.09 to 6.96), 0.24 m of wall west of it and 0.50 m east of it, and nothing behind it. The room sheet had hung on the west face of the bar pillar until 9 November 2026 (see the entry on its removal).
  - What the photographs show. DSC_0047 looks straight down the corridor from its mouth: a short, narrow passage, its floor of large dark tiles one step up from Room 2's, the left side a whitewashed brick wall in the dark, at the end a wall tiled to about shoulder height with a small basin and, above it, a round mirror in a square black frame; on the right, towards the end, a dark door frame and a black door, with tiles beyond it (the toilet). DSC_0048, taken a little to the right of 0047, shows the corridor on the left and, right of its mouth, the end of a whitewashed wall facing the camera, with the room sheet pinned about 8 cm from the corner; the same red patches of bare brick on that corner appear in 0047 (the right edge of the mouth) and 0049. So the sheet hangs on the wall west of the corridor's mouth, on Room 2's side, facing north, and the toilet lies behind that wall. DSC_0049 follows that wall west: at least 0.8 m of it shows (counted on the courses), and it runs on out of the frame. The plan (`Alaprajz_ok new.jpg`) and the reconstructor's guide (S33, page 1) draw a corridor running south from Room 2's south-west corner with the toilet block east of it, under the bar; the photographs differ in two points (there is a wall with the sheet west of the mouth, and the toilet's door is on the west side) and were followed. The S34 photographs `bar_from_room_3.webp` and `room_2_bar_kitchen_detail.jpg` show the bar and the kitchen behind it, from Room 3 and from Room 2, but not the corridor, which is out of their frames.

    - The wall with the room sheet: Room 2's south wall from x 5.85 to 6.63 (0.78 m), 0.40 m thick as before, in the same warm whitewash.
    - The mouth: x 6.63 to 7.408 (0.78 m) through the 0.40 m wall, with Room 2's floor in it and a lintel over it at 2.10 m. Its east side is the end of the bar's back wall (x 7.408), so the bar pillar stands just east of it.
    - The corridor behind it, running south from the back of the wall (z 6.13) to the tiled end wall (z 7.68): side walls of whitewashed brick in the dark (`dimBrick`, as on Room 4's dark south wall), a floor 0.10 m up of dark tiles, a dark ceiling at 2.60 m, tiles to 1.37 m on the end wall, a basin 0.46 m wide (top 0.80 m above the corridor floor) 0.25 m from the east wall, a round mirror 0.39 m across in a square black frame centred 1.61 m above the corridor floor, and a black door on the west side from z 6.72 to 7.58. No lamp: it gets only what reaches it from Room 2, through dim materials.
