    - Removed: the closed grey door and its frame (the `door` material is no longer used).
    - The room sheet (`roomsheet` in `src/data/exhibition.json`): A4, on the north face of that wall, its east edge 8 cm from the corner of the mouth (centre x 6.445), its centre 1.22 m above the floor. It shows what the other two sheets show, the exhibition text with the curator's text that is still a placeholder (Next, point 6), and opens the same text. The real sheet also carried the plan with the numbered places and the works list (DSC_0048, DSC_0049); the code that drew those was removed on 9 November 2026 (3034d36) and is not restored here. The accessible version now names both Room 2 sheets in one sentence (`scripts/text-edition.mjs`; no string changed).
  - Values and their status:
    - The corridor's width, 0.78 m: measured on DSC_0047, ±0.1 m. It is as wide at its end as at its mouth (the ratio of the two widths in the picture equals the ratio of the depths of their floor lines). The scale comes from the end wall: it is about 5.2 wall tiles wide, and with tiles of 15 cm the basin is 0.46 m, a usual size. With the floor tiles taken as 30 or 33 cm the corridor would be 0.65 to 0.70 m.
    - Its length from the step to the end wall, 1.55 m: measured on DSC_0047 from the same perspective, ±0.3 m.
    - The east side in line with the end of the bar's back wall: inferred (the left wall in DSC_0047 runs on unbroken from the mouth to the end, and the bar is next to it).
    - The length of the wall with the sheet, 0.78 m: follows from the two values above; DSC_0049 shows at least 0.8 m (±0.1 m), so it is built at the short end of what the photograph allows. Uncertainty ±0.15 m.
    - The step, 0.10 m: at least 5.5 cm shows in DSC_0047 before the frame cuts it; the rest is inferred.
    - The lintel at 2.10 m (the height of the other doorways): invented; no photograph shows the top of the mouth. The corridor's ceiling at 2.60 m: inferred from a fitting at about 2.5 m at the end in DSC_0047.
    - The wall's thickness: the model's 0.40 m kept; DSC_0047 suggests at least 0.45 m from the depth of the mouth's side. Not changed.
    - The fittings' sizes and heights: measured on DSC_0047 against the corridor's width, ±20 %. The door's place: measured (its dark frame starts about a third of the way along and runs to the end). Built shut: inferred; tiles show past it, so it may have stood open.
    - Darkness: dim materials, so that the corridor reads nearly black as in DSC_0047; inferred, by eye.
    - The sheet's place along the wall: measured on DSC_0048 (8 cm ±3 cm from the corner, by the courses and the sheet's own width). Its height: inferred, ±0.1 m. In DSC_0048 its bottom edge is 4.9 courses (0.37 m) above the upper of the two red patches at the corner, and DSC_0047, from the corridor's perspective, puts that patch 0.66 to 0.70 m above Room 2's floor; so its bottom is at about 1.07 m and its centre at 1.22 m.
  - Sight lines: the corridor opens off Room 2's south wall west of the bar pillar; nothing that stood between a visitor and a screen moved, and no screen moved.
  - Evidence: DSC_0047, DSC_0048, DSC_0049; the plan `Alaprajz_ok new.jpg` (S05) as traced in `layout.js`; the floor-plan guide (S33), page 1, and page 6, which groups DSC_0047 to 0049; S34 `bar_from_room_3.webp` and `room_2_bar_kitchen_detail.jpg` (checked: they do not show the corridor).
  - Decided by: reconstructor (a longer wall with the room sheet, a dark corridor and no door; the sheet back, A4, where the photographs show it; its text kept a placeholder); assistant (the measurements, the corridor's east side, the lintel, the fittings, showing the exhibition text on the sheet).
- Videos by room, on phones and low-power devices (18 November 2026). Which videos load now goes by the visitor's room, not by the nearest screens, on those devices only. **Who:** `lowPower` in `src/device.js`, now the one place for it: a phone or tablet (`isTouch`: a coarse primary pointer or touch events, and no fine pointer that can hover; a laptop with a touch screen and a mouse or trackpad is a computer, D10) or any device that reports 4 processor threads or fewer (`navigator.hardwareConcurrency`; 8 if the browser does not say; Safari reports 4 or 8 whatever the machine, so some Macs are in). Memory is not asked. Laptops and desktops are as before: the nearest 8 screens within 16 m and Let it Flow from the Room 2 | 3 doorway, normal loops only. **The rule** (reconstructor, 16 November 2026): the visitor's own room plays its screens' normal loops; a screen in another room that can be seen through an opening from any share of the room's floor plays a light version (smaller picture, lower bitrate, a 12 s loop), never nothing; Siluett, Let it Flow's wall projection, plays its 9 s short loop in every case; a video whose sound reaches a room (`src/audio/ambience.js`) has its sound loaded there, also where its picture is not seen or is light; leaving a room pauses its videos and coming back resumes them, nothing is unloaded; before Enter, Room 1's set loads, muted. Sound levels and placing are unchanged, and so are the rooms a sound reaches: Room 1 keeps the sound of nos 6 and 7 through the doorway, Room 3 keeps the faint sound of Natural Life and of Siluett. **Further decisions of the reconstructor (18 November 2026):** no. 15 seen from Room 2 gets its light version, as the rule says "visible at all"; Siluett's light version is the whole 9 s loop at 640 × 360 with its sound; in a doorway, between two rooms, each screen plays the better of the two rooms' versions; the light versions are made from the loops already in `public/video`, as the artist's files are not coming. **The table as built** (`src/world/videoPlan.js`; N normal loop, L light version, s sound only, Ls light version and sound, - nothing; columns no. 3, 6, 7, 8, 15, 17, Siluett, floor): Room 1 N s Ls L - - L L; Room 2 L N N L L L L L; Room 3 L Ls Ls N L Ls Ls L; Room 4 L L L L N N N N. **Evidence:** the sight lines are calculated from `src/world/layout.js` (standing points 12.5 cm apart, eye 1.62 m, straight lines against the wall blocks at their heights, the curtain and the two plinths in the Room 1 doorway; `reports/videos-by-room.md`, section 4), not measured in the gallery and not checked by eye in the running site. "Seen" is any part of the screen from at least one standing point; three cells are in on that count only: no. 15 from Room 2 (2 of 492 points, a seventh of the screen, about 3 px wide on an upright phone), no. 7 from Room 1 (21 of 1,420 points, about 21 px) and no. 7 from Room 4 (12 of 1,513 points, about 10 px). Not seen at all: nos 6, 15 and 17 from Room 1. "Heard" is a level of 0.02 or more somewhere in the room, before the facing factor, from the code's own formula. Inferred. Uncertainty: the sight lines take no furniture other than the plinths, the bar, the curtain and the walls, and no turn of the head; a cell near the limit (no. 15 from Room 2) could go either way in the gallery. Decided by: reconstructor (the rule and the four further decisions), assistant (reading the sight lines into the table; the 12 s length, the sizes and the bitrates of the light versions). **Light versions** (`scripts/make_videos.sh light`; per file in `docs/video-versions.md`): nos 6, 7, 8, 15 at 320 × 180, 180 kbit/s; no. 17 at 320 × 180, 120 kbit/s; no. 3 at 128 × 154, 120 kbit/s; the floor at 640 × 360, 400 kbit/s; all 12 s and silent; Siluett 640 × 360, 400 kbit/s, 9 s, with the loop's sound copied (+21.5 dB as encoded). The sound of nos 6, 7 and 17 alone, copied from their loops (Natural Life at its +15 dB), for the rooms where it is heard but the picture is light or not seen: `-sound.m4a`. Made on 18 November 2026 with FFmpeg 6.1.1 (`ffmpeg version 6.1.1-essentials_build-www.gyan.dev`, libx264); sound copied from the loops, never encoded again (earlier encodes the same day with TouchDesigner's ffmpeg and Intel Quick Sync were replaced and are in the branch history only). From the artist's files: Siluett (`siluett_short.mp4`) and the floor (`Let it flow_ok.mp4`), with the loops' cuts and levels. From the loops (second generation; the artist's files not on this laptop): nos 3, 6, 7 and 17; nos 8 and 15 from their new 16:9 loops (third generation, entry below). Sizes: 0.12 to 0.60 MB each, 4.0 MB for the light versions and sound files together (the loops are 43 MB). **Measured** (`tests/perf/measure.mjs --phone`, 18 November 2026, this laptop, full Chromium drawing on its GPU; before = a build of ba3b7f1, after = this branch; JSON in the session's scratchpad, not in the repository). Video and sound bytes downloaded: before Enter and in Room 1, 22.6 MB before, 6.1 MB after; on moving to Room 2, 1.8 and 2.3 MB; Room 3, 10.8 and 7.0 MB; Room 4, 2.5 and 12.0 MB (Room 4's normal loops now arrive only there); a whole visit, 37.7 and 27.4 MB. Files playing in Room 1: 5 normal loops (26.5 MB of files) before, against one loop, four light versions and two sound files (8.1 MB) after; in Room 3, 7 loops (35.9 MB) against one loop, seven light versions and two sound files (14.5 MB). Decoded video frames uploaded to the 3D view, at the end of the visit: 21.5 MB before, 13.7 MB after (all files loaded on the way are kept, as the rule says). JS heap used: 9 to 10 MB in both, unchanged (it does not hold video). Working set of the browser's renderer and GPU processes in Room 1: 413 and 392 MB before, 357 and 326 MB after; in Room 4, after the whole visit, 478 and 550 MB before, 463 and 541 MB after. These are this laptop's process counts, not a phone's memory: a phone's video decoder and GPU memory are in none of them, and `performance.measureUserAgentSpecificMemory()` is not offered there. Frame rates (6 s per room, 7 to 37 fps) moved both ways between runs while other tests used the same GPU, so they show no difference either way.
- Wiena Wrong Landscape I and II (nos 8 and 15): the loops are now 16:9, cut from the 4:3 videos by taking equal strips from the top and the bottom (the full width, the middle 0.75 of the height), and the 16:9 screens show them whole. Corrected by the artist: the gallery's TVs showed 16:9 cuts he made himself, not the 4:3 videos cropped or stretched; until he sends those cuts, the centred cut stands in for them. The earlier reading (entry above, "the 4:3 loops ... are cropped to the 16:9 screens", with a band measured off centre: no. 8 from 0.085, no. 15 from 0.148 of the height) was the assistant's reading of the photographs DSC_0089 and DSC_0120; it is replaced, and `video.mediaAspect` and `video.cropTop` are gone from `exhibition.json` for both works. The viewer now plays the same 16:9 loops. Made on 18 November 2026 with FFmpeg 6.1.1 (above), libx264, from the 4:3 loops in `public/video` (second generation; the artist's files are not coming): `w08-wiena-wrong-landscape-i.mp4` 960 × 540 at 1000 kbit/s (4.7 MB, was 6.2 MB), `w15-wiena-wrong-landscape-ii.mp4` 960 × 540 at 750 kbit/s (3.5 MB, was 4.7 MB), three quarters of the 4:3 loops' bitrates (PSNR against the same part of the 4:3 loop: 42.8 dB and 38.4 dB). `scripts/make_videos.sh wiena` remakes them, from the artist's files when they are in the folder. Decided by: artist (16:9 cuts), reconstructor (the equal strips, as a stand-in until the artist's own cuts arrive; passed on by the coordinator on 17 November 2026). Invented as to which strips: the centred cut is a stand-in, the artist's own cut may sit higher or lower. Uncertainty: the photographs suggested no. 8's picture sat about 0.04 of the height above centre and no. 15's 0.02 below; the centred cut differs from those by up to 29 and 17 rows of 720.
- Room 1, the stairs: the handrail runs the whole flight, and the wall side of the flight is corrected (18 November 2026). The reconstructor, on the before and after pictures: the wall-side end of the flight looks wrong (the open side "resembles the photos a bit"), and "the handle of the staircase finishes before the end of the staircase, which is not realistic (in general, not only in this case)".
  - Handrail: it started at the first post, 0.36 m up the slope, and ended inside the west wall's face. In DSC_0015 and DSC_0016 the rail runs on down the slope past its lowest post to about the end of the flight, and in S34 `room_1_stairs_important.webp` and `room_1_from_top_of_stairs.webp` it runs level over the landing to the street door. Now: from the foot (x = foot + 0.02) up the slope, level over the landing and through the wall's thickness to the street door (WO + 0.05), with posts every 0.75 m as before. Measured on the photographs (the rail's lower end about at the foot, ±0.15 m); the length through the wall is inferred. No other handrail or railing is built in the scene (searched `props.js`, `architecture.js`, `gallery.js`).
  - Wall side: the wall-side parapet was 0.13 m thick, with the 0.25 m coping brick overhanging the gap to the north wall by 0.12 m, so the coping floated; now the parapet is 0.25 m thick (z 0.25 to 0.50), as wide as the brick, so the brick sits on it exactly, as in DSC_0014 to DSC_0016 and S34 `stairs_room1.jpg` where the coping sits on the wall. The gap to the north wall is 0.25 m instead of 0.37 m (the 0.37 m was read at the foot only, DSC_0016; uncertainty about ±0.1 m). The grey rendered base at its foot is 0.17 m high instead of 0.25 m, level with where the sloping coping starts (DSC_0015, DSC_0016: the last bricks meet the base at its top); it no longer stands proud.
  - Not changed: slope, step count, landing height, the open side, the foot's brick pier. Collision: the wall-side obstacle uses the same z as before.
  - Decided by: reconstructor (the corrections, on her reading of the pictures). Evidence: DSC_0014, DSC_0015, DSC_0016; S34 `room_1_stairs_important.webp`, `stairs_room1.jpg`, `room_1_from_top_of_stairs.webp`. Measured: the rail's lower end, the parapet's width. Inferred: the rail through the wall. Not checked: whether the wall-side parapet is thicker higher up (hidden by chairs in the photographs).

Both say the gap was too wide, by a factor of about 0.77 and 0.57; the mean, about 0.65, is a gap of about 0.7 m instead of 1.02 m.

- Wall stains from the photographs (Brief 6, 17 November 2026). Stains seen in the 2025 photographs are now drawn where each photograph shows them, through the wall overlay in `src/world/surface.js` (`PHOTO_STAINS`: a box on the wall or ceiling, a shape, and a colour ratio read off the photo against clean wall beside the stain; no new texture). Positions are measured on one or two photographs against brick courses (7.7 cm), floor tiles (30 cm) and objects of known size or place (radiators, windows, works in `exhibition.json`); the shapes inside each outline (ragged edge, mottling) are invented. The procedural overlays already in `surface.js` (brightness drift, whitewash patches, rising damp with a tide line, pipe dirt) are unchanged; they are not tied to any photograph, and the questions on them are below. Areas left to other work and not touched: the stairs' flight, Room 4's end wall and pipes, Room 2's bar and toilet corridor, the curtain. Decided by: reconstructor (stains from the photographs only); assistant (the reading and the measurements). One line per stain (photo; wall; position; size; measured or inferred; uncertainty):
  - Room 1, ceiling, south-west corner: yellow-olive water stain with a darker rim (DSC_0028, 0029, 0030, 0031); along the south wall from the corner (x -0.15) to x 1.9, reaching z 6.35; about 2.0 × 0.65 m; colour ratio 0.93/0.90/0.80. Measured from the corner and #4's edges; ±0.3 m along the wall, ±0.2 m out from it.
  - Room 1, south wall, top by the same corner: faint yellowing under that stain (DSC_0030, 0031); x -0.15 to 1.2, from the ceiling down 0.15 m (to 0.18 at most). Inferred from two oblique views; ±0.3 m along, ±0.05 m in height.
  - Room 1, east wall (south pier, x 4.85), foot under the radiator: dark mottled damp (DSC_0023, 0024); z 5.05 to 6.25, y 0 to 0.12; ratio 0.65/0.62/0.55. Measured against the radiator (0.94 m wide, 0.85 m tall from 0.1 m); ±0.05 m along, ±0.03 m in height. Mostly hidden behind the radiator, as in the photographs.
  - Room 1, east wall, between the radiator's south end and its pipe: brown damp streak (DSC_0023); z 6.04 to 6.16, y 0.16 to 0.48; same ratio. Measured against the radiator; ±0.03 m along, ±0.04 m in height.
  - Room 1, street wall, back of the round-vent window's niche (x -0.37): three flakes in the whitewash showing grey-beige render (DSC_0011; also in 0012 and 0033), at y 1.14 to 1.19 and z 3.43 to 3.51, 3.28 to 3.33 and 3.15 to 3.22; 5 to 8 cm wide; ratio 0.87/0.85/0.74. Measured against the window (0.92 m), its sill (1.7 m) and the radiator top (0.95 m); ±0.05 m.
  - Room 1, seen and not built: dark specks along the cable run on the recess ceiling north of the doorway and a 10 cm grey flake on the north pier's corner at about 2.1 m (DSC_0020): too small to place. The north wall (DSC_0016, 0017), the south wall below the ceiling stain (DSC_0025 to 0029) and the east wall (DSC_0019 to 0022) are clean whitewash down to the floor.
  - Room 2, east wall, west face of the pillar between the storage gate and the doorway to Room 3 (x 9.85): grey streaky marks, perhaps from old fixings (DSC_0061; also in 0060); z 1.82 to 2.38, y 1.93 to 2.12; about 0.55 × 0.2 m; ratio 0.70/0.71/0.73. Measured against the 2.75 m ceiling and the face's edges (gate and doorway arris), from one oblique view; ±0.15 m both ways.

  - Room 3, west face of the north stub between Rooms 3 and 4 (x 15.6, plaster, under #9): yellow-brown damp blotches along the foot (DSC_0095; also in 0093, 0094 and 0096); z 1.87 to 2.92, y 0 to 0.30; ratio about 0.88/0.86/0.76 at the darkest. Measured against #9 (0.75 m tall, centre 1.45 m, along 2.33) and the stub's width (1.19 m); ±0.05 m both ways. The S34 photographs show the stub too small to confirm the stain.
  - Room 3, the Room 2 / Room 3 north pillar, its east face (x 10.2) and the doorway's jamb (z 2.557): dirty beige-grey whitewash on the lowest four to five courses, the brick crumbling at the base (DSC_0085; also in 0084); y 0 to 0.36 on the east face and 0 to 0.34 on the jamb; ratio 0.92/0.85/0.74. Measured by brick courses (7.7 cm); ±0.04 m in height.

The model's south wall in Room 4 is dark (`dimBrick`), so they barely show.

Not built because their places cannot be tied to the model: the north wall has a recess beside the gas meter (DSC_0100 to 0103) that the model does not have, the band lies in the pipes' zone (left to the Room 4 worker), and the repair's place along the south wall is uncertain by about ±1.5 m. Listed for the worker rebuilding Room 4.

Room 4's end wall under the projection (DSC_0113 to 0116), Room 2's bar and toilet corridor (DSC_0047, 0048, 0064 to 0066, too dark to read) and the curtain: nothing stain-like read.

Evidence and statuses: `README_room2.md` (in the private photo folder): the room 4.00 × 4.00 m from the plan (measured); the ceiling 2.75 m and its joist spacing, the kitchen's depth (back wall z 6.37) and its furniture, the storage opening's width (0.65 m) and the storage room's depth, the opening to Room 3, and the places of the tables and chairs are inferred (±0.1 m for the ceiling); the corridor 0.78 m wide measured on DSC_0047; the screens 0.80 × 0.46 m and the rest of the corrections are the reconstructor's (20 and 21 November 2026). The wall texture is Kristóf Szabó's `brick-white` (tint #f4f2ec); the floor, corridor tile and ceiling textures are generated; the README says no photo pixels. Decided by: the reconstructor (v9 accepted, 20 and 21 November 2026). Model: the Blender agent. Import: the assistant. Until Group B the code-built Room 2 still stands in the same place.
- Room 2 model, Group B: what the model replaces is out of the code (21 November 2026; `layout.js`, `props.js`). Removed: Room 2's BLOCKS (the north wall and gated opening, the storage alcove shell, the south wall with the room sheet, the corridor mouth and the four corridor blocks, the bar counter, the pillar x 7.481-7.772, z 5.264-5.73 (there is none, the reconstructor), the back wall, the five Room 2 | Room 3 wall blocks x 9.85-10.35, whose Room 3 face the model now carries), Room 2's floor, the storage and corridor floors, Room 2's ceiling, the storage and corridor ceilings and the ceiling strip x 5.40-5.85, z 5.0-5.73; in props.js the bar tops, the cabinets, shelves, glasses and bottles behind it, the table and the two chairs, the storage grille and the lumber, with their obstacles. Kept: the two enamel pendant lamps (the lighting). Room 1 | Room 2 wall: the model has Room 2's side, x 5.40-5.85 with the doorway, so Room 1's north pier now stops at x 5.40 (it ran to 5.85) and the recess's back-wall blocks and the doorway's lintel are gone; kept of them x 5.40-5.85, z 4.80-5.00, which the model does not cover. Collisions for Room 2 come back in Group D. Clashes, reported, not reshaped in code: (1) Room 1's floor runs to x 5.60 and the model's floor starts at x 5.40, both at y 0: they overlap in the doorway (x 5.40-5.60, z 3.75-4.55); (2) Room 3's floor starts at x 9.85 and the model's ends at x 10.35, both at y 0: they overlap under the Room 3 doorway (z 2.557-4.60) and the pass-through; (3) the model's walls stop at 2.75 m and its boards at 2.78 m, Room 1's recess ceiling is at 2.80 m: a 2 cm strip at x 5.40, z 3.2-4.8; (4) the model's west wall ends at z 4.80 at x 5.40 (the back of the wall with the room sheet), the recess runs to z 5.00 (closed by the kept block); (5) the model's blank A4 on the north wall (x 8.30, h 1.42) sits behind the text sheet text-r2 (x 8.395, y 1.50). Decided by: the reconstructor (20 and 21 November 2026). Evidence: README_room2.md. Smoke pass.
- Room 2 model, Group C: the screens of nos 6 and 7 and the room sheet on the model (21 November 2026; `src/data/exhibition.json`, `gallery.js`, `props.js`). Nos 6 and 7 are now 0.80 × 0.46 m (they were 0.94 × 0.53; 15 to 20 % narrower, the reconstructor, 20 November 2026), no. 6 on the west wall at z 2.744 (was 2.794), centre 1.55 m, no. 7 on the north wall at x 7.351 (was 7.201), centre 1.50 m: exactly on the model's `R2_Screen_W06_Singapore` and `R2_Screen_W07_Eden` (frames 0.80 × 0.46 × 0.05 m, checked on the model's faces). They are still driven by exhibition.json; a new `hang.onModel` makes gallery.js draw no bezel of its own (the model's frame is the bezel) and lay the picture 1 mm in front of the frame, 5.1 cm from the wall; the model's switched-off panels (M_R2_Screen_Off) are hidden, the frames, cable and power strip stay. The room sheet (drawRoomSheet, text unchanged, item 14 still open) moved from the old south wall (z 5.73, x 6.015, centre 1.22 m) onto the model's blank A4 on the wall parallel to the bar: face z 4.55, x 6.75, centre 1.50 m (README_room2.md; DSC_0048, DSC_0049). It covers the blank (0.21 × 0.296 m) entirely, so the blank is not hidden: it is one mesh with the second blank on the north wall, which stays. Clash, reported: at 0.80 × 0.46 m the picture covers the whole face of the model's frame, whose dark panel is 0.776 × 0.44 m inside a bezel of about 1.2 cm and 1 cm; the frame shows only from the side. Statuses: the screen sizes are the reconstructor's (inferred, a few cm, ap-d87); places from README_room2.md. Decided by: the reconstructor (20 and 21 November 2026). Smoke pass.
- Room 2 model, Group D: walking and the floor plan (21 November 2026; `layout.js`, `architecture.js`, `props.js`). Room 2's walls are back as collision only: BLOCKS with `collide: true` make no mesh (the model draws them), only the collision lines, which the walking, the floor plan and the plan AO read. From the model's faces: the north wall with the storage opening and its closed gate (x 5.85-9.85, z 1.50-1.73), Room 2's side of the wall to Room 1 north of the doorway (x 5.40-5.85, z 1.73-3.75), the wall with the room sheet (x 5.40-6.98, z 4.55-4.80), the corridor's west wall (x 6.73-6.98, z 4.80-7.88), the step across its mouth (x 6.98-7.76, z 5.122-5.20; not walkable, as before), its east wall (x 7.76-8.06, z 5.122-7.88), the bar counter (x 8.06-10.55, z 4.69-5.122, README_room2.md), and the Room 2 | Room 3 wall's north pier (x 9.85-10.35, z 1.50-2.557) and its part south of the doorway (z 4.60-6.37). Tables and chairs: three boxes read off the model (x 7.85-8.45, z 1.85-2.45; x 8.50-9.48, z 1.80-2.685, the east table with the chair beside it; x 7.333-7.767, z 1.873-2.327); their places are inferred (README). ROOMS r2's south edge is z 5.122 (was 5.73). Checked by walking with the arrow keys: the visitor stops 0.28 m (the walking radius) from the bar from Room 2 and from Room 3, from the corridor's step, the wall with the sheet and the tables; both doorways pass. The viewing points of nos 6 (7.37, 2.74) and 7 (7.35, 3.25), the tour's Room 2 waypoints (6.35, 4.12) and (9.382, 3.317), and the room jump (6.63, 4.12) are clear of the bar, tables and chairs; the room label reads Room 2. Decided by: the reconstructor (20 and 21 November 2026). Smoke pass, no console errors (desktop 1366 × 768 and phone 375 × 667, EN and HU).
- Texture joins S1: the floors meet at the model's edges (22 November 2026; `layout.js`, FLOORS). Room 1's floor ends at x 5.40 (R1.recess; it ran to 5.60) and Room 3's grey floor begins at x 10.35 (it began at 9.85), where Room 2's model's floor (x 5.40-10.35, z 1.50-6.37) begins and ends. Until now both lay at y 0 over x 5.40-5.60 (Room 1 doorway) and x 9.85-10.35, z 1.6-5.79 (under the Room 3 doorway and the pass-through). Seen on screen before: the model's floor mostly won; looking straight down at the Room 1 doorway faint light stripes of Room 1's floor showed through (z-fighting), at the Room 3 doorway nothing showed; both depend on the GPU's depth precision. After: no stripes, the tile change is a straight line at x 5.40 and x 10.35, and the grout lines carry on across both. Evidence: the code values and the glb's vertex extents (measured), screenshots in reports/private/texture-joins/ (before/, probe/, after/; outside the repo). Where the real floors change: at the model's edges, decided by the reconstructor (22 November 2026), not checked against the photographs in this step (DSC_0050-0052, DSC_0063-0064); the tile colours on either side are unchanged. Done by the assistant. Smoke pass.

## Accessibility pilot (from 13 November 2026, branch `wip/a11y-pilot`)
These entries are about presentation: how the site is shown and operated. None of them changes the reconstruction (geometry, hang, lights, sound placement).

- Loading screen: nothing on it moves, for every visitor and not only with `prefers-reduced-motion`. Removed: the slow drift of the key visual, the jittering grain, the card's wipe-in, the logo's wipe-in, and the easing of the progress line and the Enter button. The design is kept as a still: the same picture (held at the middle of its former drift, scale 1.05), one fixed layer of grain, the same card and logo. The fade from the loading screen into the walk after Enter stays. The rule is in `CLAUDE.md` ("Design rules"). Decided by: reconstructor. Not a reconstruction decision (presentation). Evidence: `src/style.css`, `tests/a11y/loader-weight.mjs` (two screenshots 1.3 s apart are identical; no CSS animation runs in the loader).
- Loading screen weight: the title screen itself downloaded 442 KB on a desktop window and 350 KB on a phone (file sizes before the server's compression; 3D engine and textures not counted). The background was 298 KB (`key-visual.webp`, 1600 × 900) and 206 KB (`key-visual-portrait.webp`, 832 × 900): heavy because the picture is grainy. It now loads as AVIF, the same picture at 123 KB and 83 KB (`scripts/make_loader_bg.mjs`, quality 45), with the WebP files kept as the fallback for browsers without AVIF. The title screen now downloads 267 KB (desktop) and 227 KB (phone). Decided by: reconstructor (lighter version asked for); assistant (AVIF, quality 45, chosen by comparing crops by eye). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- Text edition (`text/` in English, `text/hu/` in Hungarian): the exhibition as plain HTML with no images, no external files and no need for JavaScript or WebGL. `scripts/text-edition.mjs` (a Vite plugin, see `vite.config.js`) writes both pages at build time from the files the walk reads: `src/data/exhibition.json`, `src/i18n/*.json`, the rooms in `src/world/layout.js`. It holds the exhibition's facts, the four rooms in visiting order, the 17 works (number, title, medium, size, year, where it is, the artist's text, a link that opens the work in the walk), the exhibition text, the opening speech, the controls, the credits and the rights line. One h1, an h2 per room, an h3 per work. An artist's text that belongs to a series is printed under the first work of the series and linked from the others. Decided by: reconstructor (the edition and what it holds); assistant (the address, the layout of the page, printing a series text once). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).
- Where each work is, in words: `src/world/route.js` (`placeOf`) reads it from the hang data and the room rectangles, nothing is placed by it. Every room is entered walking towards +x (Room 1 down the stairs along its north wall, Rooms 2 to 4 through the doorways), so a wall whose face looks +z is "on the left as you enter", -z "on the right", -x "facing you", +x "behind you". Works on a side wall are counted from the end you enter at; on the other two walls from the left as you face them. A single work on a side wall is also placed in thirds along the room (near the entrance end, about halfway, towards the far end); on the walls with a doorway, left or right of it. The installations get thirds of the room's length from their `anchor`. The words are as exact as the hang data and no more: no. 14 hangs on the closet block that stands 1.1 m out from Room 3's end wall and is still described as on the wall facing you. Decided by: assistant (the rule and the wording), to be confirmed by the reconstructor. Inferred from measured or inferred positions (the hang data keeps its own status). Evidence: `hang` and `anchor` in `exhibition.json`, `ROOMS` in `layout.js`, the door points formerly in `src/main.js`. Uncertainty: the thirds are coarse on purpose; a work near a boundary (no. 7 at 0.34 of Room 2's length) could be called either.

- Keyboard focus in the dialogs (`src/ui/dialog.js`): the panels, the artwork viewer and the sound question move focus in when they open (to Close, or to "Without sound"), keep Tab and Shift+Tab inside, close on Esc and hand focus back to the control that opened them; if that control is gone by then, to the 3D view (inside) or the Enter button (title screen). The panel is named by its heading. The rooms menu says whether it is open and closes on Esc. The 3D view takes focus once you are inside and shows it as a frame clear of the HUD. Every control has one visible focus ring; the footer's real height now sets `--foot-h`, so a footer that wraps cannot cover the toolbar or the keys. Enter on a focused button no longer also opens the work in view. The on-screen keys answer Enter, Space and clicks sent by assistive technology with one short press. The viewer's loop has a Pause / Play button; its native player controls show only under the mouse (always on touch screens), because Chromium keeps every key press, Esc and Tab included, while focus is inside them. Decided by: reconstructor (what the dialogs must do); assistant (how, including the Pause button and the native controls). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- One polite live region (`#announcer`, `src/ui/announce.js`) says, for screen readers: the room when you enter it ("Room 2. Video, bar."); the work in the middle of the view when it changes while you walk or turn by keyboard ("No. 6, Artificial Paradise – Singapore. Press Enter to open."), which is the work Enter opens; each tour stop's caption; and "Tour stopped." when the tour ends. It speaks only on change and at most about once a second; messages closer together are read as one. The same work is named on screen for sighted keyboard users ("Enter to view · 6 …"). The title screen and the tour caption are no longer live regions of their own; the progress line has a name and a value; the Tour and Sound buttons say whether they are on. Decided by: reconstructor (what is announced); assistant (the "Tour stopped." message, the on-screen prompt, the button states). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- Reduced motion (`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, read live from the system in `src/motion.js`; no setting of our own, nothing stored): no glitch transition between rooms, no film grain, no chromatic aberration, no pulsing dot on the map, no camera bob, no eased zoom, no fades or transitions in the interface, and the guided tour jumps from stop to stop (the doorway waypoints are skipped; each stop keeps its time). Bloom, tone mapping and the vignette do not move and stay. Left as it is: the 3% shimmer of the Let it Flow projection's light (`letItFlow.js`), because it belongs to the reconstructed light and not to the interface, and the videos on the screens, which are the works. Decided by: reconstructor (what goes); assistant (bob, zoom, map dot and CSS transitions added to the list; the projection shimmer left alone). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation). Evidence: `tests/a11y/reduced-motion.mjs`.

- The 3D view has an accessible name: what it is, the keys that matter most (arrow keys, Enter) and where the text edition and the full list of keys are. It has `role="application"`, so a screen reader hands the keys to the walk instead of using them for its own reading commands, and it is hidden from assistive technology until the visitor has entered. Decided by: reconstructor (a name that points to the text edition and the controls); assistant (the wording, the role). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- The walk links to the text edition in three places: a skip link that is the first thing Tab reaches ("Text version of the exhibition"), a link on the loading screen under the controls line, and "Text version" in the footer. The page without JavaScript links to it as well. The Enter button still takes focus when loading is done, but only if the visitor has not tabbed anywhere yet. The walk accepts `?work=<id>` (the text edition's links carry it): it opens at that work's viewing point instead of at the foot of the stairs, and names the room and the work to screen readers on coming in. The page is called "text version" where visitors read its name. Decided by: reconstructor (the three links, the link to a work); assistant (the parameter's name, keeping the Enter press before the walk starts, the focus rule). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- Next and previous work by keyboard: Page Down goes on to the next work in visiting order (the tour's order, 17 works), Page Up back to the previous one, with the same jump as "Walk to this work"; the work, and the room when it changes, are announced. Counted from the work in front of you, else from the last one reached this way; past either end it carries on round. The keys are in the controls text (EN/HU) and so in the text edition. Decided by: reconstructor (two keys, visiting order, the existing jump); assistant (Page Down / Page Up: not character keys, so outside WCAG 2.1.4, free in the walk, the same on every keyboard layout; going round at the ends). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation). Evidence: `docs/accessibility/keyboard-test-log.md`, step 4.
- Accessibility statement: a panel reached from the footer ("Accessibility"), in both languages, and the same text as a section of the text edition. It gives the date, what was tested and how, what works, the known gaps, what is planned and the contact address from the contacts data. It is written from the test results in `docs/accessibility/` and claims no conformance. The Hungarian is unconfirmed throughout. Decided by: reconstructor (the statement and its parts; the wording is hers to approve); assistant (the draft). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).
- Checks (`npm run check:a11y`, against `npm run preview`): `tests/a11y/` holds the scripted runs: the built site loads all four rooms with no console errors (`smoke.mjs`), the loading screen's weight and stillness (`loader-weight.mjs`), the text edition (`text-edition.mjs`), reduced motion (`reduced-motion.mjs`), a keyboard-only run from page load (`keyboard.mjs`) and axe-core on the text edition and on every state of the walk (`axe.mjs`). On 13 November 2026: axe found no violations in 34 states; the keyboard run passed 29 of 29 steps. Rerun on 14 November 2026 on the final code: axe again none in 34 states, the keyboard run 31 of 31 steps (two new ones for the letter keys), the new mouse-and-focus run 14 of 14, reduced motion 13 of 13, and the new flash run no more than 2 flashes a second. The statement (EN/HU) gives these results and is dated 14 November 2026. The first runs found and I fixed: Enter on the 3D view opened the viewer and the same key press closed it again; Esc and Tab died inside a video's native controls; Enter after a jump could open the work seen before the jump; the keycaps' names did not contain their visible letters (WCAG 2.5.3); page content lay outside landmarks (the 3D view and its controls are now in `<main>`, first in the page). Not checked here: frame rate and memory on a real phone or laptop (this machine renders in software), any screen reader (`docs/accessibility/screen-reader-test.md` is the script for NVDA), other browsers. `docs/accessibility/findings.md` has the WCAG 2.1.4 finding on the single-key shortcuts and the XAUR table. Decided by: reconstructor (the checks); assistant (the scripts and the fixes). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- Letter keys only in the 3D view (14 November 2026): T, M, E, W/A/S/D and the zoom keys (+ − = _ 0) now work only while the 3D view has focus, which is the third way WCAG 2.1.4 allows a character-key shortcut. Pressing the mouse on the view gives it focus (it used to give focus up); a mouse click on the toolbar, the rooms menu or the zoom readout, and closing a panel or the viewer with the mouse, hand focus back to the view. Page Down, Page Up, Esc, Enter, the arrows and Shift are as they were. The controls text (EN/HU) says so, the statement's 2.1.4 line is rewritten in both languages, and `docs/accessibility/findings.md` has the before and after. A review of the change found mouse paths that still left the keys dead (the map, EN / HU, closing the rooms menu by mouse, the zoom readout), and three focus faults (a mouse close on the title screen sent focus to Enter, the keyboard frame stayed on the view after a mouse press, "Walk to this work" on the title screen could pull focus out of the sound question); all are fixed, and `tests/a11y/focus-mouse.mjs` checks them. On the title screen a mouse close goes back to the opener, and only a real mouse counts as one. Decided by: reconstructor (she reversed her earlier decision not to touch these keys); assistant (how focus comes back to the view after a mouse click elsewhere, and the fixes from the review). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation). Evidence: `docs/accessibility/keyboard-test-log.md`, steps 7.0a and 7.0b; `tests/a11y/focus-mouse.mjs`.

- Hungarian wording (14 November 2026): the Hungarian addresses no one where it can, with impersonal forms and no te forms. Fourteen strings were replaced or confirmed by the reconstructor and lost their `[HU: confirm]` mark: the text version's lines on the exhibition text sheet, the artist's text printed above, the small screen, the wall by the entrance and the two "beside the entrance" phrases, the two "how you get in" lines, the name of the 3D view, "A tárlatvezetés leállt.", and four paragraphs of the accessibility statement. The statement's contact line now reads "If something does not work, please tell the creator of the digital exhibition." in both languages, with those words linked to the e-mail address in the contacts data (`link` in the statement's section; no address is written in the i18n files). On the page at `text/` only, the same address is also written out as a plain line under that sentence, so it can be read, copied or printed (decided by the reconstructor, 14 November 2026). The rule is in `CLAUDE.md`. 40 marks remain for her to confirm. Decided by: reconstructor (every wording). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- The glitch pulse is removed for everyone (14 November 2026). It was the blocky band displacement and colour tear that fired when you crossed between rooms or jumped to a room or a work (`GlitchPulseEffect` in `src/fx/post.js`, added by the assistant with the first post chain). Measured against WCAG 2.3.1 with `tests/a11y/flash.mjs` (frames drawn at a fixed 60 a second, luminance followed in a 16 × 12 grid): one room change gave 1 flash in a second, one jump 3, and jumps in quick succession (the pulse fired 30 times in a second, as a held Page Down did) up to 10 flashes a second on 22% of the screen. The rule was: if it can flash more than three times a second, it goes for everyone. Nothing replaces it; a room change is now marked by the room label and the announcement only. Page Down and Page Up no longer repeat while held, since cutting between views that fast would flash by itself; after both changes the most measured is 2 flashes a second (Page Down pressed six times a second). This also ends the pulse that the sound entry above says "stays", and the reduced-motion entry's "no glitch transition" now holds for every visitor. The colour fringes (chromatic aberration) no longer swell either: they followed the pulse and a random "glitch" band from the sound, which twitched them now and then when Web Audio was missing. Film grain and steady colour fringes stay for visitors without reduced motion. Decided by: reconstructor (measure it, and remove it for everyone if it can exceed three flashes a second); assistant (the measurement method, and stopping the key repeat). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation; the effect was invented). Evidence: `docs/accessibility/findings.md`, "Flashing on a room change and WCAG 2.3.1". Uncertainty: measured in software rendering at 640 × 360, not on a real display.

- Hungarian without te forms, proposals (14 November 2026): nine interface strings that addressed the visitor as te (the page description, the sound question and its hint, the message when the 3D view cannot start, the drag hint, the two click prompts, the map's label, the scroll hint) have impersonal versions in `hu.json`, marked `[HU: confirm]` until the reconstructor confirms them; old and new are in `docs/accessibility/hu-address-forms.md`. The tenth, a paragraph of the accessibility statement, takes the reconstructor's own wording. Decided by: reconstructor (the rule and that these change); assistant (the proposed wording). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- Naming (14 November 2026): in every text a visitor reads, in both languages and on the page at `text/`, the reconstruction is "digital exhibition" / "digitális kiállítás" (no more "3D walk", "the walk", "bejárás", "3D kiállítás"), and the page at `text/` is "Accessible version (text format)" / "akadálymentes változat (szöveges formátum)" where it is first named or linked, "accessible version" / "akadálymentes változat" elsewhere. "A bejárás sorrendjében" (in the order of the visit) became "a látogatás sorrendjében", since it never named the reconstruction (in the accessible version's description and introduction, and, a day later caught in a recheck, in the accessibility statement). The canvas stays "3D view"; the site title, code names, file names and this log keep their words. The rules are in `CLAUDE.md`. Left as they were: the legal notice (two sentences say "walkable 3D reconstruction" / "bejárható 3D-rekonstrukcióként"; legal text changes only on request) and the no-JavaScript notice in `index.html` (kept on request). Decided by: reconstructor (the names and where each form goes); assistant (the wording around them, listed string by string for her). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- Hungarian and English confirmed by the reconstructor (14 November 2026): the controls text (both languages, in full sentences), the skip link and the loading-screen link ("Accessible version of the digital exhibition (text format)" / "A kiállítás digitális változatának akadálymentes változata (szöveges formátum)"), the link back from the accessible version ("Go to the digital exhibition" / "Tovább a digitális kiállításhoz"), the Hungarian announcement for the exhibition text, and the statement's paragraph on screen readers. Every other `[HU: confirm]` mark was removed after the naming change, as confirmed; nine marks remain, on the impersonal proposals of the same day, and with them the statement's sentence that the Hungarian is still to be confirmed. Decided by: reconstructor (every wording). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- The language not chosen in the navbar (EN or HU) is drawn at 0.75 opacity instead of 0.5: at 0.5 it measured 2.74:1 on the title screen, below the 4.5:1 of WCAG 1.4.3; at 0.75 it is 5.1:1 there and 10:1 inside. The chosen language stays full strength and underlined. Found by the axe rerun of 14 November 2026 (in English axe could not measure it over the backdrop and set it aside). Decided by: assistant (a fix to keep the pilot free of serious violations; the reconstructor may prefer another way to mark the choice). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- Final wording of the nine strings that addressed the visitor as te (14 November 2026): the page description (also the meta and social-card descriptions), the sound question and its hint, the message when the 3D view cannot start, the drag hint, the two click prompts, the floor plan's label and the mouse-wheel hint are set in the reconstructor's own words in both languages, and the contact line of the accessibility statement now says "by e-mail" / "e-mailben". No string carries `[HU: confirm]` any more, so the statement's known gap about unconfirmed Hungarian is deleted in both languages. The table is in `docs/accessibility/hu-address-forms.md`. The mouse-wheel hint stays where it was, on the chip under the on-screen keys; the sentence is longer, so the chip is two lines high in both languages and the zoom keys beside it moved up 14 px to stay level with the arrow keys. Decided by: reconstructor (wording); assistant (leaving the hint on its chip, the two lines). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- New rule for Hungarian (14 November 2026): the assistant writes new Hungarian strings itself, impersonal (no te, no Ön) and with the names in `CLAUDE.md`; nothing in the interface is marked `[HU: confirm]` any more and nothing waits for confirmation. Instead `docs/accessibility/hu-confirm.md` lists every Hungarian string that is new or changed since the last check, with its English, for a later reading by a Hungarian reader. `tests/tools/hu-confirm.py` writes the list by comparing `hu.json` with fingerprints of the strings already checked (`tests/tools/hu-checked.txt`, first taken on 14 November 2026 when the reconstructor had worded or confirmed every string), and fails if a mark or a key in one language only turns up. The mark handling is gone from `scripts/text-edition.mjs`. Decided by: reconstructor (the rule); assistant (the fingerprint file as the way to know what is new). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- The accessibility statement's panel in the digital exhibition shows the e-mail address as a plain line under the contact sentence, in both languages, as the page at `text/` already did, so it can be read and copied without an e-mail program. It is the address already in the contacts data (`src/ui/panels.js` reads it from there; none is written in the texts). Decided by: reconstructor. Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- Footer links and focus (14 November 2026): a mouse click on a footer or navbar link that opens a new tab or leaves the page (Instagram, the MAMŰ logo, the accessible version) now hands focus to the 3D view first, as EN / HU and the toolbar already did, so the letter keys work at once on coming back; a middle-button click does the same. A keyboard press leaves focus on the link. This closes the last open finding (number 10) of the review of the letter-key change. Checked by `tests/a11y/focus-mouse.mjs`, steps 14 to 16. Decided by: reconstructor (to fix it); assistant (how). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- The notice shown without JavaScript uses the new names (14 November 2026): its link reads "Accessible version of the digital exhibition (text format)" / "A kiállítás digitális változatának akadálymentes változata (szöveges formátum)", the same words as the skip link, and its first sentence says "The digital exhibition needs JavaScript and WebGL." / "A digitális kiállításhoz JavaScript és WebGL szükséges." The earlier "keep it the same" was meant as the same as the skip link; the assistant had read it as "leave it unchanged". Decided by: reconstructor (the link's words); assistant ("digital exhibition" in the first sentence, by the naming rule). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- Legal notice, changed at the reconstructor's request (14 November 2026). "About this site" calls the site a digital exhibition in both languages ("A non-commercial digital exhibition that can be walked through in the browser. It documents …" / "Nem kereskedelmi célú, a böngészőben bejárható digitális kiállítás. … mutatja be."), in place of "as a walkable 3D reconstruction" / "bejárható 3D-rekonstrukcióként". The Copyright section has a new line: the artworks, photographs, videos and texts are In Copyright, with a link to the rights statement (http://rightsstatements.org/vocab/InC/1.0/). The notice now carries its date, like the privacy notice. The reconstructor's name is written "Szilvia Baráth" / "Baráth Szilvia" with the accent in the legal notice and everywhere else a visitor reads it: the footer, the credits, the Contact panel, `digitalBy` in `exhibition.json`, and the README. Left as they are: the copyright line in `LICENSE` ("Szilvia Barath"), which is the licence text and waits for the reconstructor's word, and the Hungarian "kérj engedélyt" in the Copyright section, a te form in legal text that was not part of the request. Decided by: reconstructor (what to change); assistant (the wording of the two sentences and of the new line, listed in `docs/accessibility/hu-confirm.md`). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- Touch controls text in full sentences (14 November 2026), in the Behind the scenes panel, the accessible version and on the loading screen, with the same content in both languages: the stick on the left walks and steps sideways, and runs when pushed all the way; a finger dragged across the view looks around; a two-finger pinch or the + and − buttons zoom; a tap opens a work; the buttons at the bottom start the tour, list the rooms and mute the sound. The old line was a list of fragments, and left out the sideways step, the run and the buttons. Written from what `src/controls.js` does on touch. Decided by: reconstructor (full sentences, same content); assistant (the wording, Hungarian listed in `docs/accessibility/hu-confirm.md`). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- The guided tour starts at the first work (14 November 2026). Its opening stop where the visitor comes in, with the line "Down the stairs into the basement of MAMŰ: Artificial Paradise by KristofLab, 10-31 January 2025." / "Le a lépcsőn, a MAMŰ pincéjébe: …", is cut in both languages: the stop is gone from `buildTourStops` in `src/main.js` and `tourIntro` from the i18n files. Who wrote the line is not recorded (it has been in the i18n files since the Hungarian version was added on 23 October 2026). Decided by: reconstructor. Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- Whose English it is (14 November 2026): the English of the opening speech and of Human as Error was supplied by the artist, in the same Google Docs as the Hungarian. It is not a translation made for this site. The notes a visitor reads under the two English texts (viewer, About panel, accessible version) said "Translated from the Hungarian original for the digital exhibition." and "Translated from the artist's Hungarian text for the digital exhibition."; both now read "English text supplied by the artist, with the Hungarian original." The line in the status section above, the `_readme` of `en.json` and the question for the artist under "Next" are corrected or removed to match. The texts themselves are unchanged. Who made the English is not recorded. Decided by: artist (reported by the reconstructor, 14 November 2026); assistant (the wording of the note). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- A1: inside the digital exhibition the navbar and the footer sit on a steady dark band, `rgba(11,12,14,.8)`, in place of the gradient that faded to nothing (0.6 to 0), in `src/style.css`. Footer text is `#c9c9c6` (it was `--muted`, `#9a9a98`); the navbar buttons and both language buttons are at full strength inside (they were at 0.75). Because strength no longer marks the navbar button of the open panel, that button is underlined, like the chosen language, and so is a button under the mouse. Measured in a screenshot at 1366 × 768, facing the white brick of Room 1's south wall: navbar 11.0 to 13.6:1 (About 11.04, Works 11.37; they were 2.18 and 2.31), footer 7.7 to 8.9:1 ("Digital exhibition by…" 7.72, Contact 8.32; they were 1.65 and 2.00). Over pure white the band gives 9.7 for the navbar and 6.6 for the footer. The title screen and a panel opened from it are unchanged. The focus ring on a navbar button and on a footer link was looked at again in screenshots: a white ring on a `#0b0c0e` backing, clear on the band. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- A2: the room name and its subtitle (`#room-label`) sit on the same glass chip as the toolbar (`--glass`, a 1 px `--line` edge, padding 8 × 12 px, 6 × 10 px in a window up to 820 px wide), where they used to sit straight on the wall with a text shadow, in `src/style.css`. The subtitle is `#c9c9c6` (it was `#9a9a98`) with a line height of 1.5, and the chip has a `max-width` that ends 8 px before the map, so a long subtitle wraps beside the map on a phone and no longer runs under it (at 375 × 667 "Festmények · Paradise Factory" takes two lines). Measured in screenshots facing the white brick of Room 1, against the lightest pixel behind each line, with the glass still at 0.72: at 1366 × 768 the name 10.71:1 and the subtitle 6.95:1; at 375 × 667 the name 7.73 and the subtitle 5.10 (on the wall they were 1.13 and 1.41 to 3.90). In Room 4: 17.42 and 11.86. Over pure white the chip gives 6.9 and 4.7. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- A3: the glass under every chip over the 3D view (`--glass`: room label, map, toolbar, rooms menu, keycaps, wheel hint, prompt, tour caption, zoom level) goes from 0.72 to 0.85, and a new token `--hud-muted: #c4c3be` (the value in the proposal's token table) colours the keycap letters, the struck-through ♪ of the muted sound button and the wheel hint, in `src/style.css`. The keycap letters are 11 px (they were 9 px, `#9a9a98`); the drawing on each keycap sits 5 px lower so the larger letter does not touch it ("Shift" ran into its arrow), and the keycaps keep their size, so the zoom keys stay level with the arrow keys in English and Hungarian. Measured in a screenshot at 1366 × 768 facing the white brick of Room 1, against the lightest pixel behind each: keycap letters 8.45 to 9.24:1 (they were 3.6 to 3.8, and 2.8 over white), the wheel hint 8.89, the muted ♪ 8.75, toolbar labels 13.40, and the room label of A2 now 13.59 for the name and 9.13 for the subtitle. Over pure white the 0.85 glass gives 7.16 for `#c4c3be`, 11.18 for the text colour and 7.61 for `#c9c9c6`. Not changed, because the row does not name it: the subtitles in the rooms menu stay `#9a9a98`, which is 4.74:1 as measured and 4.48:1 over pure white. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- A4 (shapes and sizes): on the map (`src/ui/minimap.js`) the room numbers are 11 px at 85% white (they were 9 px at 40%); the number of the room the visitor stands in stays green. The works are 5 px marks with a 1 px dark edge (`rgba(11,12,14,.9)`, 7 px across in all), paintings and prints round and magenta, videos square and cyan (they were 3 px squares of either colour, told apart by hue alone). The numbers are drawn after the marks, so a work hung in a corner does not cover one. Measured in screenshots facing the white brick of Room 1, against the lightest pixel of the map's glass (0.85 since A3): room numbers 10.3:1 at 1366 × 768 and 10.0 at 375 × 667 (they were 3.08, and 2.5 over white); painting marks 4.47 and 4.34 (they were 2.30 to 3.36); video marks 9.29 and 9.03; against their own dark edge the marks are 5.7 and 11.8. Over pure white: numbers 9.6, paintings 4.15, videos 8.64. Not done here: the one-line key under the map, which needs a new string and comes with the later group. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- A5: the phone joystick (`#joystick`, `#joystick-knob` in `src/style.css`) has a 2 px ring at 70% white between two 1 px dark lines (`rgba(11,12,14,.85)`, drawn as an outer and an inner box shadow so they follow the circle in every browser), a fill of 0.5 (it was 0.35), and a knob at 85% white with a 1 px dark edge (it was white at 22% with a 50% white edge). Before, the ring was 1 px of white at 14%, about 1:1 on a light wall. Measured in a screenshot at 375 × 667 with the stick over the white brick of Room 1: the outer dark line against the wall 11.2:1, the ring against the dark lines 9.7 and 11.7, the ring against the fill 3.0, the fill against the wall 3.5, the knob against the fill 3.7 and against its own edge 11.3. Looked at in Room 4 too, where the light ring carries it. Size, position and behaviour are unchanged. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- A6: without JavaScript the notice now stands inside the title card, in the card's own blue on paper (`#072fcc` on `#fbfbf9`, 8.86:1, 12 px), as two paragraphs, English then Hungarian (`lang="hu"`), in `index.html` and `src/style.css`. It was red text (`#c8102e`) with no background, fixed 40 px above the bottom of the window, over the picture, and on a phone it overprinted the card and the footer. A `<noscript><style>` in the head hides what cannot work there: the progress line and its label, Enter, and also the line about headphones, the line about the keys and the card's second link to the accessible version (the notice already carries that link). The words are unchanged; the "·" that joined the two languages on one line is gone because each has its own paragraph. Looked at with JavaScript switched off at 1366 × 768 and at 375 × 667: the card holds the logo, the two meta lines and the notice, and nothing overlaps. With JavaScript the title screen is as before (no notice in the page, progress line shown). Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B1: the toolbar's words stay beside their glyphs at every width. In a window up to 820 px wide (phones, and laptops at 200%) the buttons showed only "▶", "⌖", "♪", and because the words were `display: none` those glyphs were the buttons' names too. The rule that hid `.lbl` is gone from `src/style.css`; at that width the buttons keep the 11 px letters with a padding of 11 × 9 px and a letter spacing of 0.08em, on one line. The three glyphs are `aria-hidden` in `index.html`, so the names are "Tour", "Rooms", "Sound" ("Tárlatvezetés", "Termek", "Hang") and the titles, with the keys, are their descriptions. Measured at 375 × 667 on touch: the row is 217 px wide in English and 284 px in Hungarian (buttons 129, 76 and 61 px wide, 36 px high), centred with 45 px free on each side, no wrapping and no sideways scroll; it clears the stick and the zoom buttons, which end 22 px above it. At 1366 × 768 nothing changes (245 and 317 px). Corrected in a second commit: the phone rule first matched every button inside the toolbar, so the rooms menu, which sits inside it, lost its line breaks at 375 px and grew to 1158 px wide; the rule now names the three buttons only, and the menu is 220 px wide again. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B2: the Tour button keeps its name while the tour runs. `syncTourBtn` in `src/main.js` used to swap the visible word to "Stop" / "Leállítás" while also setting `aria-pressed="true"`, so a screen reader said "Stop, toggle button, pressed". Now the word stays "Tour" / "Tárlatvezetés" (a `data-i18n` on the label in `index.html`), `aria-pressed` alone carries the state, and only the hidden glyph changes (▶ to ■, in green, on the lighter button background as before). Read from the accessibility tree with the tour running: button "Tour", pressed, and button "Tárlatvezetés", pressed. The string `hud.stop` had no other use and is removed from `en.json` and `hu.json`. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B4: the page has one h1, inside `main`, and a heading once the visitor is inside. `index.html` has a new h1 for screen readers only (`.sr-only`) as the first thing in `main#stage`, filled from the existing string `docTitle` ("Artificial Paradise: Digital Exhibition" / "Artificial Paradise digitális kiállítás"), so it follows the language. The title card's hidden heading, which was the only h1 and stood outside `main`, is now an h2 and still names the card. The room's name in the label over the 3D view (`.room-name`) is an h2 in place of a span, so heading navigation finds "Room 1" / "1. terem" and it changes with the room; `src/style.css` resets its margin and font. Read from the page: on the title screen h1 then the card's h2; inside (the card is inert) h1 then the room's h2. The label's box is the same before and after: 240.6 × 54 px with 15 px letters at 1366 × 768, 195 × 62 px with 13 px letters at 375 × 667. No new string. The title card's heading had been hard-coded English since before this pilot ("Artificial Paradise · KristofLab · Digital Exhibition"), so on the Hungarian page the card's region was named in English. It now reads "Artificial Paradise · KristofLab · Digital exhibition" / "Artificial Paradise · KristofLab · Digitális kiállítás": the last part reuses `loader.meta1`, the line the card already shows, and "Artificial Paradise" is marked `lang="en"`. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B8: each on-screen keycap is named once. The seven keycaps in `index.html` (W, Shift, ←, S, →, + and −) carried the same words as `aria-label` and as `title`, so the title became the description and NVDA read "Forward (↑ / W)" twice. The `aria-label` is gone, the letter on the keycap is `aria-hidden` like its drawing, and the title alone names the button: Chrome's accessibility tree now gives each a name and no description. The Shift keycap's name was an instruction ("Shift: run, click to switch on or off" / "Shift: futás, kattintásra be/ki"); it is now "Run (Shift)" / "Futás (Shift)" (`hud.keys.run`), and `aria-pressed`, which `src/ui/keypad.js` already kept, says whether running is on. Each name still holds the letter printed on its keycap, for speech input. Nothing changes on screen except the Shift keycap's tooltip. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B9: the zoom readout says what it does. `#zoom-level` in `index.html` was named by its text alone, "2.0× ✕", read as "2.0 times multiplication X", with "Reset zoom (0)" only as a title. It now starts with the existing string `hud.keys.zoomReset` as text for screen readers (`.sr-only`), the ✕ is `aria-hidden`, and the tooltip sits on the visible part of the button, where it is still shown under the mouse and is not read a second time as a description. Chrome's accessibility tree gives the name "Reset zoom (0) 2.0×" / "Nagyítás visszaállítása (0) 2.0×" and no description. `src/main.js` writes the number into `.val`, and `src/style.css` colours that span. On screen the button is the same, 67 × 28 px at 1366 × 768 and at 375 × 667. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B11: in the rooms menu each button is named by its room, and the room the visitor is in is marked. `buildRoomsMenu` in `src/ui/panels.js` used to put the works' titles inside the button, so the name of Room 3 was the room and seven titles in one breath. Each row is now the button ("Room 3") followed by the titles as a `small` outside it, tied to the button with `aria-describedby`; Chrome's accessibility tree gives the name "Room 3" and the titles as the description. Titles inside the button under an `aria-labelledby` would have failed the label-in-name check, which is why they moved out. In `src/style.css` the button's `::after` covers the whole row, so a click or a tap on the titles still chooses the room (tried with the mouse at 1366 × 768 and with a tap at 375 × 667), and the hover and the focus ring are drawn around the row. The button of the current room carries `aria-current="location"`; `updateRoom` in `src/main.js` passes the room on through the new `ui.setRoom`, so the mark moves as the visitor walks or jumps, and it is put back when the menu is rebuilt for the other language. On screen that row has a green name and a 2 px green bar on its left, like the green number on the map. The menu's size is unchanged: 220 × 350 px, rows of 85, 73, 121 and 61 px, titles at 9.2 px. No new string. On a touch screen the open menu lay over the stick (English, 375 × 667) or over the left 13 px of the zoom keys, hiding the "+" (Hungarian, where the wider toolbar moves the menu to x 108 to 328 and the keys sit at 315 to 361). While the menu is open, `src/style.css` now hides the stick and the zoom keys on touch screens (`visibility: hidden`), as the tour does; they come back when it closes. Checked at 375 × 667 in both languages and at 667 × 375; with a mouse the zoom keys stay. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B12: the hint under the sound question is read with it. The dialog `#sound-ask` in `index.html` was named by its question only, so "Better with headphones · M switches the sound on or off any time", the one place that mentions M before the visitor starts, was never read: focus goes straight to "Without sound". The hint now has an id and the dialog points to it with `aria-describedby`. Chrome's accessibility tree gives the dialog the name "Start with sound?" / "Hanggal induljon a kiállítás?" and the hint as its description, in both languages. Nothing changes on screen. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B13: the bars of the logotype are no longer read. In `index.html` the bars in "| Enter |" and in the brand "|Artificial Paradise|" were plain text, read as "vertical line". Each bar is now in an `aria-hidden` span. Chrome's accessibility tree gives the button the name "Enter" / "Belépés" (it was "| Enter |") and the brand reads "Artificial Paradise KristofLab". In `src/style.css` the phone rule that hides "KristofLab" in the navbar is narrowed from `.brand span` to `.brand > span`, or it would have hidden the new bars too. On screen nothing moves: Enter is 195.4 × 46 px at 1366 × 768 and 220.6 × 46 px in Hungarian at 375 × 667, the brand 239.4 and 157.1 px wide, before and after. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B14: the footer's Instagram link says what it opens, in both languages. In `index.html` the link read "Instagram ↗" in English whatever the language. Its text is now the existing string `panels.linkInstagram`, "Instagram post from the exhibition" / "Instagram-poszt a kiállításról", the same words as the link in the About panel, and the arrow is `aria-hidden`. The address is unchanged. In a window up to 760 px wide, where the footer already wraps to five or six lines over the foot of the title card, the full words would have added a line (the footer grew from 75 to 90 px in English at 375 px), so there the link shows only "Instagram" (the existing string `panels.contactLabel.instagram`, `aria-hidden`) and the full words stay as its name for screen readers, clipped from view in `src/style.css`. Chrome's accessibility tree gives the name "Instagram post from the exhibition" / "Instagram-poszt a kiállításról" at every width. Measured: the link is 245 px wide in English and 218 px in Hungarian at 1366 and 800 px, where the footer keeps its height (45 and 60 px); at 760, 375 and 320 px the link is 75 px and the footer is as tall as before (45, 75 or 105, 105 px). No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B15: the viewer's back button shows "← Previous" in English, where it showed "← Prev" against the name "Previous work" (`viewer.prev` in `en.json`, and the default in `index.html`). Speech software that matches the word on the button to its name can now find it. The Hungarian "← Előző" already sits inside "Előző mű" and is unchanged. Measured in the open viewer: the button is 107 px wide (it was 76 px); at 375 px the row of 343 px holds "← Previous" (16 to 123), the count (182 to 223) and "Next →" (283 to 359); at 320 px the three still stand apart (123, 155 to 196, 228); nothing wraps or scrolls sideways. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- C1: the hint "Drag the mouse to look around the room." goes away for keyboard users too, and it no longer sits on the work. It was retired only by a mouse drag. In `src/main.js` it now goes at the first look or move of any kind: a drag, a walking or turning key, the wheel, an on-screen key, Page Down or Page Up, a jump to a room or to a work, the start of the tour. Left alone, it goes 8 seconds after the sound question is answered. In `src/style.css` it is also out of the way while the tour runs or the rooms menu is open, and a retired hint is `visibility: hidden`, so a screen reader no longer finds it. It moved from 34% of the height (over the middle of the work) to 58 px below the crosshair, under the prompt that names a work: at 1366 × 768 it is at 442 to 474 px, the prompt at 406 to 433 px. In a window under 520 px high there is no room below (at 683 × 384 it would reach the toolbar, which starts at 270 px), so there it sits just above the crosshair, at 146 to 178 px. It now stays on one line down to a width of its text plus 32 px (the Hungarian line is 406 px wide and wrapped at 683 px before). Checked in the browser: each of arrow key, W, Page Down, wheel, keycap and T retires it; the timer retired it after 8.2 and 8.8 seconds. The words are unchanged. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- C2: the folded navbar menu (windows up to 760 px wide: phones, and a laptop at 200% zoom) works from the keyboard. In `index.html` the Menu button now comes before its links in the page; `order: 1` in `src/style.css` keeps it where it was on screen, right of EN / HU (at 375 px: languages at 254 to 315 px, the button at 323 to 359 px, as before). Tab after an open Menu now goes About, Works, Behind the scenes, Contact, EN, HU; before, it skipped the menu. In `src/boot.js` Esc closes the open menu and puts focus back on the Menu button (and that Esc does nothing else), and the menu closes when focus leaves the navbar for another control: Tab past HU lands on Enter with the menu closed, Shift+Tab from Menu lands on the skip link with it closed. A press that moves focus nowhere is still handled by the pointer listener, so a mouse click on a link of the menu still opens its panel. Tab order on the title screen: at 1366 px unchanged (skip link, About, Works, Behind the scenes, Contact, EN, HU, Enter, the card's link, the footer); at 375 px with the menu closed it is skip link, Menu, EN, HU, Enter, where it was EN, HU, Menu, so there Menu takes focus one place before the languages that stand left of it. A panel opened from the folded menu and closed with Esc puts focus on Enter, as before, because its opener is hidden by then. `tests/a11y/keyboard.mjs` passes 31 of 31 and its log is rewritten. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- C4: a panel opened during the tour stops the tour, as the viewer already did. In `src/main.js`, `onOpenPanel` calls `tour.stop()` when the tour is running. Before, the tour went on behind the panel and its captions were announced over it, and closing with × left it running. Checked in the browser, with About opened by a mouse click on the navbar and Works opened by keyboard, each during the tour: the Tour button reads as not pressed, the caption is gone, "Tour stopped." is the only thing announced in the 8 seconds after (longer than the 5 seconds the tour stays at a work), and after × the tour is still stopped. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- C9: during the tour the toolbar and its rooms menu stand above the caption. `#toolbar` in `src/style.css` has `z-index: 2`; the caption comes after it in the page and was drawn over it. Checked in the browser with the tour running, the rooms menu open and focus on "Room 4", at 1366 × 768, at 683 × 384 and on a 375 × 667 phone: the element on top at the middle of the focused row is the rooms menu, and at the middle of the Tour and Sound buttons it is the toolbar. At 1366 × 768 the caption (632 to 684 px) and the row "Room 4" (604 to 664 px) share 32 px, and the whole row with its focus ring now shows. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- E1: at 200% zoom the tour caption no longer covers the toolbar or a key. The caption stood a fixed 84 px (76 px in narrow windows) from the bottom while the toolbar rides on the footer, which wraps to two or three lines in a narrow window. In `src/style.css` the caption is now anchored 8 px above the toolbar: its `bottom` adds the footer's height (`--foot-h`), the toolbar's gap and the toolbar's own height, a new `--bar-h` that `src/main.js` keeps with a `ResizeObserver`, as `boot.js` keeps `--foot-h`. On touch screens it follows the toolbar the same way, and in windows up to 820 px wide it stays above the joystick as before (372 to 457 px on a 375 × 667 phone, unchanged). The walking keycaps are hidden while the tour runs (`visibility: hidden`, so Tab does not reach them; the arrow keys, Esc and the Tour button still stop the tour) and in any window under 520 px high. What I did beyond the row: the two zoom keys are kept, because zoom works during the tour and a mouse has no other zoom without the keyboard. Under 520 px high they take the walking keys' corner (18 px from the left, bottom on the toolbar's line), and during the tour they are hidden only in windows up to 1180 px wide, where the 640 px caption would lie over them. Measured with the tallest caption of the tour (69 px, two lines) in English and Hungarian: at 1366 × 768 the caption is at 598 to 667 px and the toolbar starts at 675 px (before, the caption ended at 684 px, 9 px into the toolbar); at 683 × 384 with device pixel ratio 2 (200% zoom) the caption is at 208 to 277 px and the toolbar at 285 px in English, 193 to 262 px and 270 px in Hungarian, where the footer is 60 px high. In every case no button of the toolbar or zoom key that can take focus overlaps the caption. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- E3: at 200% zoom the title screen shows Enter. At 683 × 384 the logo was 470 × 259 px, two thirds of the window, and Enter, which has focus, stood at 476 to 522 px, below a footer that starts at 339 px. In `src/style.css` the logo is now capped by height as well as width, `max-height: max(96px, 34vh)`: 237 × 131 px at 683 × 384, 231 × 128 px on a phone on its side (667 × 375), 370 × 204 px at 1024 × 600, and unchanged from 762 px of height up (468 × 258 px at 1366 × 768) and on an upright phone, where the width already limits it (303 × 167 px at 375 × 667). The cap alone does not bring Enter into a 384 px window, so in `src/main.js`, when loading is done and Enter takes focus, `showEnter()` sets the scroll position of the title card's own scroller (`#loader`, never the page) in one step so that the card's last line, the link to the accessible version, ends 12 px above the footer, as long as Enter stays at least 12 px under the navbar. The first version scrolled only far enough for Enter, which left the lines under it beneath the footer, with the blue text showing faintly through the footer's 92% white (reviewer's finding: at 1024 × 600 the link was at 567 to 581 px over a footer at 555 px; at 375 × 667 in Hungarian at 531 to 575 over 516). Now the scroll is 38 px at 1024 × 600 in English (52 in Hungarian), 167 px at 683 × 384 in English (207 in Hungarian), 71 px at 375 × 667 in Hungarian (30 in English) and 187 / 227 px at 667 × 375, and in all of these the link ends 12 px above the footer (at 683 × 384 in English: Enter 181 to 227 px, the link 300 to 314, the footer from 326). At 1366 × 768 nothing is scrolled. At 683 × 384 the top of the logo is then cut by the navbar, which is the sign that the card scrolls; the navbar still names the exhibition. The navbar and the footer are now opaque on the title screen (`#fbfbf9`, the card's white, in place of 92% of it), so nothing on the card shows through them.
