No smooth scroll, no transition. `#loader` also has `scroll-padding` for the navbar and the footer, so a control that Tab scrolls to is not left under either band. Stillness, checked at five window sizes in both languages: two screenshots 1.3 seconds apart are identical, no animation runs in the loader, and the page's own scroll stays 0. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- D1: on a phone the page can be pinch-zoomed again: the title screen, the panels, the sound question and the viewer with its text. `touch-action: none` sat on `html, body` in `src/style.css`, so it blocked page zoom everywhere. It now sits only on the 3D view (`#scene`) and on the layer of controls over it (`#hud`: the joystick, the zoom keys, the toolbar, the map), where a drag still looks round and a pinch still zooms the camera. Once the page is zoomed in, a finger on the 3D view would have no way to zoom it back out, so `src/main.js` watches `visualViewport` and sets `page-zoomed` on the body while the page scale is above 1.01. While it is set the 3D view and its layer take `touch-action: auto`, so two fingers can zoom the page back out, and `src/controls.js` stops turning the camera and stops the camera pinch, so the same gesture does not do both. The joystick and the keycaps keep `touch-action: none` throughout. What I added beyond the row: in `src/ui/viewer.js` a swipe on the image changes the work only with one finger and only while the page is not zoomed; otherwise moving round an enlarged image would have jumped to the next work. Checked in Chromium's phone emulation only (375 × 667 and 667 × 375, touch, English and Hungarian), with touches sent through the DevTools protocol, never on a real phone. Before the change a two-finger spread from 60 to 200 px left the page at scale 1 on the title screen, on the About panel and in the viewer. After it the same spread gives scale 2.88 on all three, and the reverse brings it back to 1. On the 3D view the same spread leaves the page at 1 and zooms the camera from 1 to 2.79; a drag of 120 by 80 px turns the view by 0.66 rad with the page at 1, no scroll and no offset; the joystick held up walks 0.94 to 1.07 m; the zoom key steps the camera to 1.25. With the page zoomed to 2.88, a drag over the 3D view pans the page and turns nothing, a swipe on the viewer's image keeps the same work, and a pinch over the 3D view brings the page back to 1 and leaves the camera at 1; after that the view zooms the camera again (2.79). No console errors. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- D3: the folded navbar menu (windows up to 760 px wide) now holds, below a line, what the footer holds: the accessible version (a link to `text/`), Accessibility, Legal notice and Privacy. Inside the exhibition on a phone the footer is put away, so before this none of the four could be reached there. In `index.html` they sit in a new `div.nav-more` at the end of `#nav-links`, from the existing strings `textEdition.nameFull`, `nav.accessibility`, `nav.legal` and `nav.privacy`, with the link's address from `textEdition.href` (`./text/?lang=en`, `./text/hu/?lang=hu`). In `src/style.css` the block is hidden in a wide window, where the footer shows; in the folded menu it has a 1 px line above it (blue at 35% on the title screen, white at 30% inside) and its link looks like the buttons. The menu now has a `max-height` of the window less the navbar and scrolls inside itself in a short window. `src/boot.js` closes the menu when its link is followed, as it does for the buttons. Measured at 375 × 667 and 667 × 375 with touch, in both languages, on the title screen and inside: eight rows of 38 px, the menu 273 px wide in English and 348 px in Hungarian (the longest row is "Akadálymentes változat (szöveges formátum)"), from 44 to 367 px; on a phone on its side it just fits a 375 px window, and in a 320 px window it scrolls (266 of 321 px shown) and Tab brings Privacy into view. Tab from Menu goes About, Works, Behind the scenes, Contact, the accessible version, Accessibility, Legal notice, Privacy, EN, HU. A tap on Legal notice opens its panel and closes the menu. At 1366 × 768 nothing changes: the block is `display: none`. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- D4: in a window up to 760 px wide the footer's links and buttons are at least 24 px tall (WCAG 2.5.8). They were 13 px tall, on rows 15 px apart. In `src/style.css` each one is now an `inline-flex` box with `min-height: 24px`, its text in the middle; a gap of 0.35em keeps "Instagram" apart from its arrow, which the flex box would otherwise have joined. The rows of the footer grow with them, and what sits above the footer follows its real height (`--foot-h`). Measured on the title screen, English and Hungarian: at 375 × 667 the footer goes from 75 to 110 px in English and from 105 to 151 px in Hungarian, and its top (557 and 516 px) stays below Enter (which ends at 439 px); at 320 × 568 from 105 to 140 px; at 667 × 375 (a phone on its side) from 45 to 58 px and from 60 to 84 px, and at 683 × 384 (a laptop at 200%) from 45 to 58 px and from 60 to 84 px. In the short windows the title card scrolls a little further so that Enter still ends 12 px above the footer (E3): 84 and 110 px of scroll at 667 × 375, 80 and 106 px at 683 × 384. The smallest target is now 47.6 × 24 px ("Contact"). At 1366 × 768 nothing changes (the footer stays 45 px). Inside the exhibition on a phone the footer is still put away; its links are in the folded menu (D3). No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- D5: on a touch screen every control of the interface is at least 44 × 44 px, the size advised for a thumb (WCAG 2.5.5). A new block at the end of `src/style.css`, under `@media (pointer: coarse)`, sets the sizes; with a mouse nothing changes. Measured at 375 × 667 and 667 × 375 with touch, in both languages, before and after: the toolbar's Tour, Rooms and Sound go from 36 to 44 px high (the toolbar from 46 to 54 px; at 375 px it is 217 px wide in English and 284 px in Hungarian, as before); Menu from 36 × 36 to 44 × 44; EN and HU from 27 × 30 to 44 × 44; Close on the panels and the viewer from 33.9 × 36 to 44 × 44; the rows of the folded menu from 38 to 44 px; the sound question's two buttons from 40 to 44 px; the viewer's Previous, Next, Walk to this work and the in-situ button from 34 to 44 px; "Exhibition text" from 14 to 44 px; the zoom readout to 44 px; the title card's link to the accessible version from 28 to 44 px (8 px above and below; it sits below Enter, which does not move). The link to the artist's page keeps its thin underline and its 15 px line; an invisible extension of 15 px above and below makes it 45 px to a finger (checked with a hit test 13 px above and below it). The rows of the rooms menu were already 61 to 121 px high. Left as they are: the footer's links at 24 px (D4), since the footer wraps over the foot of the title card and is put away inside the exhibition on a phone. On a phone on its side the folded menu (8 rows of 44 px) is now taller than the window and scrolls inside itself (D3). No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- D6: on a phone the tour caption no longer lies over the work or the map. In windows up to 820 px wide `src/style.css` had put the touch caption 210 px from the bottom, to stay above the stick: at 375 × 667 that was 371 to 457 px, over the middle of the work, and at 667 × 375 it was 96 to 165 px, over the room label and the map (worked out from the old rule and the tallest caption). Now the stick is put away while the tour runs (`visibility: hidden`; it was at 25% opacity), and the caption takes the place it has on desktop, 8 px above the toolbar. Upright, the caption spans the screen and would lie over the zoom keys, so they are put away during the tour as well; two fingers still zoom. On a phone on its side the caption is narrower, the width less 72 px on each side (523 px at 667 px), so it stays clear of the zoom keys, which stay. The Tour button stops the tour, as before. Measured with every caption of the tour in English and Hungarian: at 375 × 667 the tallest (86 px) is at 504 to 589 px, above the toolbar (597 px) and below the map (152 px); at 667 × 375 the tallest (69 px) is at 228 to 297 px, from 72 to 595 px across, clear of the toolbar (305 px), the map and room label (152 and 97 px) and the zoom keys (from 607 px). None of them overlaps another control. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- D7: the rooms menu stays on screen in a short window. It opens upwards from the toolbar and was 348 px tall, so at 667 × 375 (a phone on its side) it reached from 300 px up to -50 px: Room 1 was off the top and could not be tapped, and Tab put focus on a row nobody could see. At 683 × 384 (a laptop at 200%) it began at -83 px in English and -109 px in Hungarian, where Room 2 was hidden too. In `src/style.css` the menu now has a `max-height` of the room between the navbar and the toolbar, less 8 px (worked out from `--nav-h`, `--bar-h`, `--foot-h` and the safe area, with the touch toolbar's own offset on touch screens), and scrolls inside itself. In a window under 520 px high the titles under each room are left out of view (`display: none`); Chrome still reads them as each room's description, for example "1. terem" with "Wrong Data Landscape Dresden, Human as Error VI, …". Each row there is 34 px with a mouse and 44 px on a touch screen. Measured after, in English and Hungarian: at 667 × 375 with touch the menu is 186 px, from 114 to 300 px, rows of 44 px, Room 1 at 119 px and a tap on it reaches its button; at 683 × 384 it is 146 px, from 121 px (95 px in Hungarian); at 900 × 530 the full menu with titles fits (67 to 417 px); at 375 × 667 and 1366 × 768 nothing changes (242 to 592 px and 320 to 670 px). In all of these Tab through the four rooms keeps each focused row in view. At 400% (342 × 192) there is still no room for the menu between the navbar and the toolbar; that belongs to the compact controls of E2. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- D9: in the viewer the text of a work starts at its title. On a phone the text column (`.viewer-info`, the lower half of the viewer) kept its scroll position, so after Next the new title was off screen, and a work opened later from the map or the works list opened where the last one had been left. In `src/ui/viewer.js` the column now goes back to the top when a work opens, after Previous or Next by tap or click, and after a swipe on the image. By keyboard (Enter or Space on Previous or Next) the column stays where it is, so the focused button is not scrolled away; if the new text is shorter, the column moves just enough to keep that button in view. The arrow keys, which change the work without moving focus, do not scroll it. Measured at 375 × 667 and 667 × 375 with touch and at 1366 × 768, in English and Hungarian, after scrolling the column to the bottom: a tap on Next used to leave it at 425 to 1052 px of scroll with the new title 2 to 790 px above its top; now it is at 0 with the title at 423 px (375 × 667), 262 px (667 × 375) and 121 px (1366 × 768). Previous by tap and opening another work behave the same (a reopened work was at 1023 to 1498 px of scroll, now 0). Next pressed by keyboard at 375 × 667 left the focused button out of view before; now it stays in view. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- D12: the left and right safe areas are used, so on a notched phone on its side nothing sits under the notch or the rounded corners. `src/style.css` has two new tokens, `--safe-l` and `--safe-r` (`env(safe-area-inset-left)` and `-right`, 0 where there is none), added to the side offsets: the skip link, the title card's scroller, the navbar and the footer, the folded menu, the keyboard focus frame of the 3D view, the room label (and its maximum width), the map, the stick, the walking and zoom keys (both their desktop and their touch places), the zoom readout, the Close buttons, the panels, the viewer's image and text column, and the narrower phone tour caption of D6. Checked in Chromium's phone emulation with the safe areas set through the DevTools protocol to 47 px left and right (a notched phone on its side), in English and Hungarian. At 667 × 375 the room label moves from 12 to 59 px from the left edge, the stick from 22 to 69 px, the brand from 16 to 63 px; the map from 10 to 57 px from the right edge, the zoom keys from 14 to 61 px, Menu and the Close buttons of the panel and the viewer from 16 to 63 px, the panel's text and the viewer's title from 16 to 63 px; the toolbar stays in the middle. At 932 × 430 the same, with the room label at 65 px and the map at 61 px. The tallest tour caption stays clear of the map and the zoom keys (207 to 276 px high, 119 to 548 px across, the zoom keys from 560 px). With no safe area (375 × 667 upright, and every desktop) nothing moves. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- F4: the title screen no longer says "Sound: headphones recommended" ("Hanggal: fejhallgatóval izgalmasabb"). The same advice comes again in the question after Enter, which keeps its line ("Better with headphones · M switches the sound on or off any time"). In `index.html` the paragraph `#enter-hint` is gone, and so is its selector in the `<noscript>` style; the key `loader.hint` is gone from `src/i18n/en.json` and `src/i18n/hu.json`. Nothing else pointed at it (no `aria-describedby`, no script). The title card is 28 px shorter in both languages (637 to 609 px in English and 651 to 623 px in Hungarian at 1366 × 768, 565 to 537 px at 375 × 667), and the line about the keys now starts 22 px below Enter. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- F5: three details that looked unfinished. The dates: a visitor read them in three forms ("10-31.01.2025", "2025. 01. 10-31." and "10–31 January 2025"). Now there is one, "10–31 January 2025" / "2025. január 10–31.", with an en dash. On the title card the date is no longer part of `loader.meta2`, which is now just "MAMŰ Gallery, Budapest" / "MAMŰ Galéria, Budapest"; `index.html` puts `meta.dates` after it in its own span, so the card shows the same string as the About panel and the accessible version. The span never breaks inside (`white-space: nowrap`), so a phone no longer shows "10-" / "31.01.2025". The About panel's top line (`src/ui/panels.js`) puts the date in the same kind of span, and `.eyebrow .date` does not break either: at 375 × 667 in Hungarian the line had wrapped as "2025. január 10–" / "31.", and now the whole date moves to the third line; in a window up to 480 px wide the date takes its own line and the dot before it is put away. At 481 px the line needs 360 px in English and 368 px in Hungarian and has 406 px, so it does not wrap from there up. The two exhibition text sheets on the walls of Room 1 and Room 2 (`wall.sheetMeta`, drawn on a canvas) now read "KristofLab · MAMŰ Gallery · 10–31 January 2025" / "KristofLab · MAMŰ Galéria · 2025. január 10–31."; the line is 328 and 326 px wide at 15 px, within the sheet's 440 px. The footer: where it wraps, the "·" at the end of the line (or the start of the next) is hidden. `src/boot.js` compares each dot's line with the items on either side whenever the footer or one of its items changes size (window width, language switch) and sets `at-wrap`, which `src/style.css` turns into `visibility: hidden`, so the dot keeps its place and the wrap never moves. Checked in both languages at 1366, 1280, 1024, 800 and 761 px (and after a language switch at 1366 and 1024): no line ends or starts with a dot; at 1366 the dot after Contact is hidden, at 1024 the one after "MAMŰ Gallery, Budapest, 2025". At 760 px and below the dots were already put away (D4). The sound button: the ♪ and the struck-through ♪ are replaced by two inline SVG icons of 14 × 14 px in `index.html`, a loudspeaker with two waves when the sound is on and with a cross when it is off, drawn in `currentColor` and hidden from screen readers (`aria-hidden` on their span, as before). Which one shows follows `aria-pressed`, the state the button already reports; the off icon keeps the muted colour `#c4c3be` and the on icon is `#f2f1ed`. Checked at 1366 × 768 and 375 × 667 with touch, in both languages. No new key; `loader.meta2` and `wall.sheetMeta` changed in both languages. Without JavaScript `boot.js` cannot mark the wraps, and at 1366 × 768 the first line ended in a lone "·" after Contact (at 1024 after the gallery). The `<noscript>` style in `index.html` now does what the phone layout does: no dots, 14 px between the items. Checked at 1366 and 1024 with JavaScript off. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- F6: the title screen says "could not start" only when the digital exhibition really cannot start, and never says "Ready" after it. `src/boot.js` listened to every script error and every unhandled promise on the page while loading, so any error, a texture that failed on a slow phone say, put up and announced "This browser could not start the digital exhibition (WebGL is needed)", and a second or two later the loading ended and "Ready" was announced over it. The two global listeners are gone. The failure now comes only from the start-up itself: `import('./main.js')` failing, which happens when the file cannot be loaded or when it stops while it runs (with no WebGL, three.js throws as it creates the renderer). The failure sets the page's state to `failed`, and `markReady()` in `src/main.js` returns at once in that state, so neither the end of loading nor the 12-second safety net can announce "Ready" after it. Checked at 1366 × 768 in English and Hungarian, recording the live region: with two errors thrown 0.8 and 0.9 s into loading, before the change it said the failure (at 6.1 s) and then "Ready" (8.3 s); now it says only "Ready" ("Kész"), and Enter works. With WebGL taken away (no `webgl` or `webgl2` context) it says the failure once, at 0.1 s, and still shows it with Enter disabled after 14.5 s, past the safety net. The loading of files goes on after a failure, so `manager.onProgress` in `src/main.js` also returns in the `failed` state: before that, a failure after the textures had started loading (a shader link error, checked by making `linkProgram` throw) was overwritten within seconds by "Hanging the works… 34/34" and a full bar. Now the failure line stays, with the bar at 0 and Enter disabled. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- F7: two small things that moved for the whole visit now move for a moment and then stay still (WCAG 2.2.2). The little mouse on the chip under the walking keys rolled its wheel every 1.8 s for as long as the page was open. In `src/style.css` the roll now runs three times (5.4 s) and stops, and it starts only when the visitor is in the room, once the sound question is answered: `startHintTimer()` in `src/main.js` sets a class `in-room` on the body, and the animation is tied to it. Before that, behind the sound question, the wheel is still. The visitor's dot on the map grew and shrank by 0.4 px all the time. `src/ui/minimap.js` now keeps it at 2.6 px and swells it three times over 3 s after a jump, up to 3.8 px (2.6 + 1.2 × sin²(π t)), so the eye finds it in the new place. A jump is a room from the rooms menu or the map, and a work reached by Page Down, Page Up or "Walk to this work" (`teleport()` and `gotoWork()` call `minimap.pulse()`). With reduced motion the dot never changes size and the wheel never rolls, as before. `tests/a11y/reduced-motion.mjs` has five new checks (18 in all, all pass): with reduced motion the wheel does not roll and the dot keeps 2.6 px before, during and after a jump; without it the wheel rolls exactly three times and has stopped by the end of the run, and the dot reaches 3.8 px after a jump and is 2.6 px before it and once the 3 s are over. The test draws the map itself in a loop for those 3 s (about 57,000 draws), since the headless browser renders about one frame a second; for this `window.__ap` also gives the map's draw function (`minimap`). No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- G1: the accessible version's structure. The accessibility statement's six parts (Status, What was tested and how, What works, Known gaps, Planned, Contact) were the terms of a description list (`dt`, with the paragraphs in `dd`), so a screen reader could not jump between them by heading. In `scripts/text-edition.mjs` each part is now an h3 followed by its paragraphs, as in the panel in the digital exhibition, under the section's h2; the unused `dl.stacked` styles are gone. The h1 stood in the `header`, outside `main`, so "skip to main" landed past the page's title. Now the header holds only the line with the links to the digital exhibition and the other language, and `main` opens before the h1: the h1, the introduction, the facts, the note on positions, the list "On this page" (a `nav` inside `main`) and all the sections. Each page now has 23 h3 headings, 17 for the works and 6 for the statement, in both languages. `tests/a11y/text-edition.mjs` counted every h3 as a work; it now counts `article > h3` (17), and it also checks that the one h1 is inside `main`. All 28 checks pass. Looked at in light mode at 1200 and 375 px in both languages. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- G2: on the Hungarian accessible version the English titles are marked as English in the 17 links into the digital exhibition ("1. mű, Wrong Data Landscape Dresden: megnyitás a digitális kiállításban") and in the subtitles of Room 1 ("Festmények · Paradise Factory") and Room 4 ("Let it Flow · videó"), so a screen reader pronounces them in English, as it already did in the headings. In `scripts/text-edition.mjs` the link text is built with a new helper, `withTitle()`, which puts the title into the string's `{title}` slot through the same `title()` helper as the headings (`<span lang="en">` on the Hungarian page, plain text on the English one). A second helper, `titled()`, finds the works' titles inside a room's subtitle, the longest first, and passes each through `title()`. The Hungarian page now has 47 `lang="en"` spans, 19 more than before (17 links and 2 subtitles); the English page is byte for byte the same as before. Left as they are: titles inside the artist's and the authors' texts on the Hungarian page ("Artificial Paradise" 5 times, "Natural Life" and "Let it Flow" twice each, "Paradise Factory" once), since those texts are not to be changed. All 28 checks of `tests/a11y/text-edition.mjs` pass. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- Keyboard check, steps 7.2 and 9.1: the two steps that failed now and then under software rendering (about 1 fps headless) now wait for what they check, not for a fixed time. The live region speaks from a timer (at most once a second), and at 1 fps that timer can fire seconds late, so a fixed wait sometimes ended before the words came: 7.2 ("Esc stops the tour") once missed "Tour stopped." after its 1.5 s wait, and 9.1 (the `?work=w11-artificial-paradise-i-print` link) twice heard nothing after 0.5 s and 1.8 s, although Enter then opened the right work. In `tests/a11y/keyboard.mjs` two helpers do the waiting with `page.waitForFunction`, up to 30 s each: `hear()` waits until the live region has said every expected phrase and then takes what it said, and `until()` waits for a state in the page. Step 7.2 waits for the Tour button to read as not pressed and for "Tour stopped."; step 9.1 waits for the sound question to be open with focus inside it before Enter answers it, then for "Room 3." and "No. 11, Artificial Paradise I. Press Enter to open.", then for the viewer to be open with a title. No expectation was weakened: the checks are the same as before, word for word, and if the words never come (or come with other text) the wait ends after 30 s and the step fails on what was heard. I checked that on a blank page: with "Tour resumed." in the live region in place of "Tour stopped.", `hear()` gave up after its time-out (set to 2 s for that try) and the 7.2 check came out false. No other step changed. Run on the current `dist/` on 15 November 2026: 31 of 31. Decided by: assistant (a test fix). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- Accessibility statement after group a (Part 2a): the statement in `src/i18n/en.json` and `hu.json` (`accessibility.sections`) now says what group a changed and what its checks of 15 November 2026 found. What was tested: the axe result is dated 15 November 2026 (34 states, no violations), with a clause that the contrast over the 3D view, which axe cannot judge, was measured in screenshots; the keyboard run is still 31 of 31, and the statement now says that one step passed only on a second run, because it ran out of time waiting for an announcement under software rendering (step 9.1, the link to a work; `findings.md` gives the details); reduced motion says what the 18 checks look at, where it only said that the setting was emulated; phone width adds the digital exhibition in Chromium's phone emulation at 375 × 667 and 667 × 375 with touch, where group a measured the touch sizes and the pinch-zoom. What works: one paragraph on the dark band and glass over the 3D view, with the measured contrast facing the white brick of Room 1 (the bar at the top, the footer, the room name, the toolbar, the keys and the floor plan's numbers at 5.1:1 or more, the lowest being the room subtitle at 375 × 667 in A2, measured while the glass was still 0.72; the grey titles in the rooms menu at 4.74:1, from A3); one on touch (controls at least 44 × 44 px except the footer's links at 24 px, from D4 and D5; pinch-zoom on the title screen, the panels and the viewer while the 3D view keeps its gestures, from D1; both only in phone emulation); a sentence that opening a panel stops the tour (C4); and the reduced-motion paragraph adds the mouse under the walking keys and the dot on the floor plan (F7). Known gaps: the line "the contrast of the small labels over the 3D view ... was not measured" was no longer true, so it now says what is still not measured: only the brightest wall was measured, the tour caption, the prompt that names a work, the drag hint and the zoom readout were not, the rooms menu's grey titles would be 4.48:1 over pure white (A3), and the exhibition text sheets drawn on the walls of Room 1 and Room 2 were not measured. "The map" is "the floor plan" / "alaprajz" in two places, the name the interface gives it. The update date stays "14 November 2026" until the merge; the legal and privacy sections are untouched. The Hungarian is written impersonal and listed in `docs/accessibility/hu-confirm.md` (22 strings, 10 of them from this change, each with a note on what is new). Decided by: reconstructor (to update the statement after each group); assistant (the wording). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- Fix after review, touch sizes in the statement (Part 2a): the statement said that on a touch screen "the controls" are at least 44 × 44 px except the footer's links at 24 px. D5 measured only buttons, and D4's 24 px holds only in a window up to 760 px wide. Measured on 15 November 2026 from `dist/` in Chromium with touch (files served through `page.route`, no server), English and Hungarian: at 375 × 667 the footer's links are 24 px tall, at 1024 × 768 13 px; the links in the text of the panels are 17 to 20 px tall per line at both widths (About 6, Contact 10, Accessibility 1; a link that wraps is two lines tall); every button in the panels is at least 44 px (Close 44 × 44, the Works rows 65 px, the About summary 48 to 72 px, the Legal notice and Privacy buttons in Contact 44 px). `accessibility.sections[2].p[5]` now names the buttons that were measured and gives the 760 px condition; a new last paragraph in Known gaps (`sections[3].p[8]`) names the tablet-width footer and the panel links. `docs/accessibility/findings.md` row 4.4 says the same. The Hungarian is in `hu-confirm.md`. Decided by: reviewer (the finding); assistant (the wording and the measurement). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- **Keyboard result in the statement (15 November 2026).** After the keyboard check stopped waiting a fixed time (steps 7.2 and 9.1 wait for the announcement), a run on a fresh build gave 31 of 31 at the first try. The statement's sentence that one step passed only on a second run is cut, in both languages: it described the old test, not the site. The history of the timing failures stays in `docs/accessibility/findings.md`. The keyboard log is from this run. Decided by: assistant (to keep the statement to the current result). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- LICENSE: the copyright line now has the name with its accent, "Copyright (c) 2026 Szilvia Baráth" in place of "Szilvia Barath". Everything else in `LICENSE` is unchanged, including its first line, which says that the MIT licence covers the source code only. `README.md` and `NOTICE` had no unaccented form of the name (the README already says "Szilvia Baráth"), so they are unchanged; no other file in the repository has it, apart from the entry of 14 November above, which records the old line. Decided by: reconstructor (14 November 2026). Not a reconstruction decision (the licence text).

- Legal notice, Hungarian only (15 November 2026): the last line of the Copyright section ("Szerzői jogok", `legal.sections[3].p[2]` in `src/i18n/hu.json`) no longer uses the te form. "Bármely részének felhasználásához kérj engedélyt." is now "Bármely részének felhasználásához engedély szükséges.", the reconstructor's own wording. The English line ("Please ask before reusing any of it.") is unchanged. The notice's date moves to the day of the change: "Last updated: 15 November 2026" / "Utolsó frissítés: 2026. november 15." (`legal.updated` in both files). Nothing else in the legal notice or the privacy notice changed. `docs/accessibility/hu-confirm.md` lists the two Hungarian strings, the first with a note that it is her wording, and `docs/accessibility/hu-address-forms.md` no longer says the legal notice has a te form left. Decided by: reconstructor (her wording, 15 November 2026). Not a reconstruction decision (legal text).

- MAMŰ's page on the exhibition is linked (15 November 2026): https://mamusociety.wordpress.com/2025/01/06/5963/ (checked that day: it answers, titled "ARTIFICAL PARADISE"), with the words "MAMŰ: the exhibition page" / "MAMŰ: a kiállítás oldala". The address is `meta.links.mamuPage` in `src/data/exhibition.json`, the words are `ui.panels.linkMamuPage` in `src/i18n/en.json` and `hu.json`. In the About panel (`src/ui/panels.js`) it is the second of the links under "The real exhibition", after Artkartell, and like the others it opens in a new tab (`target="_blank" rel="noopener"`). The accessible version had no real-exhibition links, so `scripts/text-edition.mjs` puts it in a paragraph of its own under the facts at the top (artist, venue, dates, curator), from the same data, as a plain link that opens in the same tab like every other link there; checked in `dist/text/index.html` and `dist/text/hu/index.html`, and `node tests/a11y/text-edition.mjs` passed 28 of 28 on a fresh build. `README.md`, which is in English only and had no list of links, has a line under Credits. The link only leads out: nothing is loaded from the page and nothing is stored, so the privacy notice is unchanged. Decided by: reconstructor. Not a reconstruction decision (a link).

- Data correction, which photographs the images of no. 16 come from (15 November 2026). `src/data/exhibition.json` gave Let it Flow `"photo": "DSC_0113"`, but neither of its two images (`img/w16-let-it-flow-a` and `-b`, the work's picture and its two installation views) is cut from DSC_0113: `scripts/crops.json` cuts them whole from "4 terem/_artificial paradise 04 - 01.jpg" and "04 - 03.jpg", and the crop from DSC_0113 (`w16-let-it-flow`, with its `-situ` file) was taken out in commit 973c383, when the work was rebuilt from the documentation. Checked on the files as well: at thumbnail size the two images match none of DSC_0112 to DSC_0117 (best normalised correlation 0.36), and a contact sheet shows other viewpoints. `"photo": "DSC_0113"` stays, since it is the installation photograph that shows the work in place and the one the river and the projection were measured on (`src/world/letItFlow.js`); a new field, `imageSources`, names the file each image is cut from. Every other work was checked for the same mismatch between `photo` and the sources in `crops.json`. One has it: no. 3, Paradise Factory, gives `"photo": "_01_panorama"`, while its picture and its two installation views come from "1 terem/_artificial paradise 01 - 02.jpg", "01 - 01.jpg" and "01 - 06.jpg"; it gets `imageSources` too. For the other works `photo` is the source of what the site shows: the crop itself (the screens of the videos, nos. 3, 6, 7, 8, 15 and 17, whose stills are cut from the photographs) or, for the paintings and prints shown in the artist's reproductions, the in-situ view (`-situ`), which was cut from that photograph before the reproductions came in (commit c84be34) and was kept. The `_readme` at the top of the file now says what `photo` and `imageSources` mean. No code reads `photo` or the new field, so nothing a visitor sees changes, in either language, and no image file was added or moved. Left as it is: `public/img/w03-paradise-factory-situ.webp` (28 KB, cut from `_01_panorama` on 23 October 2026), which the site never shows, because no. 3 has its own list of installation views. Decided by: reconstructor. Not a reconstruction decision (a data correction).

- C3: the sound question now lies over the navbar, and nothing behind it opens a panel. Before, the navbar (z-index 70) stood above the question (60), so a mouse could open About from it while the question was on screen, and closing that panel unlocked walking under the question. Reproduced on a build of the branch at 1280 × 800: after Enter, the element under the middle of About was the navbar button, a click there opened the panel, and after its Close the question was still open while `controls.enabled` was true. In `src/style.css` `.sound-ask` has z-index 75, above the navbar and below the skip link (90); the navbar is dimmed under the question like the rest of the page, and a click where it lies lands on the question's backdrop, which answers "without sound" as before. In `src/main.js` the click handler of the navbar and the footer returns at once while the question is open, so a click that reaches a navbar button anyway (a screen reader's) opens nothing, and `onClosePanel` sets `blockers.panel` to whether the question is still open, so a panel closed under it leaves walking and zoom locked. Tab could not reach the navbar before either: the question's focus trap (`focusDialog`) keeps Tab and Shift+Tab on its two buttons, and that is unchanged. `tests/a11y/focus-mouse.mjs` has four new steps after step 3: 3a, the element under About and under HU is the question; 3b, a click on About sent to the button itself opens no panel and focus stays in the question; 3c, three Tabs and three Shift+Tabs never leave the question; 3d, a panel opened and closed under the question (through `window.__ap.ui`, the only way left) leaves walking and zoom locked and the question open with focus. On the build before the change 3a, 3b and 3d failed; with it all 21 steps pass. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- C5: a mouse click on EN or HU while a panel or the viewer is open leaves focus in the dialog. Before, the navbar's click handler handed focus to the 3D view after a mouse click on a language button, also while a dialog was open, so focus went behind the dialog. Reproduced on a build of the branch at 1280 × 800: with About open and focus on its Close button, a click on HU put focus on the 3D view while the panel stayed open; the same with the viewer open. In `src/main.js` `focusScene()` now does nothing while a panel or the viewer is open, as it already did while the sound question was open. That alone would leave focus on the EN or HU button in the navbar, outside the dialog, since the browser focuses a button when the mouse presses it, so a `mousedown` listener on the navbar stops that press from moving focus while a panel or the viewer is open: focus stays on the control in the dialog that had it (the Close button after opening, or whatever the visitor had moved to). The language switch then rebuilds the panel's text and the viewer's full-video buttons; if the focused control was one of them it would be gone, so `refresh` in `src/ui/panels.js` and `src/ui/viewer.js` now calls the dialog's `rebuilt()`, which puts focus on the dialog's Close button when focus is no longer inside. Clicks on EN and HU with no dialog open still hand focus to the 3D view (step 9, unchanged). `tests/a11y/focus-mouse.mjs` has five new steps after step 9: 9a, About open with focus on Close, HU then EN clicked: focus stays on Close; 9b, focus on a link in the About text, HU clicked: the text is rebuilt and focus is on Close; 9c, the viewer open with focus on Next, HU then EN clicked: focus stays on Next; 9d, the viewer then closed with the mouse: focus on the 3D view, as before; 9e, the viewer open on a work with a full-video button, focus on that button (it is never pressed, so the Drive player does not load), HU clicked: the buttons are rebuilt, the viewer stays open and focus is on its Close button. Each waits for the page's language to change. On the build before the change 9a, 9b and 9c failed (focus on the 3D view); with it all 25 steps pass. Step 9e was added after review, since no step checked the viewer's `rebuilt()` (on Next, step 9c, the text is rebuilt but the button is not): with that call taken out of `viewer.refresh`, 9e fails (focus on body); with it, all 31 steps of the file pass. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- C8: after "Without sound" is clicked with the mouse, the 3D view gets focus without the white keyboard frame. The question puts focus on "Without sound" when it opens, from the page's code, and the browser then shows the keyboard frame (`:focus-visible`) on that button. A mouse press on a button that already has focus does not change that, and when the click handed focus to the 3D view the browser carried the frame on to it. Reproduced on a build of the branch at 1280 × 800, each time on a fresh page: "Without sound" clicked with the mouse left the frame on the view; "With sound" clicked did not (the press moved focus to it as a mouse focus); Enter, Space and Esc left the frame, as they should. Blurring the button inside the click handler, the first try, did not help: focus given by code from no focused element shows the frame too. What works is what `controls.js` does for the view: in `src/main.js` a `mousedown` listener on the question lets go of the button under the mouse if it already has focus, so the press focuses it again as a mouse focus, and the frame no longer follows it to the view. Safari, and Firefox on macOS, do not focus a button on a press, so there a press dragged off the button before release (no click) could leave focus on the page body while the question is open. Since the question itself can take focus (below), those browsers should put focus on it instead; not checked in them, since only Chromium runs here. As a guard, after the blur the listener waits for the mouse button to come up: if no click has closed the question and focus is outside it, focus goes back to the button. Keyboard answers are unchanged. After review, the same now holds for a click or tap anywhere outside the card, which also answers "without sound": the dimmed backdrop and, since C3, the strip where the navbar lies under the question (About, HU, and Menu on a phone). The backdrop could not take focus, so focus went to the view from no focused element and the frame showed there, with the mouse and with a tap. In `index.html` the question now has `tabindex="-1"`, so a press on its backdrop or on the card's text focuses the question itself as a mouse focus, and no frame follows to the view; it stays out of the Tab order. In `src/ui/dialog.js` the Tab trap treats focus on the dialog itself like focus outside it: Tab goes to the first button and Shift+Tab to the last. Without that, Shift+Tab from the question would have left it. The panels and the viewer cannot take focus themselves, so nothing changes for them. `tests/a11y/focus-mouse.mjs` has five new steps: 4a, after step 4 ("Without sound" clicked with the mouse), no frame on the view; and at the end, each on a fresh page (the walk's own page is closed first, so they load at full speed), 17, "With sound" clicked with the mouse: sound on, focus on the view, no frame; 18, the question opened by keyboard and Enter on "Without sound": the frame shows; 19, Tab to "With sound" and Space: sound on and the frame shows; 20, Enter and then "Without sound" clicked with the mouse: sound off, focus on the view, no frame. Step 20 is the reproduction above on its own page; 4a checks the same after the walk's earlier steps, so what it sees depends on them. On the build before the change 4a and 20 failed; with it all 30 steps pass. After review, six more steps, each on a fresh page: 21, the backdrop clicked with the mouse; 22 and 23, clicked where About and HU lie under the question (no panel opened, language unchanged); 24 and 25, at 375 × 667 with touch, the backdrop and the place of the Menu button tapped (the menu not opened); in each, sound off, focus on the view, no frame; 26, the card's text clicked: focus on the question, Shift+Tab goes to "With sound", and after another click Tab goes to "Without sound". Before the `tabindex` change 21 to 25 failed (the frame showed) and 26 failed (focus on body after the click); with the `dialog.js` change taken out, 26 failed (Shift+Tab left the question); with both, all 37 steps pass, and `keyboard.mjs` passes 31 of 31. Step 27, on a fresh page, stands in for a browser that moves no focus on a press (the page stops Chromium's own focusing on `mousedown`): a press on "Without sound" dragged onto the question's text and released there leaves the question open with focus back on "Without sound". Without the guard it failed (focus on body); with it all 38 steps pass. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B3: while a panel, the viewer or the sound question is open, the page behind it is inert: out of the browser's accessibility tree and out of reach of Tab, clicks and taps, so a screen reader's virtual cursor or swipe can no longer leave the dialog. Before, the Tab trap held but nothing else did. Reproduced on a build of the branch at 1280 × 800: with About open on the title screen, none of the skip link, `#stage`, `#loader` and `#site-foot` was inert, and the browser's accessibility tree still held the skip link, Enter and the footer; with the sound question open, the navbar was not inert, and HU, About, the 3D view and the toolbar were in the accessibility tree under the question (checked again on a build of `cba355a`). In `src/ui/dialog.js` each dialog now names what lies behind it: by default the skip link's `nav.skip-nav`, `#stage` (the 3D view, the toolbar, the joystick and the on-screen keys), `#loader` and `#site-foot`. `opened()` takes the opener first, then sets `inert` on those, then moves focus in; `closed()` clears `inert` first, then gives focus back, so an opener in the footer can take it again. The navbar stays usable behind a panel or the viewer (EN and HU, the other sections, the folded menu on a phone), as C5 needs. The sound question (`src/main.js`) adds `#site-nav`, which closes the gap group b left: EN, HU and the rest of the navbar under the question are no longer in the accessibility tree, so a screen reader cannot reach them. The module keeps one list of what it made inert and works it out again from every dialog still open each time one opens or closes, so dialogs that follow each other (the works list to the viewer, the viewer to a panel through the navbar, a panel to another through a link in it, a language switch while one is open, a work opened during the tour) never leave anything inert. It only wakes what it made inert: the title screen, inert for good once inside, stays so. `inert` does not stop a script: a script's `click()` on HU under the question still switches the language after the change (checked on the build of the change). B3 does not guard against that; no visitor can send such a click, since the button is out of the tree and the question covers it for the mouse and touch. The navbar's handler in `main.js` still ignores section buttons under the question (C3). `tests/a11y/focus-mouse.mjs` has six new steps, each on a fresh page, reading the accessibility tree through the DevTools protocol (Playwright's own snapshot does not leave inert content out): 28, About open by keyboard on the title screen: the four inert, the navbar not, the skip link, Enter and the footer gone from the tree, Works, HU and Close in it; after Esc nothing inert and focus on About; 29, the Legal notice opened from the footer: the footer inert while open, focus back on the footer's button after Esc; 30, the sound question: the navbar inert too, HU, About, the 3D view and the toolbar gone from the tree, its two buttons in it; after the answer only `#loader` inert; 31, Works, a work from it, About over the viewer, Contact, the Legal notice from its text, HU and EN, Esc: inert behind each, only `#loader` after; 32, the tour started and a work opened during it: the tour stops, inert behind the viewer, only `#loader` after Esc and focus on the view; 33, at 375 × 667 with touch, About from the folded menu: the joystick and the toolbar inert, the toolbar and the zoom buttons out of the accessibility tree, the menu still opens Works over it, only `#loader` after Close. (The step first also checked that a tap at the joystick lands on the panel. A review found that the panel covers the whole screen, so the tap lands on it with or without `inert`; that check was replaced by the tree check, which fails with `#stage` awake.) On the build before the change all six failed; with `inert` cleared after focus instead of before, 29 and 32 failed (focus left on a hidden Close button); with `#site-nav` left out for the sound question, 30 failed. With the change all 44 steps pass, and `keyboard.mjs` passes 31 of 31. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- C6: a work opened from the works list now leads back to the list. Closing its viewer with the Close button, Esc or a click beside the picture reopens the list with focus on the work last shown (after Next or Previous, the work they moved to), scrolled into view. Before, focus went to the navbar's Works button (by keyboard) or to the 3D view (by mouse), and the list and the place in it were gone. Reproduced on a build of the branch at 1280 × 800: Works, Enter on no. 9, Esc: no panel, focus on Works. In `src/main.js` `onOpenWork` reads the list's opener, closes the list without handing focus back (`ui.close({ refocus: false })`), and opens the viewer with that opener and a `back` callback; focus goes from the list straight into the viewer. In `src/ui/viewer.js` the visitor's own closes (Close, Esc, the click beside the picture) call `back` with the work last shown in place of handing focus back; "Walk to this work", the navbar, the tour and a work opened any other way (Page Down, a click in the 3D view, `?work=`) close as before. In `src/ui/panels.js` `works({ opener, work })` opens the list with focus on that work's row and `scrollIntoView({ block: 'nearest' })`, which keeps to the panel's scroll padding; no. 3's screen, which Next reaches but the list has no row for, falls to no. 3's row. The reopened list keeps the control that first opened it, so Esc there goes back to the Works button, as before. `src/ui/dialog.js` gives this its three small options: `opened({ opener, initial })`, `opener()` and `closed({ refocus })`. The panels' Esc listener is added before the viewer's, so the Esc that reopens the list does not also close it. `tests/a11y/focus-mouse.mjs` has seven new steps on a fresh page inside: 34, no. 16 clicked in the list, Close clicked: the list is back with focus on no. 16, in view; 35, no. 16, Next clicked, Esc: focus on no. 17, in view; 36, no. 3, Next to its screen, a click beside the picture: focus on no. 3; 37, Esc on the reopened list: focus on Works; 38, the reopened list closed with the mouse: focus on the 3D view, as for any panel; 39, "Walk to this work" from a work opened from the list: no list, focus on the view; 40, a work opened with Page Down and Enter and closed with Close, and one opened the way a click in the 3D view opens it (`viewer.open` with no options) and closed with Esc: no list, focus on the view. Steps 39 and 40 check what must not change and pass on the old build too; 34 to 38 fail there. In `tests/a11y/keyboard.mjs` step 5.1 now expects, after Esc, the list back with focus on no. 6 in place of focus on Works: that is the change asked for. The old expectation moves to the new step 5.2, a second Esc: the list closes and focus is on Works. 5.3: Enter on Works, Tab to no. 14, Enter, → to no. 15, Enter on Close: focus on no. 15's row, in view. On the build before the change 5.1, 5.2 and 5.3 fail; with `scrollIntoView` taken out, 34, 35 and 5.3 fail (the row is below the fold). With the change all 51 steps of `focus-mouse.mjs` pass, and `keyboard.mjs` passes 33 of 33; its log is from that run. The accessibility statement still gives the old counts (31 keyboard steps, 37 mouse steps) and is left for its own update. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- C7: in a narrow window, closing a panel opened from the folded menu puts focus on the Menu button. The menu shuts when one of its items opens a panel, so on closing the item that opened it is hidden, and focus went to the fallback: Enter on the title screen, the 3D view inside. Reproduced on a build of the branch at 375 × 667 with touch: Menu, About and Esc by keyboard on the title screen left focus on Enter; inside, Menu, About and Close tapped left it on the 3D view. In `closed()` in `src/ui/dialog.js`, when the opener can no longer take focus, the dialog now looks for a shown button that opens a menu holding it (`aria-expanded` with `aria-controls` naming an ancestor of the opener, here the Menu button and `#nav-links`) and gives focus to that, before the fallback. In a wide window the navbar's links are shown, so focus goes back to them as before; the Menu button is not shown there and is never chosen. A close with the mouse inside the digital exhibition still sends focus to the 3D view, as for any panel, so the letter keys keep working; taps, like keys, now go to Menu. With C6 the works list reopened after the viewer keeps the hidden Works item as its opener, so Esc there also lands on Menu. `tests/a11y/focus-mouse.mjs` has four new steps at 375 × 667 with touch, on a fresh page: 41, title screen, Menu, Tab to About, Enter, Esc: focus on Menu; 42, Menu, Tab to Legal notice under the menu's line, Enter, Esc: focus on Menu; 43, inside, Menu, About and Close tapped: focus on Menu; 44, Works from the menu, no. 8 and Close tapped (the list is back on no. 8), Esc: focus on Menu. On the build before the change all four failed (focus on Enter, then on the 3D view); with it all 55 steps pass, and `keyboard.mjs`, which runs at 1366 × 768, passes 33 of 33 with its steps unchanged. A review found that C6 and C7 together broke one path on a phone: on the title screen, Menu, Works, a work and "Walk to this work" closed the viewer onto the Menu button (the list's hidden Works item stood in by it), `enter()` then opened the sound question with Menu as its opener, and once the question was answered focus went to Menu, not the 3D view, by tap and by keyboard. In a wide window the same path by keyboard left focus on the Works button; that was so before group c as well, as far as the code shows. `enter()` in `src/main.js` now opens the question with no opener (`soundDialog.opened({ opener: null })`), so on answering focus always goes on to the 3D view. Two new steps in `tests/a11y/focus-mouse.mjs`, each on fresh pages: 45, at 375 × 667 with touch, that path by tap and by keyboard: focus on the view; 46, at 1280 × 800, the path by keyboard: focus on the view. Without the change both fail (focus on Menu, on Works); with it all 57 steps pass. No new string. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B5: Previous and Next in the viewer now go through the 17 works and pass by no. 3's small screen, the counter says "No. 3 of 17", and the new work is said. Before, `step` in `src/ui/viewer.js` went through every entry of `exhibition.json`, the screen (`w03-screen`) included, so Next from no. 3 showed Paradise Factory again, the counter read "4 / 18" there, and nothing was said when the work changed. Reproduced on a build of the branch at 1280 × 800, in EN and HU: the viewer on no. 3 read "3 / 18"; Next showed "Paradise Factory | 4 / 18", the next one "5 / 18". In `viewer.js` Previous, Next, ← → and the swipe step through the works without the screen (`isPart` from `src/world/route.js`, the rule the tour and Page Down use); the screen can still be opened on its own (a click on it in the 3D view) and then counts as no. 3, so Next goes on to no. 4 and Previous to no. 2. The counter is built in `renderInfo`, so a language switch while the viewer is open updates it. Each step says the new work on its own topic of the live region (`announce(…, 'viewer')`), so a work in front said at the same moment (Page Down, then at once Enter and →) is not replaced by it. When the viewer closes, a work stepped to and not yet said is dropped from the queue (`unannounce('viewer')` in `close()`), so → and at once Esc do not say a work that is no longer on screen (added after review: before, the live region said "Human as Error III. No. 4 of 17." after the viewer had closed). C6 still works: a work opened from the works list leads back to the list on the work last shown; the screen can no longer be reached from the list, and if the page's code shows it last, the list's fallback to no. 3's row still holds. New strings: `ui.viewer.count` "No. {no} of {total}" / "{no}. mű a {total} közül"; `ui.announce.viewerWork` "{title}. No. {no} of {total}." / "{title}. {no}. mű a {total} közül." (the title first, as the most useful word). New check `tests/a11y/names.mjs` (what a screen reader hears), steps B5.1 to B5.7: no. 3 and Next, Previous; the screen opened on its own; round past no. 17 and back; Page Down, Enter and → at once: both the work in front and "Human as Error VI. No. 2 of 17." said; Next clicked: "Paradise Factory. No. 3 of 17." said; HU and back while the viewer shows: "3. mű a 17 közül", then "No. 3 of 17"; B5.7, → and Esc in one go: the viewer closes and "Human as Error III. No. 4 of 17." is not said (it fails without the `unannounce`). With the old `viewer.js` all six earlier steps fail; with the message put on the work topic, B5.4 fails. In `tests/a11y/focus-mouse.mjs` step 36 changes on purpose: Next from no. 3 now goes to no. 4, so the list reopens on no. 4 (before: through the screen, back to no. 3); the new step 36a shows the screen last (through `window.__ap`, with the way back to the list) and closes it: the list reopens on no. 3. All 58 steps of `focus-mouse.mjs` pass. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal, the wording and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B6: on the Hungarian page the English work titles now carry `lang="en"` in the viewer, the works list, the rooms menu and the live region, as the accessible version marks them (`<span lang="en">` in `scripts/text-edition.mjs`), so a screen reader reads them with English sounds. Before, nothing in the digital exhibition marked them. Reproduced on a build of the branch at 1280 × 800 with `?lang=hu`: the viewer's title, the works list's `<em>` and the rooms menu's line of titles had no `lang`, and the live region said "1. terem. Festmények, Paradise Factory." as plain Hungarian text. Which titles are English was decided per title from the data: all 17 titles in `src/data/exhibition.json` are the artist's own English titles (Wiena Wrong Landscape too, the artist's spelling), and `src/i18n/hu.json` gives none of them a Hungarian title, so all 17 are marked. The rule is in `titleLang` in `src/i18n.js`: a title is marked on a page in another language than English unless that language's file gives the work a title of its own (`works.<id>.title`), so a Hungarian title added later would not be marked. The accessible version marks every title on its Hungarian page without that test; with today's data the two give the same result. `src/ui/viewer.js` sets or removes `lang` on `#viewer-title` in `renderInfo`; `src/ui/panels.js` writes `<em lang="en">` in the works list and each title of the rooms menu in a `<span lang="en">`; both are rebuilt on a language switch. For the live region, `src/ui/announce.js` builds each message as nodes and puts every English title it finds in a `<span lang="en">` (longest first, so "Human as Error III" is not read as "Human as Error I" and "II"); `src/main.js` gives it the list of titles at start and again after a language switch, so this covers every message: the work in front, a room's subtitle (for example "4. terem. Let it Flow, videó."), the tour's captions and the viewer's Previous and Next. Not marked, since the row does not name them: the image's alt text (a title and a Hungarian medium in one attribute, which cannot be split), the line naming the work in front on screen, the room's subtitle on screen, the tour caption on screen and the labels on the walls. No new string. `tests/a11y/names.mjs` has four new steps: B6.1, Hungarian page: the viewer's title, 17 of 17 titles in the works list and 17 of 17 in the rooms menu marked; B6.2, switched to English while the list shows, then the viewer: no title marked, back in Hungarian the viewer's title marked again; B6.3, Hungarian page, Page Down to no. 5 and no. 6, a jump to Room 4 and Next from no. 16: each title in a `<span lang="en">`, the Hungarian words outside it; B6.4, English page: no mark in the live region or on the viewer's title. With the source files of the change taken out, B6.1 to B6.3 fail; with it all 11 steps of the file pass. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal, the wording and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B7: the 3D view's accessible name (`ui.sceneLabel`, and the same English in `index.html` before the language file is read) is shorter and names the keys that matter, Page Down and Page Up first. Before, it said "3D view of the digital exhibition: walk with the arrow keys, press Enter to open the work in front of you. The accessible version, the first link on the page, lists every work and all the keys." (37 words, read again each time focus comes back to the view) and left out Page Down and Page Up. Reproduced on a build of the branch at 1280 × 800 through the browser's accessibility tree (DevTools protocol): that name in English, and 30 words in Hungarian, neither naming Page Down. New, EN: "3D view. Page Down and Page Up go from work to work, Enter opens the work in front, the arrow keys walk and turn. All keys are in the accessible version, the first link." (34 words); HU: "3D nézet. A Page Down és a Page Up műről műre visz, az Enter megnyitja a nézet közepén lévő művet, a nyílbillentyűkkel lehet sétálni és fordulni. Minden billentyű leírása megtalálható az akadálymentes változatban, ahová az oldal első hivatkozása visz." (Hungarian corrected after review; before: "… az akadálymentes változatban van, ez az oldal első hivatkozása.") The proposal's wording said "arrow keys walk"; the left and right arrows turn, so it says "walk and turn". Checked that it is so: the first link in the page, and the first Tab stop, is the skip link to the accessible version (`./text/`), which lists every key (`panels.controlsDesk`, its "Controls" section); Page Down twice and Enter open no. 2. If a Help or Keys panel comes with F1, the last sentence may point to it in place of the accessible version. `tests/a11y/names.mjs` has three new steps: B7.1, the name read from the accessibility tree is the one in `en.json`, starts "3D view.", names Page Down, Page Up, Enter, the arrow keys, the accessible version and the first link, and is shorter than the old 37 words; B7.2, what it says is so (the first link and first Tab stop, Page Down twice and Enter); B7.3, switched to Hungarian, the name is the Hungarian one. With the old strings B7.1 and B7.3 fail. `tests/a11y/keyboard.mjs` passes 33 of 33 with its steps unchanged (step 3.3 still checks for "3D view", "accessible version" and "keys"); its log, `docs/accessibility/keyboard-test-log.md`, changes in steps 3.2 and 3.3, which quote the name, and is committed with this entry. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal, the wording and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B10: the floor plan's accessible name now says which room you are in, "Floor plan: you are in Room 2", and changes with each room and each language switch; the jump by a click stays in its title only. Before, its name and its title were both "Floor plan: click a room to go there" ("Alaprajz: egy teremre kattintva automatikusan oda lehet jutni"), which a screen reader cannot do, and said nothing of where you are. Reproduced on a build of the branch at 1280 × 800 through the browser's accessibility tree: that name in Room 1, and the same after a jump. The visible name of the map is "Floor plan" / "Alaprajz" (its title), so the new string uses it. In `index.html` the map's `data-i18n-attr` sets only its title now (`hud.minimap`, unchanged); in `src/main.js` `nameMap()` sets its `aria-label` from the new string with the current room's name, called from `updateRoom` when the room changes (by a jump, Page Down or walking) and after a language switch. The title is still exposed to a screen reader as the map's description; that is the proposal's "in the title only". New string: `ui.hud.minimapHere` "Floor plan: you are in {room}" / "Alaprajz. Jelenlegi hely: {room}" (the room's name as it is, "2. terem", so it needs no ending). `tests/a11y/names.mjs` has two new steps: B10.1, the name read from the accessibility tree after the entry, a jump to Room 2 and a move into Room 3 without a jump (the frame notices the room): "…Room 1", "…Room 2", "…Room 3", and the title is the jump hint, which the name no longer holds; B10.2, switched to Hungarian in Room 3: "Alaprajz. Jelenlegi hely: 3. terem", the title in Hungarian, then a jump to Room 4: "…4. terem". With `index.html` and `src/main.js` as before, both fail. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal, the wording and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B16: while an installation photo shows in the viewer, the image's text says it is a photo of the room: "Installation photo 1 of 2: Let it Flow in the gallery", or "Installation photo: Wrong Data Landscape Dresden in the gallery" where a work has one photo; the work's own text ("Let it Flow, Installation: video projection, plastic waste") comes back with the work. Before, `renderInfo` in `src/ui/viewer.js` gave the image the work's title and medium whatever it showed, so a photo of the gallery was read as the work. Reproduced on a build of the branch at 1280 × 800: on no. 16, after "View in situ (installation photo)", the image's alt was still "Let it Flow, Installation: video projection, plastic waste", in Hungarian "Let it Flow, installáció: videóvetítés, műanyag hulladék". `renderInfo` now picks the text by what shows; it runs again on each click of the photo button and after a language switch. Nos. 3 and 16 have two installation photos each, the other works one. New strings: `ui.viewer.situAlt` "Installation photo {n} of {total}: {title} in the gallery" / "{n}. installációs fotó a {total} közül: {title} a galériában"; `ui.viewer.situAltOne` "Installation photo: {title} in the gallery" / "Installációs fotó: {title} a galériában" (the proposal's "1 of 2" form only where there are two, so a single photo is not "1 of 1"). The title inside the alt text cannot carry `lang="en"` (B6), since an attribute cannot be split. `tests/a11y/names.mjs` has three new steps: B16.1, no. 16: the work's text, then from the accessibility tree "Installation photo 1 of 2…", "…2 of 2…", then the work's text again (read from the attribute, since the work shows as its video loop and the image is hidden then); B16.2, no. 1's one photo: "Installation photo: Wrong Data Landscape Dresden in the gallery"; B16.3, switched to Hungarian while the photo shows: "1. installációs fotó a 2 közül: Let it Flow a galériában", then English again. With the old `viewer.js` all three fail. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal, the wording and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- B17: the Google Drive player's frame and the video in the viewer have names, the axe check has a full-video state with Google blocked, and the accessibility statement says that Esc does not work inside the player and how to get out. Before, `#viewer-frame` had an empty title and `#viewer-video` no name. Reproduced on a build of the branch at 1280 × 800 through the browser's accessibility tree: the frame's title "" in both languages, and the video's name empty (on a video this Chromium cannot play, "Unable to play media.", the browser's own words). In `renderInfo` in `src/ui/viewer.js` the frame's title and the video's `aria-label` are set from new strings, so a language switch while the viewer is open updates them; the frame still loads only after a click on "Full video". The video is a loop, or for nos. 3 and 16 the video documentation that plays in the viewer, so it has two names. New strings: `ui.viewer.frameTitle` "Full video: {title}, Google Drive player" / "Teljes videó: {title}, Google Drive-lejátszó"; `ui.viewer.videoLoopName` "Video loop: {title}" / "Videóloop: {title}"; `ui.viewer.videoDocName` "Video documentation: {title}" / "Videódokumentáció: {title}". In the statement (`accessibility.sections[3]`, "Known gaps" / "Ismert hiányosságok", its date left at 14 November 2026), a new last paragraph: "Esc does not close the viewer while focus is inside the Google Drive player, because the browser keeps the keys inside the player's frame: Tab or Shift+Tab, pressed until focus leaves the player, gets out of it, and then Esc works, or the Close button closes the viewer." / "Amíg a fókusz a Google Drive-lejátszóban van, az Esc nem zárja be a műnézőt, mert a böngésző a billentyűket a lejátszó keretében tartja: a Tab vagy a Shift+Tab ismételt lenyomásával ki lehet lépni a lejátszóból, és utána az Esc működik, vagy a Bezárás gomb bezárja a műnézőt." (Corrected after review: it first said "Tab or Shift+Tab leaves the player", as if one press did, which only the one-button stand-in below showed.) That was checked in Chromium first, without loading anything from Google: the test answers the player's address with a stand-in page of its own (one button), so the frame comes from another site as the real one does; with focus on its button, Esc reached the frame's page and the viewer stayed open, and Tab or Shift+Tab took focus out of the frame into the viewer, where Esc closed it. The real Drive player was not loaded, so how many of its controls Tab passes before it leaves is not known. `tests/a11y/axe.mjs` blocks every request to Google for the walk (`page.route`, abort) and scans one more state in each language, the viewer with "Full video with sound" pressed on no. 6: 0 violations; with the old `viewer.js` it fails there on `frame-title` (serious). `tests/a11y/names.mjs` has four new steps, requests to Google blocked as in all its steps: B17.1, the video's name "Video loop: Artificial Paradise – Singapore", and for no. 3 "Video documentation: Paradise Factory" (from the accessibility tree; where this Chromium has failed to play the file, from the label, since the browser then names the video "Unable to play media."; the step waits, up to 30 s, until the tree gives the label or the clip has failed, and only then decides: read at once after the clip changed, the tree could still hold the last clip's "Unable to play media." while the new clip's error was not set yet, and the step failed once that way, fixed after review); B17.2, after "Full video" the frame is named "Full video: Artificial Paradise – Singapore, Google Drive player", in Hungarian "Teljes videó: …, Google Drive-lejátszó", and no request to Google was made before the press; B17.3, the Esc check above; B17.4, the statement says it in both languages. With the old `viewer.js` and language files B17.1, B17.2 and B17.4 fail; B17.3 checks the browser and passes either way. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal, the wording and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- A4: the floor plan now has its key, one line just under it, right-aligned with it: a square cyan mark and "Videos", a round magenta mark and "Other works" ("Videók", "A többi mű"). Before, group a had made the marks round and square, but nothing on screen said which was which. The marks in the key are drawn as `src/ui/minimap.js` draws them on the map: 5 px of colour with a 1 px dark edge (`rgba(11,12,14,.9)`), 7 px across, the map's own magenta and cyan. "Other works" and not "Paintings": the map draws every work that is not a video (`type` video or projection) round, so the round marks are the paintings, the prints and the two installations, nos. 3 and 16 (Let it Flow is a projection but has the installation type), and "Paintings and prints" would have been wrong for those two; the wording is the reconstructor's to change. One conflict is left open for her: Room 4's subtitle on screen calls Let it Flow a video ("Let it Flow · video", `rooms.r4.subtitle`), while the map draws it round and the key puts it under "Other works"; either the key's words ("Video screens", say) or the mark for no. 16 would have to change, and that is her choice (noted after review). The key (`#map-key` in `index.html`, styled in `src/style.css`) is 12 px in the HUD's muted colour `#c4c3be` on the same glass as the map: 7.16:1 at worst, worked out for the glass over pure white (over black it is higher). It is hidden from screen readers (`aria-hidden`), which get the map as its name only ("Floor plan: you are in Room 2", B10), not its marks. The map is not hidden on a touch screen (150 × 102 px at 820 px wide or less), so neither is its key. Two things moved so that the key lies over nothing: in a window under 520 px high the hint in the middle ("Drag the mouse to look around the room.") is kept narrower than the window less the map and its key on both sides, since before it lay 6 px over the map's lower corner and would have covered the key (a laptop at 200%, 683 × 384); and on a phone on its side the zoom readout ("2.0×") stands beside the zoom keys instead of above them, where it would have covered the key. On a small phone on its side, under 360 px high (568 × 320), the key stands at the left of the map, level with its top, and the room label stops short of it: under the map it lay under the zoom keys (its right end, "A többi mű", behind the + key) and under the touch hint (D11), found in review. New strings: `ui.hud.mapKey.videos` "Videos" / "Videók", `ui.hud.mapKey.other` "Other works" / "A többi mű". New check `tests/a11y/hud.mjs`, steps A4.1 to A4.5: at 1366 × 768 the key is one line 0 to 8 px under the map and right-aligned with it, at least 11 px, at least 4.5:1, its marks 7 px with the dark edge, square cyan and round magenta, and hidden from screen readers; the key's mark colours and edge, as the page shows them, are those `minimap.js` draws with, read from that file (A4.2; at first it only read `minimap.js` and passed without the key, corrected after review); switched to Hungarian, the Hungarian words, still one line; the key over none of the map, the room label and the hint; and on phones (375 × 667 in both languages, 320 × 640 and 667 × 375 in Hungarian) and at 200% (683 × 384), zoomed in so the readout shows and the hint brought back, one line, inside the window, over none of the map, the room label, the stick, the zoom keys, the zoom readout, the toolbar and the hint, and the hint not over the map; at 568 × 320 in both languages, the key at the left of the map, 0 to 8 px from it and level with its top, over none of the same, and D11.2 at 568 × 320 too. With `index.html` and `src/style.css` as before, A4.1, A4.2, A4.3 and every A4.5 fail; with the change all 10 steps pass. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal, the wording and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation). (Note of 16 November 2026: the reconstructor decided that Let it Flow is an installation, not a video. The key has no group for installations, so Let it Flow stays among "Other works"; Room 4's subtitle now reads "Let it Flow · installation" / "Let it Flow · installáció" in place of "· video" / "· videó" (`rooms.r4.subtitle` in `src/i18n/en.json` and `hu.json`). Decided by: reconstructor. Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).)

- D11: on a touch screen the sound question names the Sound button in place of the M key, and inside, a touch hint says how to move by touch. Before, a phone visitor read "M switches the sound on or off any time", with no keyboard to press it, and once inside got no hint at all: the hint in the middle (`#click-to-look`) was hidden on touch. Reproduced on a build of the branch at 375 × 667 with touch: the question's hint was the M line, and inside the hint was not shown. The sound button on the toolbar shows a loudspeaker and the word "Sound" ("Hang"), at the bottom of the screen, so the touch hint names it so. In `src/main.js`, on a touch screen, the question's hint and the hint inside take their touch strings (`setText`, so a language switch while either shows changes it); the desktop words are unchanged. The touch hint sits just above the middle of the view (`src/style.css`, `body.touch #click-to-look`), since below the middle, on a 320 × 640 phone, it would reach the stick; it lies over none of the stick, the toolbar, the zoom keys, the map, its key and the room label at 375 × 667, 320 × 640 and 667 × 375. It goes at the first touch on the 3D view or on the stick, once the visitor is in the room, or by itself 8 seconds after the sound question, as on a computer; nothing is stored, so it shows again on the next visit. It cannot take focus and takes none: focus stays on the 3D view. It is hidden from screen readers (`aria-hidden`), so it is never read: with VoiceOver or TalkBack on, a drag moves the screen reader's focus and does not look round, so its gestures are not that visitor's; the toolbar's buttons and the accessible version are. On a computer the hint in the middle stays as it was and is still read. With reduced motion it goes at once (the existing rule switches every fade off). The proposal's words were "hold the stick to walk". Tested first: pressing and holding the stick does nothing yet (D2 is not built), it walks only when pushed, so the hint says "push". A drag looks round, and a tap on a work opens it. That last one did not always hold: the browser sends a mouse click after a tap, and when the viewer had just opened under the finger, the click landed beside the picture (or on it before it had loaded) and closed the viewer again at once. Reproduced at 375 × 667: a tap at the middle in front of no. 5 opened the viewer and the click after it, on `.viewer-media`, closed it; in front of no. 1 the click landed on the picture and it stayed. In `src/controls.js` a tap that opens something now cancels its touchend, so no click follows. (A tap on a work during the tour opens it through `pointerup` in `main.js`; that path is left as it was and may have the same fault.)
