  Checked on a build: a new step in `tests/a11y/hud.mjs`, H1.1, reads from the page that the accent is #8fa8ff and that no element's colour, background, border, outline or shadow is the old green, works out the accent text on glass (5.76) and a pressed keycap's letter (8.63), and checks the bar on a pressed toolbar button (none when not pressed) and the bold name and accent bar of the current room; it fails if the accent goes back to green or any of those marks is taken out. `tests/a11y/reduced-motion.mjs` looks for the visitor's dot by its colour, so its colour there changed from #9dff6a to #8fa8ff on purpose (the expectation is the same). Results: on a build of this step, with the machine heavily loaded (load average 20 to 25 on 4 cores from other test runs), H1.1 passed in a run of its own (twice), and in `hud.mjs` A4.1 to A4.5, D11.1 to D11.6, D2.1, D2.2, D8.1, F3.1 to F3.4, F1.1, F1.2, F1.4 to F1.6, F1.8 and F1.9 passed (60 steps, no console errors); F1.3 and F1.7 stopped on a page load timeout and E2, F2 did not get to run (the page took over 90 s to load), so those are not checked for this step. `axe.mjs` (without --write): 37 pages with 0 violations, including the rooms menu open and the walk inside, before it stopped on a click timeout at the last Hungarian page. `loader-weight.mjs` passed (no change to the loading screen). `reduced-motion.mjs`: the map dot checks pass with the new colour (it keeps its size with reduced motion, swells for 3 s after a jump without); its still-frame screenshot timed out under the load and the wheel check found the wheel still running at the end, both timing under the load, not the colour. `focus-mouse.mjs`: steps 1 to 5 passed, then it timed out under the load; nothing about focus or its ring changed in this step. The full suite is still to run. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- D10 and A7: open, not built in group e. (Superseded on 17 November 2026: D10 and A7 were built in Part 1, with H1 steps 2 to 4, on 16 November; see the entries "D10", "H1, step 2" to "H1, step 4" and "A7" below. The rest of this entry is kept as it was written.) D10 (a touch-screen laptop is treated as a phone): unchanged, checked in the code on 16 November: both `isTouch` tests (`src/controls.js:40` and `src/main.js:33`) are still `matchMedia('(pointer: coarse)').matches || 'ontouchstart' in window`, and the mouse press on the 3D view still returns at once when `isTouch` is set (`src/controls.js:108`), so on such a laptop the mouse cannot look round or open a work, and the keycaps and the footer stay hidden. Notes for building it: headless Chromium cannot stand in for a touch-screen laptop. With no options it reports `any-pointer: fine`; with touch on (with or without the mobile flag) it reports `pointer: coarse`, no `any-pointer: fine` and no `any-hover`; `'ontouchstart' in window` is false in every case. So a test needs an init script that answers `matchMedia('(any-pointer: fine)')` as true, and the layout has to follow the `body.touch` class, not CSS `any-pointer` queries. The places to change: `isTouch` in `src/main.js` and `src/controls.js` (for example coarse and not also fine), the mouse guard in `src/controls.js` (a `pointerdown` that looks at `pointerType === 'mouse'` for each press), the other `isTouch` uses in `src/main.js` (whether Next work by mouse also opens the work, and the aim under the mouse in `updateHover`) and `isTouch` in `src/ui/panels.js`. To check: `tests/a11y/focus-mouse.mjs` and `tests/a11y/hud.mjs` again. A7 (interface type of 9 to 11 px monospace capitals): unchanged and not started; it belongs to the look (H1, steps 2 and 3: labels 12 to 13 px at least, sentence case, letter spacing 0.06em at most), to be checked with `tests/a11y/hud.mjs` and the axe run. Both are still on the list for Part 2; nothing in the scene or the texts changed with this entry. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (the proposal and how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation). (Note of 16 November 2026: D10, A7, H1 steps 2 to 4 and the tour's possible tap fault (a tap on the tour may close what it opens, as D11 fixed elsewhere) are continued in a separate session, starting from f9f524b. Decided by: reconstructor.)

- D11, the tour's tap fault: on a phone, a tap during the tour opens the work at that stop and no mouse click follows it (16 November 2026). The D11 entry left this path open ("may have the same fault"). It had it. Reproduced on a build of `ba3b7f1` at 375 × 667 with touch, the tour brought to a stop and the view tapped: the tap opened the viewer through `pointerup` in `src/main.js`, and the browser's mouse click after the tap then landed on the viewer under the finger. At no. 1, tapped at 187, 280, the click landed beside the picture (`.viewer-media`) and the viewer closed again at once. The tour caption lets a tap through to the view under it, and there the click landed lower in the viewer: at no. 5 on "View in situ (installation photo)", at no. 16 on "On the artist's website", which opened a new tab. The other taps of the tour held and are unchanged: a tap on Tour starts it and it stays on, a second tap stops it, a tap on Next work or Rooms stops it; the stick is hidden during the tour. The fix reuses D11's: a tap during the tour now goes the way a tap outside it goes, through `controls.onTap` (`src/controls.js` calls it while the tour has the controls, `tapOnly`, set in `syncEnabled`), opens the work under the finger or else the work in view, as before, and returns true, so `controls.js` cancels the touchend and the browser sends no click. The `pointerup` in `main.js` now leaves a finger's touch alone and serves the mouse as before. The mouse has no such fault: at 1366 × 768 a click on the view during the tour, at no. 1, no. 5 and no. 16, opened the work, it stayed open, and the click went to the 3D view. One thing follows: by touch, during the tour, only a tap opens the work (under 10 px and under 350 ms, the same test as outside the tour); before, lifting the finger after a drag or a slow press on the view opened it too. Tried on the build with the fix: after a drag of 80 px and after a press of 450 ms on the view the tour goes on and nothing opens. Whether a slow press should open the work, in the tour and outside it, is a question for the reconstructor. With the phone on its side (667 × 375) the tap on the view opens the work at the same three stops and no click follows. Checked on a build: new step D11.7 in `tests/a11y/hud.mjs` (tap on the caption at no. 5 and no. 16, tap on the view at no. 1: the work opens, stays open, no click follows, no new tab; Tour tapped on, tapped off); it fails on the build before the fix (clicks on "View in situ", the artist's link and `.viewer-media`, two tabs, no. 1 closed) and passes after. Chromium's phone emulation only; not tried on a real phone, in Safari on an iPhone, or with a pen. (Added 17 November 2026, after the review of Part 1: D11.7 now runs as well on the touch-screen laptop of D10, the entry below, where touch works beside the mouse. With the fix taken out locally (the `pointerup` in `main.js` serving touch again and `tapOnly` out of `controls.js`, not committed), D11.7 fails on the phone and on the laptop: the click after the tap lands on `.viewer-info` or `.viewer-media`, and on the laptop it closes the viewer at all three stops. With the fix both pass.) The message of commit `d64f9bb` names "Walk to this work" for no. 5; it was "View in situ". No new strings. Decided by: reconstructor (to look into it, and to fix it as D11 did); assistant (the way through `onTap`). Not a reconstruction decision (interface).

- D10: a laptop with a touch screen gets the computer's interface, and touch works on it as well (16 November 2026, Part 1; this entry written on 17 November after review, from the commits `1edb1cf`, `d51a2db` and `6fc3d3d`, merged in `ff6f2d8`). Before, any device whose browser has touch events was taken for a phone: `isTouch` was `matchMedia('(pointer: coarse)').matches || 'ontouchstart' in window`, once in `src/main.js` and once in `src/controls.js`, and Chrome on a laptop with a touch screen has `ontouchstart`. So `body.touch` was set there: the stick showed; of the keycaps only the two turning keys; no footer and no first-use card; the hint and the sound question had the touch words; and the mouse press on the 3D view returned at once (`controls.js`), so the mouse could not look round or open a work. Reproduced on a build of `ba3b7f1` with the new checks (below): headless Chromium at 1366 × 768 with touch on, told by an init script what Chrome on such a laptop answers. D10.1, D10.2, D10.3 and `focus-mouse.mjs` step 47 failed there: the `touch` class was set, two keycaps, the stick, no footer, no card, the touch words; a mouse drag did not turn the view, nothing was named under the mouse and a click opened nothing; by touch there was no Back keycap to hold; after Tab and a mouse press the keyboard frame stayed on the view. D10.4, the phone, passed (it is a guard). What changed. (1) One test, in the new `src/device.js`, used by `main.js` and `controls.js`: `isTouch` now means a phone or a tablet, a touch screen (the old test, unchanged) and no mouse or trackpad, where a mouse or trackpad is `(any-pointer: fine)` together with `(any-hover: hover)`. The second half is the assistant's addition to the proposal's "`any-pointer` for what to show": a pen is a fine pointer too but does not hover, so a phone with a pen stays a phone; this rests on how Chromium for Android reports a pen, not tested on a device. (2) What a press does is decided press by press, not by device. In `controls.js` the mouse press on the view no longer asks `isTouch`: a `pointerdown` listener on the window notes the kind of pointer of each press (`pointerType`), and the mouse path leaves out only the presses a finger made, that is the mouse events a browser sends of its own after a tap. A finger is still handled by the touch listeners, which never asked about the device, so on the laptop a drag looks round, two fingers pinch to zoom and a tap opens a work; a tap that opens something still cancels its `touchend`, so no click follows and the viewer stays open (D11). There is no stick on the laptop: by touch the keycaps walk, as they do under the mouse. (3) In `main.js` the aim under the mouse (the line "Click to view · no. title" and the hand cursor) follows `pointermove` of a mouse or a pen, not `mousemove`, and a touch on the view ends it until the mouse moves again: a finger leaves no cursor behind. Every other use of `isTouch` keeps the meaning "phone or tablet": the `touch` class on the body, which the layout follows; the touch words of the sound question and the touch hint; the lower picture quality and the wider field of view; Next work without "Press Enter" (the laptop has an Enter key, so there a tap on the button counts as a click); the touch hint in place of the first-use card; Help with the touch controls first (`src/ui/panels.js`). So on the laptop all of these are the computer's: its words ("Drag the mouse to look around the room.", "M switches the sound on or off any time"), the first-use card, and Help with both controls texts, the computer's first. Not changed, and named here on purpose: `src/ui/viewer.js` shows a loop video's own player controls always when the first pointer is coarse and otherwise only under the mouse, so on the laptop a finger does not bring them up (the Pause / Play button under the video works by touch); the title screen's line before entering and the 44 px touch targets follow the style sheet's `(pointer: coarse)`, the first pointer, so on a real laptop they are the computer's line and sizes (the toolbar's buttons 32 px high), which the accessibility statement says since the review of Part 1. A finger's tap during the tour goes through `controls.onTap` on the laptop as on a phone (the tour's tap fault, the entry above). Checked on a build (16 November 2026, Chromium, software rendering). Headless Chromium cannot be a touch-screen laptop by itself, so `tests/a11y/lib.mjs` has `TOUCH_LAPTOP`: touch on, and an init script that makes `matchMedia` answer as Chrome on such a laptop does (`pointer: fine`, `hover: hover`, `any-pointer: fine` and `coarse`, `any-hover: hover`) and gives the window `ontouchstart`. The style sheet's own media queries cannot be answered that way and still see a coarse pointer there, so the 44 px sizes apply in the check and would not on a real laptop. `tests/a11y/hud.mjs` has four new steps: D10.1, on the laptop no `touch` class, the five keycaps, the footer and the first-use card show, no stick, the computer's words in the hint (not hidden from screen readers) and in the sound question, Help with the computer's controls first; D10.2, a mouse drag turns the view, the mouse over no. 5 names it, a click opens it and it stays open; D10.3, a finger dragged turns the view and the name under the resting mouse goes, two fingers pinch to zoom, the Back keycap held with a finger walks and lets go, a tap on no. 5 opens it and it stays open, and the mouse dragged straight after turns the view again; D10.4, a phone (375 × 667, touch, no mouse) is as before. `tests/a11y/focus-mouse.mjs` has step 47: on the laptop, focus by Tab shows the frame on the view and a mouse press on the view drops it, as on any computer. On a build of the change all four D10 steps and step 47 passed, with no console errors. After the review of Part 1 (17 November 2026), D10.3 also fails if the browser's mouse click follows the tap (it was read and printed, but did not count), and D11.7 runs on this laptop too (the entry above). With the D11 cancel of the touchend taken out of `controls.js` locally (not committed), D10.3 fails on the laptop: the tap opens no. 5 and the browser's click follows it (it landed on the picture, so the viewer stayed open, which is why the old D10.3 passed). On 18 November, on a build of `wip/p1-fixes`, `hud.mjs` passed 84 of 84 and `focus-mouse.mjs` 59 of 59 (the 3D view on the GPU). Limits: not tried on a real touch-screen laptop, tablet or phone, nor in Firefox or Safari; an iPad or an Android tablet with a mouse attached now gets the computer's interface as well (touch still works there, without the stick), which follows from the proposal and was not tried. Nothing in the scene, the texts of the works or the sound changed. The accessibility statement's known gap for this says what was built and that it was checked in Chromium set up to answer like such a laptop, not on a real one (EN and HU, in `docs/accessibility/hu-confirm.md`; the statement's date is unchanged). No new strings. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal, and that the laptop gets the computer's interface with touch still working); assistant (how it is built, and the hover half of the test). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- H1, step 2: the panels are paper ("paper for words" in `docs/design/ui-ux-proposal.md`, and the panel in `docs/design/mockup-interface.html`) (16 November 2026, Part 1, `69592cf`, with `e541a7a`; this entry and the three after it written on 17 November after review). Before, read on a build of `ba3b7f1` at 1366 × 768 and 375 × 667: About, Works, Behind the scenes, Contact, Help, Accessibility, Legal notice and Privacy were light text on a dark overlay as wide as the window, rgba(8, 8, 10, 0.9), with section headings in the accent in 11 px capitals, a × of 34 px for Close at the window's corner, and a white focus ring. Changed (`index.html`, `src/style.css`): a panel is a paper card #fbfbf9 (`--paper`), 560 px wide at most, with 24 × 28 px of padding, corners of 10 px and a pale hairline (`--paper-edge` #c9ccd3, a token since the review), as the mockup draws it, on the same dark ground (which stays, so a click beside the card still closes it). In it: text in ink #16181d at 16 px, the title at 32 px in sentence case, section headings in the sans at 15 px semibold, secondary text (the line over the title, notes, the works' numbers and data, the credits' labels) in muted ink #4a4f5a at 13 px, links in the blue #072fcc, underlined; lines between the works and round the tags in `--paper-line` #8a8f99. Close is a button that says "Close" ("Bezárás"; the string `viewer.close`, which was its hidden name already), inside the card at its top right, blue with a blue edge; the buttons in a panel (Legal notice, Privacy, "Show the short guide again") look the same, and turn to paper on blue under the mouse. The focus ring inside a panel is the blue (`#panel { --focus }`; white would not show on paper). The accent #8fa8ff is nowhere in a panel. A row of the works list under the mouse has its title underlined (it had a faint tint). The legal and privacy texts are not reworded. Over a panel opened from the title screen the navbar had light text on a gradient that faded to nothing, which would not read with the card under it, so there it now has the same steady band as inside, rgba(11, 12, 14, 0.8), its buttons at full strength, and a focused button gets the dark backing it has inside. The viewer is not touched by this step: it stays dark. Contrast: the rows "Step 2" in the H1 table above. Checked on a build: new step H1.2 in `tests/a11y/hud.mjs` opens About, Works, Contact, Help and Accessibility inside and About from the title screen, one page at a time, and reads from the page that the card is #fbfbf9, that every text in it is 4.5:1 or more on paper, that nothing in it is in the accent, that the focus ring on Close, a link, a button, a row of the works list and the opening speech is #072fcc, 2 px, and that the navbar is on a steady band with its text at 4.5:1 or more with paper under it. On a build of `ba3b7f1` it fails (no paper card; the headings in the accent; light text that would be 1.09:1 on paper). On 18 November 2026, on a build of `wip/p1-fixes` after the review, with the 3D view drawn on the GPU, the whole of `hud.mjs` passed (84 of 84, with the console check) and `focus-mouse.mjs` too (59 of 59). No new strings. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (how it is built: a card on the dark ground, the navbar's band, the underline for a row under the mouse). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- H1, step 3: the glass chips, and the white focus ring on them (`4101823`, with `e541a7a`). Before, on a build of `ba3b7f1`: the chips over the 3D view (room label, floor plan and its key, toolbar, rooms menu, keycaps, wheel line, zoom readout, the two hints, the first-use card, the tour caption) had square corners and an edge of white at 14% (1.42:1 against the glass over white), and most of their text was 10 to 11 px monospace capitals with letters 0.12 to 0.18em apart. Changed (`src/style.css`): every chip is the glass rgba(11, 12, 14, 0.85) with a 1 px edge of white at 45% (`--edge`) and corners of 8 px (keycaps and the wheel line 6 px), as in the mockup. Their text is in sentence case: the room name in the sans at 15 px semibold with its subtitle at 13 px in `--hud-muted` (it was #c9c9c6 at 10 px), the line that names the work in front and the two hints at 13 px, the map key and the wheel line at 12 px in the sans, the tour caption's first line at 13 px semibold (still in the accent, 5.76:1) and its data line at 13 px, keycap letters and the zoom readout in the mono at 12 px, the rooms menu's rooms at 13 px and the works under them at 12 px. Those works are now in `--hud-muted` #c4c3be: the grey #9a9a98 they had was 3.40:1 on a row under the mouse over a white wall, a gap the statement named; it is 5.43:1 now. Focus: the ring is the same rule as before (2 px, `--focus`, white inside the exhibition). On a toolbar button, a keycap and the zoom readout it now stands 2 px outside the control with a dark halo of 5 px round it (`--halo` #0b0c0e), as the mockup draws it, so it shows where it reaches past the chip onto a white wall; before it was drawn inside the control. A pressed button or keycap keeps its own mark under the halo. In the rooms menu the ring stays inside the row, since the menu scrolls. The navbar and footer keep the dark backing on focus, and the 3D view its frame. Positions and sizes of the chips are as they were, but for the chips' own height where the type grew. Contrast: the rows "Step 3" in the H1 table above. Checked on a build: new step H1.3 in `hud.mjs` reads 13 chips (glass 0.85; an edge of 1 px, white at 45%, at 3:1 or more against the glass over white and over black; corners of 6 px or more), the text colours on them (4.5:1 or more, also the works on a row under the mouse; the glass read from the toolbar), and the ring on a focused toolbar button (pressed and not), a keycap and a row of the rooms menu. On a build of `ba3b7f1` it fails (edges 1.42:1, square corners). On 18 November 2026, on a build of `wip/p1-fixes` after the review, with the 3D view drawn on the GPU, the whole of `hud.mjs` passed (84 of 84, with the console check) and `focus-mouse.mjs` too (59 of 59). No new strings. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- H1, step 4: the buttons (`c38081a`). Before, on a build of `ba3b7f1`: toolbar buttons, the viewer's Previous, Next and "Walk to this work" and the buttons in panels were 11 px monospace capitals in square boxes; a pressed toolbar button had a faint white tint and the bar of step 1; the viewer's Close was a × of 34 px with a hidden name; the navbar's button for the open panel was underlined inside, but on the title screen it was told by its strength alone (opacity 1 against 0.75). Changed (`src/style.css`, `index.html`): toolbar buttons are words in the sans at 13 px semibold, in sentence case, with corners of 6 px, 32 px high or more (44 px on a touch screen, as before; 12 px with the word under the sign on a phone). A pressed one (Tour running, Sound on; aria-pressed) has an edge all round and the bar along its foot, both in the accent, on a tint of the accent (0.16) laid on the glass, as the mockup draws it; not pressed it has none of the three, and Sound still changes its sign. The rooms menu's rows keep the regular weight, so the bold of the current room still tells it. In the viewer, which stays dark, Previous, Next, "Walk to this work" and the photo and video buttons are words in the sans at 13 px semibold with the chips' light edge and corners of 6 px, and Close says "Close" on the glass with the same edge, at the same place as the ×. Enter and the two buttons of the sound question keep their blue outline and the mono, in sentence case with their letters 0.06em apart (Enter was capitals 0.3em apart); the bars beside Enter stay. The button for the open panel is underlined in the navbar on the title screen as well. No state is told by colour alone: pressed (edge and bar), current room (bold and bar), pressed keycap (edge, sinks by 1 px), chosen language and open panel (underline), focus (ring). Contrast: the rows "Step 4" in the H1 table above. Checked on a build: new step H1.4 in `hud.mjs` reads the five toolbar buttons (sans, 600, 13 px, no capitals by CSS, corners, height), the three marks of a pressed one and their absence when not pressed, Sound's two signs, the rooms menu's weights and bar, a pressed keycap's edge and shift, the underlines in the navbar inside and on the title screen, Close's word in a panel and in the viewer, the blue buttons on paper at rest and under the mouse, and the edges of the viewer's buttons over white and black. On a build of `ba3b7f1` it fails (11 px monospace capitals at weight 400, square, 29 to 31 px high). Found in the review of Part 1 (18 November 2026): with the edge and the 12 px words the phone toolbar (the word under the sign) grew from 65 to 70 px, and at 375 × 667 and 320 × 640 its top met the stick by 2 px (F2.1 failed, in software and on the GPU alike). Its buttons now have 4 px above and below in place of 6, so the bar is 66 px and clear of the stick; the buttons are still 44 px or more. In the same review H1.2 was made to wait until the navbar's 0.4 s colour change has ended: on the GPU it read the colours half way (1.68:1). On 18 November 2026, on a build of `wip/p1-fixes` after the review, with the 3D view drawn on the GPU, the whole of `hud.mjs` passed (84 of 84, with the console check) and `focus-mouse.mjs` too (59 of 59). No new strings. Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (how it is built, and that Enter keeps its outline). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- A7: larger interface type (`bfa2f50`, with `e541a7a`; the statement in `a5dff8a`). Before, on a build of `ba3b7f1`: most interface type was 9 to 11 px monospace capitals with letters up to 0.3em apart (navbar 11 px, footer 10 px, the title card's lines 11 px, the sound question, toolbar, room label, hints, keycaps, the viewer's data, link, headings and buttons, the panels' headings and notes). Changed: with steps 2 to 4 of H1 and, in this step, the rest (`src/style.css`, `src/ui/viewer.js`, `src/ui/minimap.js`): no text in the interface is under 12 px, in capitals by CSS, or with letters more than 0.06em apart. Sentence case needed no string changed: every string was already written in sentence case and the capitals came from CSS, so the interface strings in EN and HU are untouched. The navbar keeps the mono, as the mockup has it, at 13 px (12 px in the folded layout up to 760 px wide) with letters 0.06em apart; between 761 and 960 px wide the word "KristofLab" beside the title is put away so the four links fit. The footer is in the sans at 12 px. On the title card the lines under the logotype are mono at 13 px, the progress line 12 px, the line on the keys and the link to the accessible version in the sans at 13 px; the card is still a still image, nothing on it moves. The sound question is in the sans at 16 px semibold with its hint at 13 px. In the viewer the work's data, the link to the artist's page, "Exhibition text", the notes and "loading…" are in the sans at 13 px, the description's heading at 15 px semibold in the light text colour, the count in the mono at 12 px; the credit under the exhibition text is a note at 13 px (it was 11 px in a colour written into `viewer.js`). On the floor plan the room numbers are drawn at 12 px (11 px before) and the N of the compass at 12 px (8 px before). Nothing is animated that was not, and the reduced-motion rule is as it was. The accessibility statement no longer names small type as a known gap, and gives the rooms menu's titles at their new contrast (EN and HU). Contrast: the rows "A7" in the H1 table above. Checked on a build: new step A7.1 in `hud.mjs` reads every element with text of its own on the title screen, the sound question, the navbar and footer, the controls over the 3D view (also those not on screen at the moment), the eight panels and the viewer for two works, in English and Hungarian at 1366 × 768 and in English on a phone, and fails on any text under 12 px, in capitals by CSS, or with letters more than 0.06em apart. It cannot read what is drawn on the floor plan's canvas (the room numbers and the N, set to 12 px in `minimap.js`). On a build of `ba3b7f1` it fails (1,176 texts read, 657 wrong; the smallest 9.17 px, letters up to 0.300em apart). On 18 November 2026, on a build of `wip/p1-fixes` after the review, with the 3D view drawn on the GPU, the whole of `hud.mjs` passed (84 of 84, with the console check) and `focus-mouse.mjs` too (59 of 59). Decided by: reconstructor (to carry out the proposal); assistant (how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- 6.2, the measured or guessed view (Part 6, 17 November 2026; CLAUDE.md roadmap, item 3). A switch on the toolbar, "Measured or guessed" (a button with aria-pressed, between Sound and Next work; in a window up to 760 px wide it is in the folded menu instead, as Help is, and only inside the exhibition), draws every part of the rooms by the status of its paradata row: measured solid, inferred diagonal stripes, invented dots, omitted cross-hatch, not recorded plain grey with no marks, each mark in its own colour on a light ground, so a status never rests on colour alone. The view has a fixed light of its own, so dark Room 4 shows as well. The works are not repainted: a hung work keeps its picture or screen and gets a band 7 cm wide round it, outside its edge, between two thin dark edges, in the pattern of its row; Paradise Factory and Let it Flow keep theirs under the pattern laid over them, half transparent, the work showing between the stripes. The pools of light behind the works (ap-d22, invented) show as dotted pools. A legend on glass under the floor plan and its key names each status, its pattern in words and as a swatch, and the number of parts: measured 9, inferred 31, invented 5, omitted 1, not recorded 12, of 58. Nothing moves, with or without reduced motion; off puts back every material and takes away every band and overlay. Which row each part shows is in `src/data/paradata-scene.json`: 58 parts, each with the row chosen and why (the row whose element names the part most closely: walls ap-d01, floors ap-d03, the street wall ap-d07, the doorways ap-d09 and ap-d10, furniture ap-d20, the storage ap-d13, paintings ap-d25, prints and screens ap-d26, the monitor ap-d35, the installations' parts ap-d37, ap-d38, ap-d39, ap-d41, ap-d43, ap-d45, and so on). In the code a part is named by `userData.paradata`, or per vertex in the merged meshes of the walls and the furniture (`pd` keys in `layout.js`, `mark()` in `props.js`, `src/world/paradataTags.js`), so the number of draw calls is unchanged. The rows come from `src/data/paradata.json` (6.3), loaded only when the view is first switched on. The accessible version lists under each room its parts by status; it has no credits per work, so it does not list rights either. New strings EN and HU under `ui.paradata`, listed in `docs/accessibility/hu-confirm.md`. Gaps the mapping shows: 12 parts have no row (the ceilings of Rooms 1 to 3, the stairs, the left walls of Rooms 3 and 4 with their bricked-up openings and the ledge, the closet block in Room 3, the vaults and beams of Room 4, the projector, the track lights, the fittings of Rooms 1 and 4); the stains (ap-d74, invented) and the baked shadows (ap-d75, invented) are inside the wall materials and cannot be shown apart; the floor projection and the strips beside it (ap-d45 inferred, ap-d47 invented) are one mesh and show as ap-d45; the projector in the scene hangs from the ceiling conduit, where ap-d45's note places it above the screen of Natural Life. Decided by: reconstructor (the view); assistant (the row for each part, the patterns, the colours and the switch's place: proposals for the reconstructor's check). Not a reconstruction value: no geometry, light or sound changed. On a computer window 761 to 1280 px wide the toolbar, one button longer, now keeps clear of the on-screen keys and the zoom keys and wraps onto a second row where it must (measured at 780 to 1366 px, EN and HU: no overlap). Checked on a build (18 November 2026, GPU Chromium): `tests/a11y/paradata.mjs` (new) 10/10: the switch named and not pressed at rest; Tab from the 3D view reaches it, Enter switches it on and off; the legend's counts are the data's (9, 31, 5, 1, 12); all 58 parts are in the scene and no mesh is without one; 93 meshes of works keep their materials; off restores every material (181 meshes) and leaves no band or overlay; Hungarian; the folded menu at 375 × 667; reduced motion, two frames the same. smoke 2/2, keyboard 35/35, axe 40 states with no violations (the view on, with its legend, added in both languages), reduced-motion 14/14.
- Brief 4, references and the Concept page (branch `wip/references`, 16 and 17 November 2026; not merged): links from the exhibition's texts to what they mention, the opening speech's three footnotes, and a Concept page in the navbar with Themes, Works, Timeline and Network views, plus a Connections line in the viewer. Built on the pilot's panel, focus handling, Esc and reduced-motion rule; no new live region, storage or runtime dependency. The full entry, with who chose what, is the section "References and the Concept page" near the top; its colour pairs are in the H1 table above. Checks: `tests/a11y/concept.mjs` (log in `docs/accessibility/concept-test-log.md`) and the Concept page's four views in `tests/a11y/axe.mjs`.
- Full videos from YouTube (17 November 2026): the artwork viewer plays the full videos of nos. 3, 6, 7, 16 and 17 from YouTube in place of Google Drive. Presentation, not reconstruction: nothing in the rooms changes, and the loops in the 3D rooms stay local (`public/video/`). Decided by: reconstructor (her instructions of 17 November, Brief 5a, with the five addresses). The player is YouTube's embed from `https://www.youtube-nocookie.com/embed/<id>?rel=0&hl=<en|hu>`, made only when "Full video with sound" is pressed, as the Drive player was: no autoplay, no preloading, nothing from YouTube or Google is asked for before the press. `rel=0` keeps the videos suggested at the end to the same channel; `hl` gives the player's controls the site's language. No. 16 had no full video before: it now has Siluett, the video projected on the end wall, on a button named "Siluett: full video with sound" ("Siluett: a teljes videó hanggal"), and pressing it again goes "Back to the video documentation" ("Vissza a videódokumentációhoz"), since no. 16's viewer plays its documentation, not the loop. Siluett and Natural Life are unlisted on YouTube: they are embedded the same way, and the site adds no link to YouTube or the channel. What the YouTube player still shows by itself (YouTube's, not the site's, and not checked here because the tests block YouTube): the video's title and the channel's name and picture at the top, YouTube's logo, which opens the video on youtube.com, and when the video ends, other videos from the same channel. The Wiena videos (nos. 8 and 15) stay on Google Drive, as do the documentation videos and the floor video as source files. Data: `video.full` entries in `src/data/exhibition.json` take a `youtube` id that wins over `drive` (the Drive ids are kept beside it), so moving no. 8 or 15 later is one line, and an entry's `title` names a full video whose title is not the work's (Siluett). Code: `src/ui/viewer.js` (`playerSrc`). The frame is named "Full video: <title>, YouTube player" ("Teljes videó: <title>, YouTube-lejátszó"); the Drive player keeps its name. Privacy and legal notice, at her request, dated 17 November 2026: the privacy section names YouTube (embedded from youtube-nocookie.com) next to Google Drive, both Google services; the legal notice has a new section "Video players" ("Videólejátszók") naming Google Ireland Limited as the provider of both. The accessibility statement names the YouTube player next to the Drive player (not tested with a screen reader; Esc inside the player is the same known gap). Tests: `tests/a11y/youtube.mjs` (new), `names.mjs` B17 and `axe.mjs` now open no. 6's YouTube player with YouTube blocked or answered by a stand-in page.

- A4, the key's groups (17 November 2026, the reconstructor's correction from her localhost check): the floor plan's key now has four groups, "Paintings", "Videos", "Installations", "Other works" ("Festmények", "Videók", "Installációk", "Egyéb művek", her words), and the map draws each work in its group by its `type` in `src/data/exhibition.json`. Before, the key had two groups, "Videos" and "Other works" ("A többi mű"), and every work that was not a video was a round magenta mark. The groups, in `MAP_GROUPS` in `src/ui/minimap.js`: `painting` goes to Paintings (nos. 1, 2, 4, 5, 9, 10, 14), `video` (and `projection`, which no work has now) to Videos (nos. 6, 7, 8, 15, 17, and the small screen of no. 3, `w03-screen`), `installation` to Installations (no. 3, Paradise Factory, at its plant, and no. 16, Let it Flow), and any other type to Other works: that is the three prints, nos. 11 to 13 (`print`), which match none of her four words clearly and stand there until she says otherwise. No. 3 keeps two marks, as before: a triangle at the plant and a square at its screen. Each group has its own shape, so none is told by colour alone (A4): paintings round, videos square (as before), installations a triangle pointing up, other works a diamond; each about 5 px of colour, of about the same area (19 to 25 px²), with the 1 px dark edge `rgba(11,12,14,.9)` all round (now a 2 px stroke under the fill, so the triangle's and the diamond's corners get the same edge). Colours from the H1 tokens, no new one: paintings magenta #ff4fa3 and videos cyan #5fe7ff as before, installations the light of text on glass #f2f1ed (`--text`), other works magenta like the paintings, told from them by the diamond. The accent #8fa8ff is left out, since it is the visitor's dot. The key is drawn from the same table: `index.html` holds four empty marks and their words, `minimap.js` fills each mark with the outline, colour and edge it draws on the map. It stands in two rows of two (Paintings, Videos / Installations, Other works) under the map, right-aligned with it, 12 px in `--hud-muted` on the glass, hidden from screen readers as before. Contrast, worked out for the map's glass rgba(11,12,14,0.85) over pure white (worst) and over black (marks need 3:1): magenta 4.31 and 6.50, cyan 8.97 and 13.53, light 11.61 and 17.50; against their own dark edge 6.27, 13.05, 16.89 (over white). The key's words 7.1:1 at worst, unchanged. New strings: `ui.hud.mapKey.paintings` "Paintings" / "Festmények", `ui.hud.mapKey.installations` "Installations" / "Installációk"; changed: `ui.hud.mapKey.other` HU "A többi mű" to "Egyéb művek" (her wording; listed in `docs/accessibility/hu-confirm.md` all the same). `tests/a11y/hud.mjs`: A4.1 now reads two rows, four words and four marks of four shapes with their edge; A4.2 compares each of the key's marks with the group `minimap.js` draws (shape, colour, edge, read from the page); new A4.6 checks every work's group against its type, and new A4.7 every mark's contrast; A4.3 and A4.5 read two rows. Open for the reconstructor: whether the prints belong under "Other works" or "Paintings", and whether Other works should have a colour of its own rather than the paintings' magenta. Decided by: reconstructor (the four groups and their words); assistant (the shapes, colours and layout). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- The accessible version's links to a work go straight to the work (17 November 2026, the reconstructor's correction 3 from her localhost check). Reproduced on a build of `fbf10e4`: "Open No. …, in the digital exhibition" on the accessible version (`text/`, `scripts/text-edition.mjs`) is a plain link to `../?lang=…&work=<id>` in the same tab, so it loads the exhibition page again from the start, and `src/main.js` only set the 3D view at the work: the visitor got the title card and had to wait for "Ready", press Enter and answer the sound question, also when she had been inside a moment before (the exhibition's own links to the accessible version are same-tab links too, so the page she was in is gone by then). Now: (1) a link to a work never shows the title card. `index.html` marks the page (`html.direct`) before the first paint when the address has `work=`, `src/style.css` keeps the card hidden then (it comes back if the exhibition cannot start, with its failure line, or if the id is unknown), and `main.js` goes in at once, standing at the work, without waiting for the files: the rooms fill in behind as they load, as they did after the 12 s safety net. (2) The sound question opens over the 3D view at the work, at once, with focus on "Without sound" as before; nothing sounds until the visitor chooses (the Web Audio mix is set up before any click, so the browser holds it, and "With sound" or M starts it, `setSound`). (3) Once the question is answered the work opens in the artwork viewer: a dialog named by the work's title, focus on Close. Esc leaves focus on the 3D view in front of the work, from where Page Down and Page Up go on; the room and "Press Enter to open" are no longer announced on arrival (the viewer says both). The link's own words are "Open No. …", so opening the work is what it promises; standing in front of it with the 3D view focused is the other way the brief allowed, a one-line change in `announceArrival` if the reconstructor prefers it. (4) Coming from inside without sound: the exhibition's links to the accessible version (skip link, title card, footer, folded menu) add `?sound=off` when the visitor is inside with the sound off, the accessible version hands it on to its links to a work and to the other language, and the exhibition then skips the question and opens the viewer at once. With the sound on, the question comes again, since a browser starts sound only after a click or key on the page itself. Nothing is stored; the choice travels in the address only (`ap-lang` stays the only stored key). (5) While the accessible version is open, the exhibition's engine is fetched in the background (`rel=prefetch`, after the page's load, not with the browser's data saver on), so the link starts it from the cache: the build's entry script and style, `main.js`'s chunk and the texture decoder, 5 files, 1.66 MB, about 0.56 MB as sent compressed (gzip -9: 37 + 8 + 256 + 15 + 245 kB). The accessible version itself is 46 to 52 kB (15 to 17 kB compressed), so this is about 37 times its own weight, for every visitor with JavaScript on, whether or not a link to a work is followed. The rooms' own files are not fetched (their weight before "Ready" is not measured yet); with JavaScript off nothing is fetched and the links work as before. Not done: keeping the very page the visitor was in (the accessible version shown inside the exhibition, or a second tab), which would keep her place and sound without a reload, but changes how the accessible version opens; left as an option for the reconstructor. Checked on a build: new `tests/a11y/go-to-work.mjs` (by keyboard only: before entering, the title card's skip link, Tab to the link to no. 6, Enter; inside without sound, Shift+Tab to the skip link, the link to no. 14; inside with sound, the link to no. 3; it reads where focus lands and its role and name from the browser's accessibility tree, the 3D view's distance from the work's viewing point while the question is open, and every frame drawn whether the title card or the question was on screen): run on 18 November on the GPU (after 9f4972c) in its second form, below. `tests/a11y/keyboard.mjs` step 9.1 now expects this (no title card, the question with focus at once, Enter opens no. 11 in the viewer with focus in it), 35 of 35 steps passed on 18 November (with 1.1 to 1.3 for the new tab, below); `tests/a11y/text-edition.mjs` now expects the 5 engine files with JavaScript on and nothing with it off or with the data saver, 29 of 29 passed on 18 November. The NVDA script (`docs/accessibility/screen-reader-test.md`) 2.5, 3.1 and 3.3 to 3.9 now expect the new way. No new strings. Decided by: reconstructor (go straight to the work, the question over it, the background loading); assistant (how it is built: the viewer on arrival, `?sound=off`, the engine only). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- A4, the key's groups, her answers of 18 November 2026: the prints (nos. 11 to 13, `type` print) now count as paintings and are drawn round and magenta with them (`MAP_GROUPS` in `src/ui/minimap.js`: Paintings takes `painting` and `print`). "Other works" keeps its place in the key and its diamond, now in an orange of its own, #ff9f40 (new token `--orange` in `src/style.css`; the canvas and the key take it from `MAP_GROUPS`), so the four groups differ in colour as well as shape: magenta circle, cyan square, light triangle, orange diamond, none in the accent blue of the visitor's dot. Contrast of the orange mark on the map's glass: 6.43:1 over pure white, 9.69:1 over black, 9.35:1 against its own dark edge (marks need 3:1; added to the H1 table). Between the marks' colours themselves the orange is a different hue from the other three and from the accent (luminance ratios 1.1 to 1.8, so it is told by hue and shape, not lightness). At present no work has a type that falls in "Other works": the group is in the key with no mark on the map until one does. `tests/a11y/hud.mjs` A4.6 now expects the prints under Paintings, and A4.7 also checks that the four colours differ and none is the accent. Decided by: reconstructor (the prints, a colour of its own); assistant (the orange). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- The accessible version in a tab of its own, her answer of 18 November 2026 (the second-tab way, in place of the reload of 17 November for a visitor who was already inside). The exhibition's four links to the accessible version (skip link, title card, footer, folded menu) open it in a new tab (`target="ap-text"`, so following one again reuses that tab), and say so: their accessible name ends in "(opens in a new tab)" / "(új lapon nyílik meg)" (a hidden span, `textEdition.newTab`), and a ↗ shows after the words, as on the Instagram link. The exhibition names its own tab "ap-exhibition" and says on a BroadcastChannel ("ap-exhibition") that it is open, with its address, whenever it loads, enters, switches language or is asked; when it closes it says "gone". The accessible version (its inline script, `scripts/text-edition.mjs`) asks on load; if the exhibition answers, its links to a work and "Go to the digital exhibition" are pointed at that tab: `target="ap-exhibition"` and the exhibition's own address with the work in the fragment (`#work=<id>&lang=…`, or `#exhibition`), and their text ends in "(in the tab where it is open)" / "(abban a lapban, ahol már meg van nyitva)" (`textEdition.otherTab`, in a `.tab-hint` span inside the link, so screen readers get it in the name). Following one is a same-document navigation in the exhibition's tab, which the browser brings to the front: no reload, so the place, the sound and its choice stay. `src/main.js` (`fromHash`) then: inside, closes what is open, stops the tour, goes to the work and opens it in the viewer with focus on Close (the sound as the visitor left it, no question); not inside yet (the title card), goes straight in at the work as from a link to a work, with the title card gone at once (no fade, `html.direct`), the sound question over the work and then the viewer; with the question still open, the answer opens that work. The fragment is taken off again at once, so the same link works a second time, and the language of the accessible version is carried (`lang=`). "Go to the digital exhibition" only brings the tab to the front (on the title card, focus on Enter once it is ready). If there is no exhibition tab, or no BroadcastChannel, or JavaScript is off, the links are plain links as before: the exhibition loads in the accessible version's tab at the work (17 November entry), with `?sound=off` handed on. If the exhibition's tab closes, the links point back. Same-origin messages and the address only; nothing is stored (window names are not storage, and `ap-lang` is still the only stored key). The accessibility statement (section "What works", first paragraph, both languages) now says that the accessible version opens in a new tab and where its links to a work go. Kept as they were, by her answers: the work opens in the viewer (10), and the engine is fetched in the background while the accessible version is open (11). One limit: the accessible version can find the exhibition's tab by name only if it was opened from it (the browser's rule for named targets); opened on its own (a bookmark, a typed address) while an exhibition tab exists elsewhere, its link opens a new exhibition tab at the work, also with no title card. Whether a browser brings the named tab to the front is its own behaviour (Chrome and Firefox do for a followed link); headless Chromium counts every tab as in front, so the tests check focus inside the tab, not the switch. New strings: `ui.textEdition.newTab`, `ui.textEdition.otherTab`; changed: `accessibility.sections[2].p[0]` (two sentences added), both languages, listed in `docs/accessibility/hu-confirm.md`. Checked on a build, on the GPU (9f4972c), 18 November: `tests/a11y/go-to-work.mjs`, rewritten, 13 of 13 steps: A, no exhibition tab (the accessible version opened alone fetches the engine, 5 files; the link to no. 6 is a plain link whose name says nothing of a tab; the exhibition loads in that tab, the sound question over the 3D view at no. 6 (0.000 m from its viewing point) with focus on "Without sound", then the viewer named "Artificial Paradise – Singapore" with focus on Close, Esc to the 3D view, and the title card in none of the frames drawn); B, exhibition tab not yet entered (the skip link, named "… (text format) (opens in a new tab)", opens a second tab whose links point at the first; the link "Open No. 6, …, in the digital exhibition (in the tab where it is open)" sends that tab, not reloaded, to the question over no. 6 and then the viewer; no title card once followed); C, entered without sound (no. 14: no reload, no question, the viewer with focus on Close, the sound still off, Esc to the 3D view at the work); D, entered with sound (no. 3: no reload, no question, the sound still on and its audio running; the exhibition tab then closed, and the links are plain links again). One fault found and fixed on the way: a closing exhibition tab still answered a question asked while it closed, so the links stayed pointed at it; it now stops answering at pagehide, and the accessible version asks again whenever it comes to the front. `tests/a11y/keyboard.mjs` 35 of 35 (1.1 now reads the skip link's name with "(opens in a new tab)" and its target; 1.2 the new tab; 1.3 its first link back to the exhibition's tab with no reload); the log is written again on purpose. `tests/a11y/text-edition.mjs` 29 of 29. `tests/a11y/hud.mjs` 82 passed, 3 failed: F2.1 at 375 × 667 and 320 × 640 (the toolbar meets the stick by 2 px), which fail the same way on `fbf10e4` before this work. The key in two rows first made A4.5, D2.1 and F1.2 fail where a window is under 520 px high (it met the zoom readout, the touch hint and the first-use card by 2 to 3 px), so there the rows are set close (13 px lines, 28 px high), on a touch screen the key stands at the left of the map level with its top (as it did under 360 px high only), and the hint in the middle, the room label and the first-use card leave it 205 px; all three then passed. `tests/a11y/axe.mjs` (without --write): 38 states with 0 violations. Decided by: reconstructor (the second tab, messaging without storage, "new tab" said in both languages); assistant (how it is built). Not a reconstruction decision (presentation).

- 6.1, Read aloud: the panels and the artwork viewer have a Read aloud button that reads their text with the browser's own speech synthesis (`speechSynthesis`, `SpeechSynthesisUtterance`), in the page's language (17 November 2026). A panel reads what it shows (`#panel-body`, a closed fold such as the opening speech left out); the viewer reads its text column from the room line to the artist's text: the room and number, the title, the details, the caption and, once filled, the descriptions of 6.4 and the room's audio description, not the buttons, the artist's link or the folded exhibition text. In `src/ui/readAloud.js`: a voice is taken only if it lives on the device (`localService` true), for `en-GB` first and then any English on the English page, for `hu-HU` or any Hungarian on the Hungarian page, so the words are not sent anywhere (Chrome's "Google" voices are online ones and are passed over). With no such voice the button reads "Read aloud: no English voice on this device" / "Felolvasás: ezen az eszközön nincs magyar hang", carries `aria-disabled="true"`, reads nothing in a voice of another language, and a press says why through the live region. Some browsers list their voices a moment after loading: until they do, the button reads "Read aloud", and a press waits up to 1.5 s for the list. The text goes to the voice a paragraph at a time, a paragraph over 280 characters cut between sentences. The button is a real `<button type="button">` with `aria-pressed`; its word changes, "Read aloud" / "Stop reading" ("Felolvasás" / "Felolvasás leállítása"), so the state is not told by colour; pressed, it also looks as on hover. In a panel it comes right after Close (Tab reaches it next), in the viewer right under the title. Enter or Space starts and stops it; focus stays on it. Start, stop and the end are said through the existing live region (`#announcer`, `src/ui/announce.js`, topic `read`): "Reading aloud." / "Reading aloud stopped." / "Reading aloud finished." It stops when the panel or the viewer closes, when another text takes the place of the one read (another panel through a link inside the panel; Previous or Next in the viewer), when another reading starts, when the language is switched, and when the page is left. Where the browser has no speech synthesis there is no button. Nothing is stored (no rate, no voice, no setting): the only key is still `ap-lang`; the privacy text is unchanged, since nothing new is stored or sent. Colours: no new pair. In a panel the button is the blue on paper (8.86:1) like Close, pressed the paper on the blue (8.86:1, the pair the hover uses); in the viewer the light text on the dark viewer, pressed on the hover's white 0.1 tint. Added to the H1 table below: viewer button text #f2f1ed on the viewer's dark ground rgba(8, 8, 10, 0.9): 14.28 over white, 17.79 over black; on the pressed tint: 10.52 and 14.40 (needed 4.5, passes); paper #fbfbf9 on the blue #072fcc: 8.86 (passes). The accessibility statement's "What works" has one more paragraph, in both languages, saying what the button does, that it uses a voice on the device and that nothing is sent or stored; its date is unchanged. New strings: `ui.readAloud.start` "Read aloud" / "Felolvasás"; `stop` "Stop reading" / "Felolvasás leállítása"; `noVoice` "Read aloud: no {language} voice on this device" / "Felolvasás: ezen az eszközön nincs {language} hang"; `noVoiceSaid` "Reading aloud is not possible: this device has no {language} voice." / "A felolvasás nem lehetséges: ezen az eszközön nincs {language} hang."; `started` "Reading aloud." / "A felolvasás elindult."; `stopped` "Reading aloud stopped." / "A felolvasás leállt."; `done` "Reading aloud finished." / "A felolvasás véget ért."; `lang.en` "English" / "angol", `lang.hu` "Hungarian" / "magyar"; and the statement's paragraph. Checked on a build, with the GPU (tests at 9f4972c), the new `tests/a11y/read-aloud.mjs`, with speechSynthesis stood in for by an init script (headless Chromium has no voices): RA1 to RA13 pass (the button, next after Close; Enter starts it, Space stops it, aria-pressed and the label follow, focus stays, the live region says start, stop and the end; it stops on Esc, on another panel through a link, on Next in the viewer, on the language switch; the Hungarian voice on the device is taken, not the online one; with only an online Hungarian voice the button says so and nothing is spoken; no button without speech synthesis; only `ap-lang` stored). `smoke.mjs` 2 of 2, `keyboard.mjs` 35 of 35, `focus-mouse.mjs` 59 of 59 (the extra Tab stop after Close broke none of their steps, so they were not changed), `axe.mjs` 38 states with no violations, `text-edition.mjs` 28 of 28. A first smoke run under load (software renderer, before the GPU change) read Room 3 where Room 4 was expected after 3.5 s; rerun alone, it passed. Not tried with a real voice, a screen reader, Firefox or Safari, or on a phone. Decided by: reconstructor (the brief: what it reads, the button, when it stops, nothing stored or sent); assistant (only voices on the device, the "no voice" wording, the place of the button, reading a paragraph at a time). Not a reconstruction decision (interface).
- 6.4, places for the descriptions to come: empty fields for the texts the reconstructor will send, shown only once filled (17 November 2026). In `src/i18n/en.json` and `hu.json`, `accessDescriptions` (beside the artist's texts in `descriptions`, kept apart so a series id can never meet a field's name): `visual.<work id>` for what each of the 17 works shows; `videoText.<work id>` for each video in words, nos. 3 (`w03-screen`, the video on the small screen), 6, 7, 8, 15, 16 (the projection) and 17; `videoDocText.<work id>` for the video documentation the viewer plays, nos. 3 and 16 (`video.viewer` in `exhibition.json`); `space.<room id>` for the audio description of each room, r1 to r4. Each is an empty string; a text goes between the quotation marks, or a list of strings for several paragraphs, in each language (an empty Hungarian field does not take the English text). The `_readme` inside `accessDescriptions`, a sentence in `exhibition.json`'s `_readme` and a line in `README.md` ("Edit the content") say where each text goes. Where they show once filled: in the artwork viewer, under the caption and the full-video buttons and before the artist's text, "Description" / "Leírás", "Video description" / "A videó leírása" and "Description of the video documentation" / "A videódokumentáció leírása" (h3, `#viewer-describe`), and after the artist's text "Listen to the description of the room" / "A terem leírásának meghallgatása" for the work's room (`#viewer-space`); no. 3's screen, opened from the 3D view, shows its video's text. In the accessible version, in each work's article after the caption, h4 headings (no. 3's screen video under no. 3), and in each room's section after its opening paragraph, an h3 with the room's text. While a field is empty there is nothing: no heading and no box (the two containers in the viewer stay `hidden`); the accessible version is the same as before byte for byte apart from 6.1's paragraph in the statement. Read aloud reads them when filled. Shared in `src/localize.js` (`paragraphs`, `workDescriptions`, `roomDescription`), so the viewer and `scripts/text-edition.mjs` read the fields the same way; `buildTextEdition` takes the sources as an argument for a test. No description was written: all fields are empty. The room's text shows in the viewer with every work of that room; whether it stays there, or goes only to the accessible version or to a panel of its own, is still open. Checked on a build, DS1 to DS6 in `tests/a11y/read-aloud.mjs` pass: every field empty, nothing shows in the viewer for any of the 18 entries and none of the headings is in the accessible version; filled with test words in the page's memory (never in the data files), the viewer shows each under its heading (the room's text in Room 1 only, the video's at no. 6, the documentation's at no. 16), Read aloud reads them in that order, the Hungarian page shows its own fields only; the accessible version built in the test from the data with test words puts each under its heading in its work or room (no. 3's screen video under no. 3, a list as paragraphs). Screenshots of where the room's text shows now were taken for the reconstructor (Room 1 and Room 3, 1366 x 768 and 375 x 667, EN and HU, test text in memory only; in the scratchpad, not committed). Headings changed on 18 November 2026 at the reconstructor's word: the room's text is headed "Listen to the description of the room" / "A terem leírásának meghallgatása" (her Hungarian; the English follows it). She keeps the room's text under each work in the viewer for now (open question), and the descriptions of the video documentation in the accessible version. Decided by: reconstructor (the brief: the places, empty, shown only once filled); assistant (the key names, the headings' words and where they stand). Not a reconstruction decision (interface and text places).

## How to continue
`npm install && npm run dev` → http://localhost:5173. Floor plan + walls: `src/world/layout.js`. Texts (EN/HU): `src/i18n/*.json`. Works data and hang: `src/data/exhibition.json`. Furniture: `src/world/props.js`. Installations: `src/world/paradiseFactory.js`, `src/world/letItFlow.js`.
Regenerate images: `npm run images` (needs `reproductions/`, the room folders from Drive, and for the screens and in-situ views the original photos `DSC_*.JPG`, `_01_panorama.jpg` and `room_1/` from the private folder `artificial-paradise-photos`, copied into the repo root, where they are git-ignored; they were removed from the repository and its history). Regenerate wall textures: `npm run textures -- <folder of the Drive brick PNGs>` (also rebuilds the KTX2). Paradise Factory plant: `npm run plant -- <the .glb from Drive>`. Video loops: `scripts/make_videos.sh <folder of Drive videos>` (ffmpeg). River: `python3 scripts/make_river.py "PLANE javított.png" public/tex src/data/river.json` (NumPy + SciPy + Pillow). Real sizes from the artist: Let it Flow 4 × 7 m (the floor video's frame is 7 m long and spans the 4 m room; the plastic, registered to the frame, is about 4.7 × 1.6 m) and Paradise Factory 2.5 × 2.5 m (window wall to the far LED bar, channel's north end to the screen; `sx` in `paradiseFactory.js`). After changing any texture in `public/tex`: `node scripts/make_ktx2.mjs` writes the `.ktx2` versions the site loads (the `.webp` stay as fallback) and copies the Basis transcoder to `public/basis`.
Surface variation (anti-tiling, damp, pipe dirt, whitewash patches): `src/world/surface.js`; pipes register their dirt with `dirtAlong()` in `props.js`.
References: after changing `references/*.csv`, `node scripts/make_references.mjs` writes `src/data/references.json` (it checks every anchor against the texts in `src/i18n` and lists what it found; the network's layout needs the devDependency `d3-force`). The Concept page and its checks: `src/ui/concept.js`, `node tests/a11y/concept.mjs`.
Text edition: `scripts/text-edition.mjs` writes `text/` and `text/hu/` on every build (and serves them in `npm run dev`); where a work is, in words: `src/world/route.js`. Accessibility checks: `npm run build && npm run preview`, then `npm run check:a11y` in a second terminal (Playwright needs its browser once: `npx playwright install chromium`). Hungarian strings new or changed since the last check by a Hungarian reader: `python3 tests/tools/hu-confirm.py --write` (the list is `docs/accessibility/hu-confirm.md`; `--checked` empties it once they are read). Loader background: `node scripts/make_loader_bg.mjs`. Tool versions, at build time and today: `scripts/VERSIONS.md`.
Surface variation (anti-tiling, damp, pipe dirt, whitewash patches): `src/world/surface.js`; pipes register their dirt with `dirtAlong()` in `props.js`; stains seen in the photographs are listed in `PHOTO_STAINS` in `surface.js` (a box on the wall or ceiling each, at most 16).
Text edition: `scripts/text-edition.mjs` writes `text/` and `text/hu/` on every build (and serves them in `npm run dev`); where a work is, in words: `src/world/route.js`. Accessibility checks: `npm run build && npm run preview`, then `npm run check:a11y` in a second terminal (Playwright needs its browser once: `npx playwright install chromium`). Hungarian strings new or changed since the last check by a Hungarian reader: `python3 tests/tools/hu-confirm.py --write` (the list is `docs/accessibility/hu-confirm.md`; `--checked` empties it once they are read). Loader background: `node scripts/make_loader_bg.mjs`.
Paradata in the site: `src/data/paradata.json` is a copy of the tab `paradata_3` of the Google Sheet "paradata" (spreadsheet `1Ie77FU_RlFTL8H4XV998ru3IHVdbq24ffNbZrLGd2xc`), with the time of its last change in `source.lastChanged`; the site never reads the Sheet. To refresh it after the Sheet changes: download the tab as CSV (File > Download > Comma-separated values) or fetch its values with the Sheets API (spreadsheets.values.get, range `paradata_3!A1:Z1000`, saved as JSON), read the Drive file's last change (File > Version history, or the Drive API's modifiedTime), then run `node scripts/paradata-import.mjs <file> --modified <ISO time>`. It rewrites `src/data/paradata.json` and the works' `reconstruction` in `src/data/exhibition.json`, and stops if a title in related_titles names no work or a new row says "all placed works" with no rule for it. Check the diff, then run the build.
